> **Headline of rev 4.** Five blockers, and four of them are the same shape: **rev 3 named a mechanism and did not supply the code that makes it work.** **(1) The denial recorder had no `recordGateDenial()`.** The class ended after `shouldEmit()` while the render callback called a method that did not exist. Rev 4 supplies the body, its six injected dependencies, and the two negatives that make its scope a property. **(2) The single-site container census could not pass against the tree** — rev 3 required `Container::getInstance()`, `app(` and `resolve(` to total exactly one in `bootstrap/app.php` and zero in `app/`, and `bootstrap/app.php` already calls `app()` four times while `app/` carries pre-existing helper calls. It is replaced by an **exact AST assertion** on the one `Container::getInstance()` node plus a **shrink-only ceiling** on `app/`'s legacy sites. **(3) The attribution rule missed the accepted contract's ungated `Gate::authorize()` arm** — the shape of 91 POS routes today — because it keyed on `PermissionDeniedException` or a `can:` in the stack, and a bare `Gate::authorize()` on an `AUTH_ONLY` route has neither. Rev 4 keys on the framework type `AuthorizationException`, which is exact in both directions (a bare `abort(403)` has no `previous`), and recovers the ability from a **`DeniedAbilityTrail`** fed by Laravel's own `GateEvaluated` event. **(4) `Phase 0.5.5` could not compile or run:** its `AuditService::record(eventType:, metadata:)` omits five required parameters. It now calls **`recordTenantWide()`**, which is wave 1's own company-less path. **(5) `ApiTokenRevoked` could not be persisted** — it carried no tenant, while the subscriber's only persistence method requires a non-null company id. The event gains `tenantId` and `actorUserId`, the job carries and re-binds them, and the **handler** goes through `persistTenantWideEvent()`. Plus the majors: the last-admin writer recipe is written in the variables `removeRole` actually has after 0b; EC-16a's queue oracle splits into a real `jobs`-table assertion and a separate `Queue::fake()` arity test, because `assertPushed` and `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database` are mutually exclusive; the membership census ships **one** fixed exemption instead of a conditional map; and the two conditional add-list entries are resolved. Both producers are re-pinned to **rev 6.3**, with the `$permissionWriteLock` reconciliation stated.

*(Rev 3 was the fix round on gate r2 — register `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r2.md`; prompt `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-234-round-2-2026-09-11.md`. Its ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 2 → rev 3** section.)*

> **Headline of rev 3, so a reader does not have to reconstruct it.** Four blockers, and every one is a place where rev 2 corrected the *shape* of a mechanism and left its *behaviour* wrong. **(1) The `principal_kind` CHECK admitted arbitrary values** — rev 2's constraint was `principal_kind = 'human' OR (…the service shape…)`, which a row carrying `principal_kind = 'robot'` and the service shape satisfies. The accepted spec requires `principal_kind IN ('human','service')` as a separate conjunct, with the equivalent SQLite trigger arm, and `PrincipalKindMigrationTest` now attempts an **invalid kind** on both drivers. **(2) Task 2a-8 called a scaffold CLI wave 1 does not ship** — rev 2 used `--module`/`--resource`/`--verb`/`--legacy`; wave 1 **rev 6.3** (`949dfabdf`) declares `permissions:scaffold {module} {resource} {verb} --qualifier= --template=* --sod-group= --dry-run`, positional and with **no `--legacy`**, so the conversion-before-issuance entry condition was unreachable as written. Step 1 now uses the real signature and states the **manifest edit** `pos.override_discount_limit` needs instead of a flag that does not exist. **(3) Lane 2b's numbered commits were not independently buildable** — Task 2b-4 called `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()` (introduced by 2b-6) and routed through `MembershipRevocationService` (2b-5). The lane is **re-ordered** so every `Phase 0.5.n` compiles and is green in isolation: seats before the provisioning service that calls them, revocation before the surface that routes through it, issuance after the controller it routes on. **(4) Denial auditing was attached where the 403 never arrives** — rev 2's `RecordAuthorizationDenial` middleware expected `$next($request)` to return a response an exception renderer had annotated, but Laravel's `prepareException()` converts `AuthorizationException` into `AccessDeniedHttpException` and the **`withExceptions` render callback** is what produces the 403 (`dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:212,270-308`); the middleware never sees it. The recorder now hangs off **that render callback**, recording after the response is built. Plus the majors: the post-lock query oracle stops asserting a sequence that counts `acquire()` once while the controller path takes it twice; `RoleUpdateRefused.role_id` becomes **non-nullable** and the no-admin-role branch gets its own integrity exception; a self effective-permission read goes through `forSubject()` and projects the request token; an **unscoped** token yields `token_scope: null`; `CreateServiceAccountRequest` obtains its actor; `RoleUpdated` and `RoleDeleted` ship with bodies; `MembershipRevocationService` becomes a complete task with its service, job and event bodies; the service-target census stops contradicting itself; and **every task ends with an exact `git add` block**.

> **Headline of rev 2, so a reader does not have to reconstruct it.** Four blockers, all in the machinery rather than in the settled contracts: **`LastAdminFloor` now acquires wave 0b's `PermissionWriteLock` as the first statement of the mutation transaction**, with the lane's `wlota1a:<tenant>` key and its `roles.name` → `roles.id` order, before it takes its own `users`/`model_has_roles` locks — a floor that took a second, different lock order was a deadlock, not a floor; **the `principal_kind` schema moves into 2a, ahead of its callers**, so `refuseServiceTarget()` and the floor's human clause ship as behaviour instead of as `return null;` structural stubs (2b keeps token issuance behind `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest`); **`EffectivePermissionResolver` becomes an interface plus one named implementation with complete bodies** — owner-grant lookup, `permission:` parsing, unknown-ability handling, the `token_id` selector and DTO assembly, so the `owner grants ∩ token scope` claim is a proved property; and **every named failure type and event gains a deliverable** — `LastAdminFloorViolation`, `LastAdminFloorViolationException`, `AdminPermissionFloorViolation` and EC-13's `RoleUpdateRefused` (`role_id`, `reason`, `principal_id`, `token_id`) are in the created-file inventory with bodies. Plus the majors: the **human token TTL is actually implemented** in `AuthController::tokenExpiresAt()`; the `down()` drops **both** SQLite triggers and restores every prior column state in reverse; the writer census and the file inventory are reconciled; denial-source detection gets a concrete mechanism; **2a stops redefining the master plan's one-week soak**; the generic-target census is restricted to the enumerated write methods; and every wave-1 artefact is **re-pinned to the name wave 1 rev 6 actually ships** (`apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json`, a sorted **list** of `<file>:<line>` sites).

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make role management safe to operate — no tenant can lose its last admin, no `roles.manage` holder can empty the admin role, role reads are gated **and shaped**, every role mutation and every write-route denial lands on the audit chain with the acting principal and credential named — and make a service account a first-class principal whose MCP/API token can only ever **narrow**, never widen, its owner's permissions.

**Architecture:** One `users` table with two `principal_kind`s (convention 11 — a second `service_accounts` table would be a duplicate surface). The narrowing is enforced in exactly one place, `User::hasPermissionTo()`, which every permission-based idiom already routes through via Spatie's Gate callback — so 917 call sites inherit it with no edit; the eight **role-name** idioms that do not are converted in 2a and that conversion is a hard entry condition of token issuance in 2b. Status, tenant-claim and membership enforcement live in one middleware attached to the **global `api` group** (attachment, not merely priority), proven by a live-router coverage test with no allow-list. The last-admin rule is one domain service called by every writer, proven by a static census, and it takes its locks in a deterministic order.

**Tech Stack:** Laravel 12 / PHP 8.4 / Sanctum 4 / Spatie laravel-permission 6.25 / Stancl Tenancy 3.10 / PHPUnit 11 / PHPStan L8 / Pint / Redis 7 / React 19 / TanStack Query 5 / Vitest / Playwright.

## Entry condition

**Three things for 2a, and a fourth for 2b that is a test result rather than a merge.**

1. **Wave 1 merged into local `dev`.** 2a consumes `PermissionRegistry`, `PERMISSION_MODULES`, `roles.is_system` / `template_key` / `template_version` / `customised_at`, and wave 1's `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` baseline.
2. **Wave 1 DEPLOYED** — `tenants:migrate-rolling` + `permissions:sync-fleet` green on staging. 2a reads `roles.is_system` as the *only* system-role predicate; on a tenant whose roles were never adopted, every `is_system` is `false` and `SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED` would never fire — a silent regression of today's (bad, but non-empty) name list.
3. **Wave 1's one-week staging soak COMPLETE, with zero unintended diffs.** The master plan's entry condition is *"wave 1 merged and soaked"* and wave 1's own exit is a **one-week** staging soak (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-programme-execution-plan.md` §2, wave-1 exit). **This plan does not redefine it.** Rev 1 substituted *"one deploy cycle"* for the week on the ground that 2a touches no sync path; a phase-zero check may **verify** an accepted programme gate, it may not **weaken** one (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-5**). 2a **and** 2b both wait for the full week, and 2a now also ships the `users` migration (**Q-w2-4**), so the two lanes have the same blast-radius argument in any case.

   **This is not a grep.** The orchestrator confirms it from wave 1's handback (`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w1-<ISO date>.md`) and the staging soak record, and records the soak's start and end dates in this wave's handback.

4. **(2b only) `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` green on `dev`.** Spec §8's *"additional entry condition on 2b, from B-1"*: no scoped token may be issuable until the role-name authorization sites are converted. The test asserts the **backend** half of wave 1's `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` baseline carries **no site** in `CreateDraftCountingRequest.php`, `InventoryCountingController.php` or `DiscountPermissionResolver.php`. `service-accounts.tokens.create` **stays unrouted** until it is green.

   **The baseline is `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json`** — wave 1 rev 6.3's actual path and shape (Task 1.16 Step 7's stage list), a **sorted list of `<file>:<line>` strings relative to `projectRoot`**, generated by `apps/api/tools/generate-permission-baselines.php`. Rev 1 invented `tests/PHPStan/baselines/role-name-authorization-baseline.json` and a **map keyed by file**, and its `assertArrayNotHasKey()` would therefore have passed vacuously against the real artefact (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-7** and citation audit item 4). Task 2a-8 Step 7 ships the corrected test.

Wave 1's own entry condition already requires that lane's five-push staging protocol to be complete, so by construction it has merged; re-prove it rather than assuming.

## Global Constraints

- [ ] **Commit subjects follow `AGENTS.md:16`** — *"Commits must follow `Phase <major.minor.patch>: <imperative summary>`"*. **The predecessor ordinals, read out of the sibling plans rather than assumed:** wave 0a `Phase 0.1.<task>` (`…-wave-0a.md`), wave 0b `Phase 0.2.<task>` (`…-wave-0b.md` rev 5), and **wave 1 `Phase 1.<task>`** — a *two-segment* subject outside the `0.x` series, declared at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:150` (rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`) and used at every one of its commit sites (`Phase 1.1` … `Phase 1.19`, with `Phase 1.4a`–`Phase 1.4i`). **`Phase 0.3.*` is therefore never taken.** Rev 1 asserted wave 1 used `0.3` and the gate found the claim unbacked (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 1**).
      **Lane 2a keeps `Phase 0.4.<task>` and lane 2b `Phase 0.5.<task>`** — free, non-colliding, and in programme order behind 0a and 0b — see **Q-w2-1**. Waves 3 and 4 keep `0.6` and `0.7` on the same basis. Orchestrator documents (this plan, gate registers, handbacks) keep the house `docs(...)` / `review(...)` convention; `AGENTS.md`'s rule governs the code history.
- [ ] **Both trailers on every commit**, as the last two lines, in this order:
      ```
      Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
      Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Bp1WuD39YqaMWiHR8vPDNN
      ```
      Where a task says *"commit (`<subject>`, both trailers)"* it means: that subject line, a body explaining **why** (not what — the diff says what), then exactly those two lines. The shorthand is not a licence to omit them.
- [ ] **Path-scoped commits, with an EXACT add list per commit.** `git add <explicit paths>`, never `git add -A`, never `git add .`, and never a glob or an ellipsis. **Every task below ends with a `git add` block naming each file it stages**, and the task's commit is exactly those paths — that is what makes the commit an independently revertable unit rather than an intention (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 3**). A task that ships more than one commit states one add block per commit, in order, with its own subject. **If a file is not in the add list, it is not in the commit**: finish it in its own task rather than widening the list.
- [ ] **Constructor injection only** (`private readonly`), never the `app()` helper — CLAUDE.md rule 13. This wave introduces five new collaborators (`LastAdminFloor`, `EffectivePermissionResolver`, `MembershipRevocationService`, `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`, `AuthorizationDenialRecorder`) and **every** one is constructor-injected into the controllers and middleware that use it. A middleware resolved out of the container gets its dependencies through its own constructor; Laravel resolves it.
- [ ] **Strict types** in every new PHP file (`declare(strict_types=1);`), no `mixed`, no `any` in TypeScript. **Enums** for every status/type value — `PrincipalKind`, `TokenScopeFailure`, `DenialCause`; no magic strings.
- [ ] **EVERY role-grant, role-assignment and membership writer this wave adds or edits takes wave 0b's shared lock FIRST.** One transaction; `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` as its **first statement**, on the key `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` (`PermissionWriteLock::key()`, adopted verbatim from `lane/w-lot-a-1a`); then `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` over the affected `roles` rows ordered **`roles.name` ASC, then `roles.id` ASC**; then any writer-specific locks; then a re-read of the decision inputs; then the write (`…-wave-0b.md` rev 5, Task 15's five obligations). **A second advisory namespace is a second serialization domain — i.e. no serialization at all — and a second row order over `roles` is a deadlock rather than a race** (`…-wave-0b.md` rev 5, Global Constraints; `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-spec-codex-gate-r9.md:197`). This wave introduces three such writers — `LastAdminFloor` (Task 2a-2), `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` and the two dedicated `/service-accounts/{id}/roles` actions (Task 2b-4), and `MembershipRevocationService` (Task 2b-5) — and **each is added to `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` in the commit that introduces it**. Rev 1 made the floor the first statement of the transaction *instead of* the lock and locked `users` without `model_has_roles`; that is the shape of the defect the lock exists to prevent (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-1** and cross-plan **BLOCKER 1**).
      > **`pg_advisory_xact_lock` is re-entrant within one transaction**, so a writer that already holds the lane key (`RoleController::store/update/destroy/assignRole/removeRole`, all of which wave 0b already made `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`) may call the floor without a second acquisition changing anything: the second take is free and the lock is released once, at commit. That is why the floor can state *"acquire first"* unconditionally rather than branching on whether its caller already did — a branch that would have to be right at nine call sites.
      > **It is free, but it is NOT invisible, and the query oracle must say which path it is measuring** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 1**). `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` **always issues** `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on PG — wave 0b's body has no held-already branch (`…-wave-0b.md` rev 6, `PermissionWriteLock::acquire`) — so the controller path's statement sequence is `advisory, advisory, roles, users, model_has_roles`, with **two** advisory statements, while the floor driven directly is `advisory, roles, users, model_has_roles`. Rev 2 asserted the second sequence over the first path, which is a **false oracle**: it would have been red on correct code. Task 2a-2 Step 1 therefore ships **two** cases, one per path, each asserting its own true sequence.
- [ ] **The narrowing is enforced in ONE place.** `User::hasPermissionTo()` and `User::getAllPermissions()`. No task in this wave adds a `tokenCan()` check to a controller, a policy or a FormRequest. If a reviewer finds one, it is a defect, not defence in depth: 917 call sites cannot be kept in step by hand.
- [ ] **`EnforceTokenScope` does not authorize.** `can:` still does. The middleware performs exactly the four fail-closed checks of §4.1.4 and nothing else, after a strict subject-type no-op.
- [ ] **NO BARE PERMISSION-KEY LITERAL IN A PRODUCTION FILE — every new authorization site spells its key as the generated enum case's `->value`** (plan gate r5, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 1**). Wave 1 rev 6.3 ships `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral`, whose `PERMISSION_METHODS` list is `['can', 'cant', 'hasPermissionTo', 'hasAnyPermission', 'hasAllPermissions']` and which also reads `->middleware()` arguments carrying the prefixes `can:` and `require.any.permission:` (`949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:9000-9010,9226-9243`). Its baseline is **shrink-only and site-exact** — `<relative path>:<line>:<key>` — so it absorbs only the sites that existed when wave 1 generated it and emits on **every new one** (`…-wave-1.md:9024-9036,9141-9161`). Its exemptions are **paths**, not filenames: the two generated-artefact regexes, `app/Shared/Domain/Authorization/*`, the seeder shim, the three permission commands, and `tests/` (`…-wave-1.md:8984-8986`). A controller, FormRequest, service, middleware or `routes.php` is **not** exempt, so rev 4's and rev 5's bare literals would have made several of this wave's own commits red under the per-commit whole-`app/` PHPStan run this plan already requires. **Two forms, and only these two:**
      | Site shape | The form this plan uses |
      |---|---|
      | a method call — `can()`, `cant()`, `hasPermissionTo()`, `hasAnyPermission()`, `hasAllPermissions()`, `Gate::authorize/allows/denies()` | `can(IdentityPermission::RolesView->value)` |
      | a route middleware string in `routes.php` | `'can:'.IdentityPermission::RolesView->value` — a **`Concat`**, never the joined literal |
      **Both are invisible to the rule by construction, and that is a documented property rather than a hope:** `flatten()` returns a key only for a plain `PhpParser\Node\Scalar\String_` (or a `String_` element of a one-level array literal), and its docblock states *"Anything that is not a plain String_ — an enum ->value, a concat, a variable — is NOT a literal and is deliberately invisible to this rule"* (`…-wave-1.md:9203-9243`). Wave 3 states the same route form independently (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-3.md:904`), so the two consumer waves ship one idiom. **Runtime behaviour is unchanged**: the concatenated value is the same string Laravel's `Authorize` middleware parses, and `AuthorizationDenialRecorder::abilityFromRouteStack()`'s `substr($m, 4)` reads the **resolved** middleware string, not the source expression.
      **Case names are PascalCase of the WHOLE key**, splitting on `.`, `-` and `_` — the rule wave 1 adopted **in place of** a module-prefix-stripped form, *"collision-free by construction"* (`…-wave-1.md:3589`). So `inventory.countings.override_ownership` is `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership`, **not** `::CountingsOverrideOwnership`, and `pos.override_discount_limit` is `POSPermission::PosOverrideDiscountLimit`, **not** `::OverrideDiscountLimit`. Rev 5 carried both stripped spellings and they are corrected throughout this revision. *(Rev 6.1: the POS key was `pos.discount_unlimited` with case `::PosDiscountUnlimited` until Q-w2-7 was ruled; the naming rule that produces the case is unchanged — it is applied to the re-spelled key.)* **The POS enum's class name is `POSPermission` in `app/Modules/POS/Domain/Enums/POSPermission.php`, and its manifest is `POSPermissionManifest`** — wave 1 names the artefact after the module directory `POS`, not after a PascalCased `Pos` (`…-wave-1.md:2929,2942,3636,4446,4459`); rev 5 wrote `PosPermission`/`PosPermissionManifest` in six places, which would have staged files wave 1 never created.
      **Every key this wave checks, with its enum case and where the case comes from:**
      | Key | Enum case | Where the case exists |
      |---|---|---|
      | `roles.view` | `IdentityPermission::RolesView` | wave 1 Task 1.5, `IdentityPermission` — 8 cases (`…-wave-1.md:4599`) |
      | `users.manage_location_access` | `IdentityPermission::UsersManageLocationAccess` | wave 1 Task 1.5 (`…-wave-1.md:4603`) |
      | `inventory.countings.create` | `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsCreate` | **expected already present** — Phase 0's registry probe decides; if ABSENT, Task 2a-8 Step 1 scaffolds it, and `permissions:scaffold` writes the enum case as one of its **five** artefacts (`…-wave-1.md:8521`) |
      | `inventory.countings.override_ownership` | `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership` | **created by Task 2a-8 Step 1's `permissions:scaffold Inventory inventory.countings override --qualifier=ownership` run** — artefact 2 of five. **The resource argument is the NESTED `inventory.countings`** and the derived action joins its qualifier with an **underscore**: `make()` is `key: $resource.'.'.$verb->value.'_'.$qualifier` with no module prefix (`…-wave-1.md:645-648`). Spec §4.1.3a (b) spells the key with a hyphen, which `make()` cannot produce; **the enum case name is identical under either spelling**, because the transform splits on `.`, `-` **and** `_` |
      | `pos.override_discount_limit` | `POSPermission::PosOverrideDiscountLimit` | **created by Task 2a-8 Step 1's `permissions:scaffold POS pos override --qualifier=discount_limit` run** — artefact 2 of five. **Q-w2-7 is RULED (orchestrator, 2026-09-11): alternative (B).** The key `pos.discount_unlimited` rev 5 carried reached neither constructor — `legacy()` is closed to a key absent from the shrink-only baseline and `make()` has no `discount` verb — so it is re-spelled resource `pos`, verb **`Override`** (a declared `PermissionVerb` case, `…-wave-1.md:487`), qualifier `discount_limit`. It now **derives**, so it is scaffolded like every other key and there is **no hand edit and no `legacy()` entry**: the generator writes the manifest entry, the enum case and the three locale stubs, and wave 1's `PermissionEnumManifestParityTest` set-equality assertion (`…-wave-1.md:3591`) is satisfied by construction |
      | `service-accounts.create` / `.view` / `.update` / `.delete` and `service-accounts.tokens.create` / `.delete` | `IdentityPermission::ServiceAccountsCreate` / `ServiceAccountsView` / `ServiceAccountsUpdate` / `ServiceAccountsDelete` / `ServiceAccountsTokensCreate` / `ServiceAccountsTokensDelete` | **created by Task 2b-4 Step 0's `permissions:scaffold Identity <resource> <verb>` runs** — each writes the manifest entry **and** the enum case. **All six derive**: the four CRUD keys on resource `service-accounts`, and the two token keys on the **nested** resource `service-accounts.tokens` with the declared verbs `create`/`delete`. **Q-w2-7's ruling (2026-09-11) re-spelled the last two** from `.issue-token` / `.revoke-token`, which reached neither constructor — `PermissionVerb` declares no `issue` and no `revoke`, and `make()` joins a qualifier with an underscore. No key in this wave now STOPs on derivation |
      **The one thing this constraint does NOT license:** editing `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral`, widening its baseline, or adding a path to its allow-list. Wave 1 is final at rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`; the correction is entirely on this plan's side.
- [ ] **Every generic user-targeting write refuses a service target.** `UserController` ×5 and `RoleController` ×2, with 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES` — except `setPosPin`, whose more specific `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN` **wins and is not replaced**.
- [ ] **The `principal_kind` check precedes every `Hash::check`.** On login (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:258`) and on the POS PIN path (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/PosAuthController.php:90`). With `password` nullable, an inverted order is a TypeError before any kind check runs.
- [ ] **Attribution is added ONCE, into the `$metadata` argument.** `AuditService::record()` merges `principal_id`/`token_id` into `$metadata` immediately before constructing the `AuditEvent`, **never** into `$attributes` — that array is Eloquent mass assignment filtered by `$fillable`, which lists neither name, so an `attributes:` write lands nowhere at all, silently (spec §4.6.5, rev-3 M-4).
- [ ] **Denial dedup's read-and-clear is ONE atomic Redis operation** — Lua `GET`+`DEL` (preferred), `MULTI`/`EXEC`, or `GETDEL` where the deployed server allows it. Two round trips lose increments under exactly the storm the rule exists to describe.
- [ ] **Events are immutable forever** (CLAUDE.md rule 8). `identity.role.created` / `.updated` / `.deleted` and `authz.denied` are frozen on merge; a later change is `RoleUpdatedV2` / `AuthorizationDeniedV2`, never an edit. Each class is registered in `DomainEventSubscriber` **in the commit that introduces it** — an unregistered event writes no audit row, silently.
- [ ] **No `origin` push.** Merge into **local** `dev`; the owner promotes. `dev` is fast-forward-only and never force-pushed (CLAUDE.md rule 21).
- [ ] **Worktrees:** `lane/rbac-w2a` then `lane/rbac-w2b`, both created from the shared checkout, never edited in the shared `dev` worktree:
      ```
      git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w2a -b lane/rbac-w2a dev
      # …after 2a merges into local dev…
      git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w2b -b lane/rbac-w2b dev
      ```
      > **Two lanes, not the one the dispatch brief named.** The brief says `lane/rbac-w2`. Spec §8 and the master plan both split wave 2 into **2a** and **2b** with **different entry conditions** — 2b may not open until 2a has merged *and* `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` is green — and a single branch cannot express "2a merged first". **This plan implements `lane/rbac-w2a` + `lane/rbac-w2b`.** The bounded alternative is one `lane/rbac-w2` running every task in order with a single merge; it costs the ability to merge 2a while 2b is still in fix rounds, and it makes 2b's entry condition unfalsifiable (the test would be green on the same branch that is about to break it). Recorded rather than decided silently.
- [ ] **PHPUnit by path only.** Never the full suite locally (memory rule: it crashes the laptop).
- [ ] **PG lane env — both variables, always, never the shared default, never two PG legs in parallel:**
      ```
      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 \
      DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w2a DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w2a_central \
      ./vendor/bin/phpunit <path>
      ```
      Lane 2b uses `autoerp_test_w2b` / `autoerp_test_w2b_central`.
- [ ] **Redis lane env** for the one Redis integration test (Task 2a-7): `REDIS_HOST=127.0.0.1 REDIS_PORT=6379 REDIS_DB=14 CACHE_STORE=redis`. **`REDIS_DB=14` is this lane's, deliberately not 0** — the test writes and deletes keys and must never touch a running app's store.
- [ ] **Empirical evidence, not compilation** (owner rule, 2026-08-31). Every browser probe registers `page.on('response')` 5xx capture **and** console-error capture and asserts **zero** of each; toasts are asserted by text, not by absence of error. "Tests pass" is not the evidence for a wave that changes who can do what.
- [ ] **Convention 09 (second-of-everything)** is satisfied by the three named tests in the "Convention-09 obligations" section below, not by a waiver. Roles/permissions/users are already excluded from `TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest` with stated reasons; this wave **confirms the ratchet stays green rather than editing it**.
- [ ] **Convention 11 (one surface per concept).** A service account has exactly one write surface, `/service-accounts/*`. Any second one found in review is a defect.

## File Structure

### Lane 2a — created

| Path | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Enums/PrincipalKind.php` | `Human` \| `Service`, with `canLogIn()`, `canHoldPosPin()`, `canBeImpersonated()`. **Moved from 2b by Q-w2-4** so its callers ship with behaviour. |
| `apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_09_20_100000_add_principal_kind_to_users.php` | §5.1 — three columns, **two** named CHECKs on PG (`users_principal_kind_check` closing the domain to `('human','service')`, `users_service_principal_check` on the shape) / the equivalent **two-clause trigger pair** on SQLite, `password` nullable; the **five-step** `down()`. **Moved from 2b by Q-w2-4**; the domain check and its invalid-kind test cases are rev 3 (plan gate r2, **B1**). |
| `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PrincipalKindMigrationTest.php` | **Seven cases on both drivers**: an **invalid `principal_kind`** on insert (service shape and human shape) and on update, the four forbidden service states, a valid service row, the no-backfill read-back, and the `down()`'s trigger removal. **Moved from 2b**; the three invalid-kind cases are rev 3 (plan gate r2, **B1**). |
| `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/UserFillableContractTest.php` | `$fillable` carries the three new names, so a `User::create()` cannot silently drop them. **Moved from 2b.** |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LastAdminFloor.php` | The **sole** implementation of floor F-2. `assertSurvives*` methods; **`PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` first**, then `roles` `FOR UPDATE` by `name`→`id`, then `users` by `id` ASC, then `model_has_roles`. |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LastAdminFloorViolation.php` | The `final readonly` factory — **`make(string $code, int $roleId): LastAdminFloorViolationException`** — that gives all nine call sites one 422 body shape. **`$roleId` is non-nullable**, because `RoleUpdateRefused.role_id` is (rev 3's M2); the supplied implementation in Task 2a-2 has this signature and rev 3's inventory row still printed the one-argument form (gate r3, Wave 2 **MINOR 1**). |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LastAdminFloorViolationException.php` | The thrown type. `extends \RuntimeException`, carries `code` and the operator message; rendered by the existing handler. |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminTemplateRoleMissingException.php` | **New in rev 3.** The one floor path with no role to name: a tenant carrying no `template_key = 'admin'` row. Rendered 409 `ADMIN_TEMPLATE_ROLE_MISSING`; **refuses the mutation** and names the provisioning defect rather than emitting a `RoleUpdateRefused` with `role_id = null` (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 2**). |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloor.php` | Floor F-1: the `admin` role's permission set is not operator-editable. |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloorViolation.php` | F-1's thrown type. `extends \RuntimeException`, code `ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR`. |
| `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/RoleUpdateRefused.php` | **EC-13's required event** — `identity.role.update_refused`, carrying **`int $roleId` (NOT nullable)**, `reason`, `principal_id`, `token_id`. Emitted by **both** floors when a role mutation is refused; **not** an `authz.denied`, because the caller *is* authorized to reach the endpoint. Rev 3 makes `roleId` non-nullable and removes the `= null` default from the violation factory, so no call site can emit a refusal the audit chain cannot attribute (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 2**). |

## Decisions and open questions

Seven. Two are the spec's own **OQ-1** and **OQ-2**, both of which this wave **implements** and either of which the owner may still overrule; five are gaps this plan had to resolve, each with the default implemented and the bounded alternative.

### OQ-1 — do service accounts count toward the last-admin floor? **Implemented: NO.**

Spec §9.2's applied default, hard-coded here in three places: `LastAdminFloor`'s definition of *A* filters `principal_kind = human`; `LastAdminFloorTest::a_service_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor` (EC-4); and the glossary row Task 2a-1 does **not** touch (wave 3 owns the glossary sweep). **If the owner rules YES**, the bounded edit is exactly three: drop the `principal_kind = human` clause from *A*, delete EC-4's test method, re-word the Service-account glossary row. Nothing else moves.

**ASK BEFORE 2a MERGES, not before 2b** (plan gate r2, **minor 3**). Rev 1 put the question before 2b because the column was 2b's; **Q-w2-4 moved the column into 2a**, so 2a is now the lane that ships the floor, the `principal_kind = human` clause *and* EC-4's test. A YES ruling arriving after 2a merges is a revert of a merged commit rather than an edit to an unmerged one. The three-change edit is the same either way; only the cost of asking late changed.

### OQ-2 — default TTL for a service-account token? **Implemented: service 365 d, human 90 d, POS 1 y unchanged.**

Spec §9.2's recommended default. Three facts make this a change to an existing policy, not a greenfield choice: `dev:apps/api/config/sanctum.php:43-53` sets a **global 30-day** expiration; `dev:apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:201-219` makes a per-row `expires_at` **override** it; and POS terminal tokens already carry an explicit one-year `expires_at` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:146-176,294-310`).

**All three are expressed as explicit per-row `expires_at` values and the global stays at 43 200 minutes**, so no existing token's lifetime changes retroactively and the override semantics already in `AppServiceProvider` keep working. **Human back-office tokens LENGTHEN, 30 → 90 days** — that is the half of this that is a real decision, and it is called out in the handback rather than buried in a constant. **If the owner rules differently**, the bounded edit is `TokenTtl`'s three constants plus `ServiceAccountTokenIssuanceTest`'s three assertions; the mechanism is unaffected. **There is no unlimited option on any of the three** — `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest` rejects a null/absent `expires_at` by supplying the default, and rejects a value beyond the ceiling.

### Q-w2-1 — the `Phase` ordinal is the LANE's, not the wave number

`AGENTS.md:16` requires `Phase <major.minor.patch>: <imperative summary>`. Wave 0a took `Phase 0.1.<task>` and wave 0b `Phase 0.2.<task>` — i.e. the middle number is the **programme lane ordinal**, not the wave name (there is no `Phase 0.0.x`). The dispatch brief for this plan says `Phase 0.<wave>.<task>`, which read literally would give wave 2 `Phase 0.2.<task>` — **a collision with wave 0b's history**.

**Rev 2 corrects the predecessor claim.** Wave 1 does **not** use `Phase 0.3.<task>`: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:150` (rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`) declares *"Commit subjects are `Phase 1.<task>: <imperative summary>`"* and every one of its commit sites uses it (`Phase 1.1` … `Phase 1.19`, with the `Phase 1.4a`–`1.4i` manifest series). Rev 1 of this plan asserted `0.3` without reading the sibling plan, which the gate flagged as a downstream re-pin finding (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 1**).

**The consequence is that the ordinal this plan already implements is still correct, and now for a stated reason**: `0.1` and `0.2` are taken, `0.3` is **free and unused**, and **lane 2a = `0.4`**, **lane 2b = `0.5`**, wave 3 = `0.6`, wave 4 = `0.7` collide with nothing. The gap at `0.3` is deliberate and is recorded in the handback so a later reader does not look for a missing lane.

**Phase 0 confirms it mechanically** before the first commit is written, over **both** shapes:
```
git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp log dev --oneline --grep='^Phase [0-9]' -60 \
  | sed -E 's/^[0-9a-f]+ (Phase [0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?)\..*/\1/' | sort -u
```
**Expected:** `Phase 0.1`, `Phase 0.2`, `Phase 1`. If a predecessor took something else, take the next two free ordinals **and say so in the handback**, so waves 3 and 4 can re-anchor. The bounded alternative — one ordinal per *wave*, with `2a`/`2b` as the patch prefix (`Phase 0.2a.1`) — is rejected because `AGENTS.md`'s pattern is three integers and a non-numeric segment would break any tooling that parses it.

### Q-w2-2 — who ships the RolesPage grouping, banner and *Re-apply* button

Spec §8's wave-2a row and the master plan both list *"`RolesPage` grouped by registry module with the template-version banner and Re-apply template"*. But **wave 1's own verification checklist already asks for that UI probe** (`UI probe: Roles grouped by registry module, translated labels, template-version banner, no-op re-apply on a pristine role`), while **no wave-1 task ships a `RolesPage.tsx` edit**: Task 12 ships the `customised_at` writer and the *Re-apply* **action** (a `RoleController` endpoint), Task 17 ships `PERMISSION_MODULES` in the generated map, and wave 1's frontend reviewer prompt says *"Did the wave touch `usePermissions.ts` or `uiAliasPermissions.ts`? It must **not**"* without saying the same of `RolesPage.tsx`.

**This plan implements the FE in 2a** (Task 2a-9), on the ground that wave 1 ships the endpoint and the grouping data and wave 2a ships the screen that consumes them — which is also what makes `is_system` and the grouping one commit rather than two half-screens. **Phase 0 checks the tree**: `git log dev --oneline -3 -- apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx`. **If wave 1 did ship it**, Task 2a-9 reduces to deleting `SYSTEM_ROLES` (`dev:apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx:35`) and reading `is_system` from the payload, and the reviewer is asked explicitly to confirm there is **no duplicate grouping implementation** (convention 11). Either way the screen exists once.

### Q-w2-3 — **CLOSED: "soaked" means the master plan's full one-week soak, for BOTH lanes**

The master plan's 2a entry condition is *"Wave 1 merged and soaked"*; wave 1's own exit is a **one-week** staging soak with zero unintended diffs, which is also the input to OQ-4.

**Rev 1 split it** — soak-started for 2a, soak-complete for 2b — on the ground that 2a touches no sync path. **Rev 2 withdraws the split.** A wave's phase zero may **verify** an accepted programme gate; it may not **redefine** one, and *"has run at least one full deploy cycle"* is a different, weaker condition wearing the same word (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-5**). Two facts make the withdrawal cheap as well as correct: the rev-2 re-split (**Q-w2-4**) moves the fleet-wide `users` migration **into 2a**, so 2a now has exactly the blast radius the split cited as 2b's reason; and the soak's week runs in parallel with wave 1's own handback and gate rounds rather than after them.

**This plan implements: both lanes open only once wave 1's one-week staging soak is COMPLETE with zero unintended diffs.** The orchestrator records the soak's start and end dates in this wave's handback. There is no bounded alternative on offer: shortening an accepted programme gate is an owner decision, not a plan-author one, and no owner question is opened for it because the master plan already answers it.

### Q-w2-4 — **RE-SPLIT: the `principal_kind` SCHEMA lands in 2a, ahead of its callers; 2b keeps token issuance**

The rev-4 and rev-5 spec rulings put `refuseServiceTarget()` on **both** `UserController` (five writes) and `RoleController` (`assignRole`/`removeRole`), and put the floor's human clause on `LastAdminFloor` — but rev 1 of this plan put the `principal_kind` column in 2b, so both shipped in 2a as **structural stubs**: `refuseServiceTarget()` whose body was `return null;` and `applyHumanClause()` whose body was a comment. **The gate is right that those are production-facing placeholders, not implementation-complete commits** (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-2**; CLAUDE.md rule 1). Rev 1's own defence — *"a no-op guard is a complete implementation of 'no principal is a service yet'"* — is withdrawn: it is a complete implementation of a fact the schema does not yet make true, which is the definition of a stub.

**Rev 2 moves the schema, not the callers.** The dependency runs one way — `principal_kind` has no dependency on anything in 2b — so the fix is to land it **first**:

| Deliverable | rev 1 | **rev 2** |
|---|---|---|
| `PrincipalKind` enum, the `users` migration (three columns, **two** named CHECKs / the two-clause SQLite trigger pair, `password` nullable), its **five-step** `down()` (**M1-2**), `$fillable` / `casts()`, the three `User` predicates | 2b (Task 2b-1) | **2a (Task 2a-0, first task in the wave)** |
| `refuseServiceTarget()` **with its real predicate** + `UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest` + `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` | 2a stub / 2b census | **2a, with behaviour** (Task 2a-2a) |
| `LastAdminFloor`'s human clause + EC-4 (`a_service_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor`) | 2a comment / 2b test | **2a, with behaviour** (Task 2a-2) |
| The `principal_kind` writer census, the five notification selectors, the PIN-surface predicates, the seat counters | 2b | **2b, unchanged** — they teach existing writers about a column that already exists |
| `EnforceTokenScope`, the narrowing generalisation, `ServiceAccountController` / provisioning / memberships, **token issuance and revocation**, `/auth/me`, the FE | 2b | **2b, unchanged** |

**Token issuance stays in 2b, behind `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest`** — that entry condition is about the role-name conversion (spec §8's B-1), not about the column, and moving the schema forward does not touch it. `service-accounts.tokens.create` is still unrouted until the test is green.

**Nothing in 2a can create a service row**, because `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` is 2b's and `CreateUserRequest` may not set `principal_kind`. That does **not** make 2a's refusal a stub: the predicate reads a real column, and `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` (2a) inserts a `principal_kind = 'service'` row **directly through the model factory** — which the CHECK permits, since it constrains the row's *shape*, not its provenance — and asserts each of the seven writes returns 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES` with the target's rows **unchanged**. The same arrangement gives EC-4 a real service admin in 2a. `ServiceTargetRefusalIsInertTest` is **deleted from this plan**; there is nothing inert left to assert.

**The cost, stated:** 2a becomes eleven tasks rather than nine and ships the wave's one migration, so 2a — not 2b — is the lane whose rollback is the destructive **five-step** `down()`, and 2a's deploy gains `tenants:migrate-rolling` as a real step rather than a ceremonial one. **The bounded alternative** — move `refuseServiceTarget()`, its call sites and the floor's human clause wholesale into 2b — is the gate's other offered branch and remains available; it costs a second pass over `UserController::{update,destroy,activate,deactivate,resetPassword}` and `RoleController::{assignRole,removeRole}`, which are exactly the methods 2a already rewrites for `LastAdminFloor`, and touching seven methods twice across two lanes is how a call site gets missed. **The orchestrator ruled for moving the schema.**

### Q-w2-5 — `inventory.countings.create` and `pos.override_discount_limit` are declared in 2a, not wave 1

Spec §4.1.3a (b) requires two **new** permission keys for the role-name conversion: `inventory.countings.override_ownership` (enum case `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership`) and `pos.override_discount_limit` (enum case `POSPermission::PosOverrideDiscountLimit`), both `admin`-only by template, both preserving today's behaviour exactly. **The spec spells the second one `pos.discount_unlimited`; Q-w2-7 (RULED 2026-09-11) re-spelled it**, because the spec's spelling reaches neither `make()` nor `legacy()` — the spec line is owed the same edit and is handed back. Wave 1 built the registry but declares neither (they do not exist in the 304 + 19).

**This plan declares the ABSENT keys in 2a through `php artisan permissions:scaffold`** — `inventory.countings.override_ownership` and `pos.override_discount_limit` are new by construction (neither is in wave 1's 327), while **`inventory.countings.create` is expected to already exist**, because it gates the counting-draft route today via `CreateDraftCountingRequest`. **Phase 0's registry probe decides**, and the expected count is therefore *"two new, one verified"* rather than *"all three"* (plan gate r1, **minor m1-2**): Task 2a-8 Step 1 scaffolds **exactly the keys the probe reported ABSENT**, and its verification line reads *"the registry now returns PRESENT for all three"*, which is true on either branch. A third scaffold that turns out to be unnecessary is not harmless — wave 1's `PermissionCatalogueMigrationProgressTest` fails on an exact-partition mismatch the moment a key is declared twice. All three go into their owning module's manifest with `templateDefaults: ['admin']` — never `'admin'` written literally, which wave 1's registry rejects as a build error, so the correct form is an **empty** `templateDefaults` plus the implicit-admin rule; Phase 0 reads `PermissionDefinition::make()`'s signature and the task uses whatever wave 1 actually shipped. **The bounded alternative** — convert the six counting bypasses to `can(InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsUpdate->value)` and reuse an existing key — is rejected: it would **widen** who may override another operator's counting from `admin` to every `manager`, which is a behaviour change smuggled inside a refactor.

**Deploy consequence, stated:** 2a's deploy runs `permissions:sync-fleet`, which creates the new rows and re-runs `admin = activeKeys()`. Until it does, the six converted bypasses deny for everyone including `admin` — so **the migration and the sync are one deploy, and the sync is not optional** (Deploy step 2 below).

### Q-w2-6 — `max_service_accounts` default 5 is a **per-tier** value, and the trial tier gets it too

Spec §4.1.7 says *"a new plan limit `max_service_accounts`, default 5"* and separately that a limit absent from a tier's array *"falls back to whatever default the reader passes, which is how a cap silently becomes zero or unlimited"*.

**This plan writes `5` into every tier's array including `trial()`** (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Billing/Domain/PlanLimits.php:161-173` and its siblings), and `PlanLimitsContractTest` asserts **every** tier array contains the key — so a ninth tier added later fails the build rather than inheriting a silent default. The bounded alternative — a lower cap on `trial` — is a **commercial** decision, not an engineering one; if the owner wants it, it is one integer per tier and no code change.

### Q-w2-7 — **RULED (orchestrator, 2026-09-11): option (B), re-spell. Three keys were undeclarable under wave 1's closed verb enum and shrink-only legacy ceiling; all three are re-spelled and now derive through `make()`**

**Status: RULED. Applied in rev 6.1 throughout this plan (and in the wave-3 plan's two references).** No wave-1 edit, no `PermissionVerb` case added, no `legacy()` entry, no baseline regeneration, no ceiling raise.

| Was (rev 5/6, undeclarable) | Is (ruled) | Decomposition |
|---|---|---|
| `pos.discount_unlimited` | **`pos.override_discount_limit`** | resource `pos`, verb **`Override`**, qualifier `discount_limit` |
| `service-accounts.issue-token` | **`service-accounts.tokens.create`** | nested resource `service-accounts.tokens`, verb **`Create`**, no qualifier |
| `service-accounts.revoke-token` | **`service-accounts.tokens.delete`** | nested resource `service-accounts.tokens`, verb **`Delete`**, no qualifier |

**Verb-existence check, run against the pinned producer before the re-spelling was applied.** `PermissionVerb` is a closed 32-case enum at wave 1 rev 6.3 `949dfabdf` (`…-wave-1.md:451-493`). `case Override = 'override';` is declared at `:487` under *"exceptions and IO"*, `case Create = 'create';` at `:455` and `case Delete = 'delete';` at `:457` under *"read / write lifecycle"*. **All three verbs the ruling uses already exist**, so the ruling adds no case and touches no wave-1 artefact. Neither `issue` nor `revoke` nor `discount` nor `unlimited` is declared — which is what made the old spellings undeclarable.

**Nested resources are already the shipped idiom, not an invention for this ruling.** `RESOURCE_PATTERN` admits kebab-case *"with at most one nested segment"* (`…-wave-1.md:580`, and `make()`'s own throw message), and the catalogue already spells `inventory.transfers.create` and `inventory.adjustments.post` that way (`…-wave-1.md:4098-4106`); `catalog.attributes.*` is the other family. `service-accounts.tokens` is exactly one nested segment on a kebab-case root, so it passes unchanged.

**The reasoning, stated once.** Both constraints the rev-5/rev-6 analysis identified are real and neither is this wave's to relax:

- `PermissionVerb` is **closed**. A verb it does not declare is *"a build error, not a new verb"*, and adding `Issue`/`Revoke`/`Discount` is a **producer** change to wave 1, which is final at rev 6.3 `949dfabdf` and which this plan's Global Constraints forbid editing.
- The legacy escape hatch is **shrink-only and keyed by permission key**. `legacy_actions_are_within_the_shrink_only_ceiling` fails on *"a key absent from the baseline"* **before** it looks at `LEGACY_ACTION_CEILING = 59` (`949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:1032`; `legacy()`'s docblock, `…-wave-1.md:668-672`), so a 60th key is growth whatever the ceiling says. Raising the ceiling — the rejected alternative (A) — would have re-opened a door wave 1 shut deliberately, and would have established that it can be raised again next wave.

Between "change the producer" and "change the spelling of a key nobody holds", the spelling is the smaller and more honest change. **All three keys are brand new** — none exists in wave 1's 327, none is granted to any role today, none appears in a migration or an API contract — so re-spelling before first declaration costs a spec line each and **no migration, no rename map, no deprecation**. `pos.override_discount_limit` additionally lands in a namespace that already has its shape: it is the exact mirror of the catalogue's existing `pos.approve_discount_limit_override` (`…-wave-1.md:4467`) — *approve someone else's override* versus *hold the override yourself*. The two token keys stop pretending a token is an adjective on a service account and say what they are: **create and delete on the `service-accounts.tokens` resource**, which is also why they need no qualifier and so never meet the underscore-join arithmetic that broke `issue --qualifier=token`.

**What the ruling changed in this plan (rev 6.1).** Every occurrence of the three keys — routes and their `can:` middleware (always `'can:'.<Enum>::<Case>->value`, never a joined literal), the `permissions:scaffold` invocations and their derived-key probes, the manifest rows, the enum case names (PascalCase of the WHOLE key, splitting on `.`, `-` and `_` — so `PosOverrideDiscountLimit`, `ServiceAccountsTokensCreate`, `ServiceAccountsTokensDelete`), template defaults (`admin`-only, i.e. no `--template` and no `templateDefaults` entry — unchanged by the ruling), the behaviour test renamed `OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest`, the `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` failure message, the `git add` lists, the deploy and verification checklists, and the 2b entry-condition prose. **Both executable STOPs are removed** — Task 2a-8 Step 1's legacy-growth probe and Task 2b-4 Step 0's derivation STOP — because there is nothing left for them to stop on: no key in this wave reaches `legacy()`, and all three re-spelled keys derive. **Spec §4.1.3a (b) and §4.1.7 carry the old spellings and are owed the same re-spelling**; that edit is outside this plan's file scope and is handed back as a spec-maintenance item.

**`inventory.countings.override_ownership` is unaffected** — it decomposed cleanly through `make()` already, and needed only the derivation correction rev 6 applied.

## Task 2a-1: `roles.is_system` replaces the two string lists

**Files**

- Modify: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php` (`dev:228`, `dev:277`, and the two payload builders at `dev:141-149`, `dev:167-175`)
- Modify: `apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx` (`dev:35`)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/SystemRoleProtectionTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Consumes: `roles.is_system`, `roles.template_key`, `roles.template_version`, `roles.customised_at` (wave 1's migration `2026_09_18_100000_add_template_columns_to_roles.php`).
- Produces: the four template columns on the `GET /roles` and `GET /roles/{id}` payloads, which Task 2a-4 then **shapes by gate** and Task 2a-9 renders.

**Why this is first.** Every later task in 2a reads `is_system` — the floor, the shaping, the events. Landing the column's consumption first means one re-pin of `RoleController`'s line numbers rather than five.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write the test first (red).** `SystemRoleProtectionTest` covers EC-12 in both directions and proves the guard is driven by the **column**, not by a name:
      - a seeded `manager` (adopted by wave 1's sync, `is_system = true`) cannot be renamed → 422 `SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED`, and `roles.name` is asserted **unchanged in the database**;
      - the same role cannot be deleted → 422, and the row is asserted **still present**;
      - a **custom** role literally named `owner` — which today's `$systemRoles = ['super-admin','admin','owner']` protects and which is not a Spatie role at all — **can** be renamed and deleted, because `is_system = false`;
      - a custom role named `admin` cannot exist (wave 1 adopted the real one), so the test instead asserts a custom role named `supervisor` with `is_system = false` is freely mutable;
      - the payload of `GET /roles` carries `is_system`, `template_key`, `template_version` and `customised_at` for a `roles.view` caller.
- [ ] **Step 2 — delete both lists and drive off the column.** In `RoleController`, remove the `$systemRoles` array at `dev:228` (rename guard) and `dev:277` (delete guard); replace each with `if ($role->is_system) { return response()->json([...], 422); }` using the controller's existing error-body shape. **Do not** invent a second helper — the two guards are two lines.
      > **The error code is unchanged from what a client already sees.** Today both paths return 422 with a message naming the role; the code becomes the stable `SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED` string spec §4.6.1 names, and the FE reads the code, not the message.
- [ ] **Step 3 — widen the two payload builders** (`dev:141-149` index, `dev:167-175` show) with the four template columns. **Task 2a-4 shapes them by gate**; this step only makes them available.
- [ ] **Step 4 — FE:** delete `const SYSTEM_ROLES = ['super-admin', 'admin', 'owner']` (`dev:apps/web/src/features/settings/RolesPage.tsx:35`) and every reference to it; the rename/delete affordances read `role.is_system` from the payload. `RolesPage`'s **grouping** and the **banner** are Task 2a-9 — this step is the guard only, so a reviewer sees the two concerns in two diffs.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run:**
      ```

      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w2a DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w2a_central \
