## Task 2a-2: `LastAdminFloor` — one service, every writer, deterministic locks

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LastAdminFloor.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/LastAdminFloorTest.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/LastAdminFloorWriterCensusTest.php`
- Modify: `RoleController.php` (`dev:350-359` `assignRole`, `dev:390-410` `removeRole`), `UserController.php` (`dev:380-389` the `syncRoles` in `update`, `dev:470-484` `destroy`, `dev:509-579` `activate`, `dev:630-642` `deactivate`)

**Interfaces**

- Produces: `LastAdminFloor::assertRoleRemovalSurvives()`, `::assertRoleReplacementSurvives()`, `::assertDeactivationSurvives()`, `::assertDeletionSurvives()`, `::assertMembershipRemovalSurvives()` — the last consumed by **2b**'s `MembershipRevocationService`. Plus `LastAdminFloorViolation` (factory), `LastAdminFloorViolationException` (thrown type) and the `RoleUpdateRefused` emission each refusal makes.
- Consumes: wave 0b's `PermissionWriteLock::acquire(string $tenantId): void` / `::key(string $tenantId): string` (`App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks`); `roles.template_key = 'admin'` (wave 1); and `users.principal_kind` — **which Task 2a-0 has already landed** (Q-w2-4), so the human clause is a real `where()` and `LastAdminFloorTest::a_service_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor` (**EC-4**) ships **here**, not a lane later.

**The definition, verbatim from spec §4.6.3.** Let *A* = principals that are **all** of: `principal_kind = human`, `status = active`, holding the role whose `template_key = 'admin'`, **and holding at least one active `user_company_memberships` row**. Any operation that would make `|A| = 0` is refused with 422. The floor is **per tenant**, not per company (roles are tenant-scoped, D1). The check runs **inside the mutation's transaction**.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `LastAdminFloorTest` first (red).** Nine cases, every one asserting **rows**, never a bare status:
      | Case | EC | Assertion |
      |---|---|---|
      | `removing_the_admin_role_from_the_last_admin_is_refused` | EC-1 | 422 `LAST_ADMIN_ROLE_REMOVAL`; the `model_has_roles` pivot row is **still present** |
      | `deactivating_the_last_admin_is_refused` | EC-2 | 422 `LAST_ADMIN_DEACTIVATION`; `users.status` **unchanged** |
      | `deleting_the_last_admin_is_refused` | EC-3 | 422 `LAST_ADMIN_DELETION`; the row is **not** soft-deleted |
      | `a_membershipless_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor` | EC-4a | two admins, one with no active membership; deactivating the other is refused |
      | `update_with_a_different_role_cannot_empty_the_admin_population` | EC-4b | `PATCH /users/{id}` with `role: 'manager'` on the sole admin → 422; the pivot is unchanged. **This is writer #5, the one rev 1 missed** |
      | `the_floor_is_per_tenant_not_per_company` | conv-09 | **two companies in one tenant**; company B has its own `owner` membership; removing the admin role from the tenant's only admin is still refused |
      | `a_second_active_admin_makes_every_operation_allowed` | — | the allow path for all four writers, so the test proves the floor is passable |
      | `two_concurrent_removals_leave_exactly_one_admin` | EC-19 | **PG only**, two real connections; the loser gets 422; `mark-skips` on SQLite |
      | `an_impersonated_call_hits_the_same_floor` | EC-8/§4.6.3 #8 | an impersonation session reaching `removeRole` is refused identically |
      | `a_service_admin_does_not_satisfy_the_floor` | **EC-4** | a `principal_kind = 'service'` principal holding the admin role and an active membership is **not** in *A*: deactivating the one human admin is refused. **Ships here, in 2a** — Task 2a-0 landed the column (Q-w2-4). The service row is inserted through the factory, which the §5.1 CHECK permits |
      | `the_floor_acquires_the_shared_write_lock_before_its_own` | **EC-20a** | **PG only, and the floor is driven DIRECTLY** — `DB::transaction(fn () => $floor->assertDeactivationSurvives($target, $tenantId))`, with **no controller**, so the transaction contains exactly **one** `acquire()`. A query-log capture then asserts the statement sequence is, in order: `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended($1, 0))` with binding `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId`; the `roles` `FOR UPDATE` ordered `name, id`; the `users` `FOR UPDATE` ordered `id`; the `model_has_roles` `FOR UPDATE`. **This is the test that fails if someone "simplifies" the order** |
      | `a_caller_that_already_holds_the_lock_takes_it_twice_and_is_not_blocked` | — | **PG only, and it is the CONTROLLER path.** `RoleController::removeRole` acquires the lane key as its transaction's first statement and then calls the floor, which acquires it again. The query log therefore contains **two** `pg_advisory_xact_lock` statements with the **same** binding, and the assertion is exactly that: `advisory, advisory, roles, users, model_has_roles`, and the request **completes** rather than self-deadlocking |
      | `the_floor_outside_a_transaction_throws_LogicException` | — | driver-independent, so the contract is meaningful on the fast lane where the lock itself is a no-op |
      | `a_tenant_with_no_admin_template_role_refuses_with_its_own_code` | — | **New in rev 3** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 2**). Delete the tenant's `template_key = 'admin'` row directly, then attempt a deactivation: **409 `ADMIN_TEMPLATE_ROLE_MISSING`**, `users.status` **unchanged**, and **no `RoleUpdateRefused` row** — there is no role to name, and emitting one with `role_id = null` is what EC-13's contract forbids. The message names `permissions:sync --tenant=` |
- [ ] **Step 2 — write the service.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\PrincipalKind;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\UserStatus;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Builder;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role;

/**
 * Floor F-2 — spec 4.6.3. The SOLE implementation; nine writers call it and
 * none re-implements it, because nine copies of a rule is how writer #5
 * (UserController::update's syncRoles) came to be missing for a whole revision.
 *
 * A = principals that are ALL of: principal_kind = human, status = active,
 *     holding the role whose template_key = 'admin', AND holding at least one
 *     ACTIVE user_company_memberships row.
 *
 * LOCK ORDER — inherited from wave 0b, NOT invented here (plan gate r1, B1-1
 * and cross-plan BLOCKER 1). Every assert* method MUST be called inside the
 * mutation's own transaction and takes its locks in exactly this sequence:
 *
 *   1. PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)   -- the SHARED advisory lock,
 *      key 'wlota1a:'.$tenantId, ALWAYS FIRST. pg_advisory_xact_lock is
 *      re-entrant within a transaction, so a caller that already holds it
 *      (RoleController::store/update/destroy/assignRole/removeRole are all
 *      wave 0b LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS) is unaffected: the second take is free and
 *      the lock is released once, at commit. That is why this is unconditional
 *      rather than a branch that would have to be right at nine call sites.
 *   2. roles FOR UPDATE, ordered roles.name ASC then roles.id ASC -- wave 0b's
 *      order, over the admin-template role. A SECOND row order over `roles` is
 *      a deadlock rather than a race: for `manager` vs `general_manager` the
 *      name and id orders are opposite.
 *   3. users FOR UPDATE, ordered users.id ASC -- the floor's own candidate set.
 *   4. model_has_roles FOR UPDATE for those users, ordered (model_id, role_id).
 *      Rev 1 locked (3) and not (4), so a concurrent writer could detach the
 *      surviving admin's pivot between this read and the caller's write.
 *   5. Re-read A from committed state and decide.
 *
 * A floor that takes a different lock, or the same locks in a different order,
 * is not a stricter floor: it is a deadlock and an unserialised read.
 *
 * The `roles` lock at step 2 is SINGLE-ROW (one admin-template row), so wave 0b's
 * every_multi_row_roles_lock_uses_the_lane_row_order case does not demand an
 * order clause of it -- 0b rev 6 lists three multi-row locks and three single-row
 * exemptions, and this writer is new to both lists. The ORDER BY is written
 * anyway: a predicate that returns one row today returns two on a tenant that
 * carries BOTH a NULL-team legacy admin role and a tenant-scoped one, and an
 * unordered multi-row lock is exactly the deadlock the lane's order prevents.
 */
final readonly class LastAdminFloor
{
    public function __construct(
        private PermissionWriteLock $writeLock,
        private LastAdminFloorViolation $violationFactory,
    ) {}

    /** @throws LastAdminFloorViolation */
    public function assertRoleRemovalSurvives(User $target, string $roleName, string $tenantId): void
    {
        if (! $this->isAdminRole($roleName, $tenantId)) {
            return;
        }

        $this->assertPopulationSurvivesWithout($target, $tenantId, 'LAST_ADMIN_ROLE_REMOVAL');
    }

    /**
     * PATCH /users/{id} with a different `role` calls syncRoles(), which is a
     * WHOLESALE replacement: it removes the admin role without ever touching
     * removeRole(). Writer #5.
     *
     * @param  list<string>  $nextRoleNames
     * @throws LastAdminFloorViolation
     */
    public function assertRoleReplacementSurvives(User $target, array $nextRoleNames, string $tenantId): void
    {
        $adminRoleName = $this->adminRoleName($tenantId);

        if ($adminRoleName === null || in_array($adminRoleName, $nextRoleNames, true)) {
            return;   // the target keeps admin; the population cannot shrink
        }

        $this->assertPopulationSurvivesWithout($target, $tenantId, 'LAST_ADMIN_ROLE_REMOVAL');
    }

    /** @throws LastAdminFloorViolation */
    public function assertDeactivationSurvives(User $target, string $tenantId): void
    {
        $this->assertPopulationSurvivesWithout($target, $tenantId, 'LAST_ADMIN_DEACTIVATION');
    }

    /** @throws LastAdminFloorViolation */
    public function assertDeletionSurvives(User $target, string $tenantId): void
    {
        $this->assertPopulationSurvivesWithout($target, $tenantId, 'LAST_ADMIN_DELETION');
    }

    /**
     * Consumed by wave 2b's MembershipRevocationService. Removing a membership
     * only threatens the floor when it removes the principal's LAST active one
     * — the clause that makes A "principals who can actually get somewhere".
     *
     * @throws LastAdminFloorViolation
     */
    public function assertMembershipRemovalSurvives(User $target, string $companyId, string $tenantId): void
    {
        $remaining = DB::table('user_company_memberships')
            ->where('user_id', $target->id)
            ->where('company_id', '!=', $companyId)
            ->where('status', 'active')
            ->count();

        if ($remaining > 0) {
            return;
        }

        $this->assertPopulationSurvivesWithout($target, $tenantId, 'LAST_ADMIN_MEMBERSHIP_REMOVAL');
    }

    /** @throws LastAdminFloorViolationException */
    private function assertPopulationSurvivesWithout(User $target, string $tenantId, string $code): void
    {
        if (DB::transactionLevel() === 0) {
            throw new \LogicException(
                'LastAdminFloor must be called inside the mutation transaction — a floor evaluated '
                .'outside one is read against state the mutation has not yet taken locks on.',
            );
        }

        // STEP 1 — the SHARED lock, first, on wave 0b's key. Re-entrant, so a
        // caller that already holds it pays nothing. NOTE: acquire() always
        // ISSUES pg_advisory_xact_lock; re-entrancy is PostgreSQL's, not a
        // held-already branch here -- which is why the controller path's query
        // log carries TWO advisory statements and the direct path ONE, and why
        // Step 1 ships two oracles rather than one (plan gate r2, MAJOR 1).
        $this->writeLock->acquire($tenantId);

        // STEP 2 — the admin-template role ROW (not just its name), in wave 0b's
        // order. The row is carried, not the name, because every refusal below
        // must name role_id and EC-13's contract has no nullable arm
        // (plan gate r2, MAJOR 2).
        $adminRole = $this->lockAdminRole($tenantId);

        if ($adminRole === null) {
            // No admin-template role on this tenant. This is NOT a last-admin
            // refusal -- there is no role to name, so a RoleUpdateRefused emitted
            // here would carry role_id = null and break EC-13's attribution
            // contract for every reader of the audit chain. It is a PROVISIONING
            // defect: wave 1's sync guarantees the adopted admin-template row
            // exists on every tenant, and its absence means the tenant was never
            // synced. Fail closed with its own type, which still refuses the
            // mutation and names the real problem instead of mislabelling it.
            throw new AdminTemplateRoleMissingException($tenantId);
        }

        $adminRoleName = $adminRole->name;
        $adminRoleId = (int) $adminRole->id;

        // STEP 3 — the candidate users, id ASC. DETERMINISTIC: two concurrent
        // admins each removing the other take the same row locks in the same
        // sequence, so one blocks and re-evaluates against committed state (EC-19).
        $candidateIds = $this->candidateQuery($tenantId, $adminRoleName)
            ->orderBy('users.id')
            ->lockForUpdate()
            ->pluck('users.id')
            ->all();

        // STEP 4 — the pivots for those users, in a deterministic order. Without
        // this a concurrent writer can detach the surviving admin's role between
        // step 3 and the caller's own write.
        if ($candidateIds !== []) {
            DB::table('model_has_roles')
                ->whereIn('model_id', $candidateIds)
                ->where('model_type', $target->getMorphClass())
                ->orderBy('model_id')
                ->orderBy('role_id')
                ->lockForUpdate()
                ->get();
        }

        // STEP 5 — decide from the locked read.
        $survivors = array_values(array_filter(
            $candidateIds,
            static fn (string $id): bool => $id !== (string) $target->id,
        ));

        if ($survivors === []) {
            throw $this->violationFactory->make($code, $adminRoleId);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Locks the admin-template role in wave 0b's canonical order and returns the
     * ROW. Rev 3 returns the model rather than the name, because every refusal
     * must carry role_id (EC-13; plan gate r2, MAJOR 2) and a name cannot supply
     * one. The predicate is tenant-OR-NULL because the legacy NULL-team topology
     * can carry the row without a tenant_id, exactly as wave 1's sync resolves
     * roles (spec 4.3.0 clause 3).
     */
    private function lockAdminRole(string $tenantId): ?Role
    {
        /** @var Role|null $role */
        $role = Role::query()
            ->where('template_key', 'admin')
            ->where(static fn (Builder $q): Builder => $q
                ->where('tenant_id', $tenantId)
                ->orWhereNull('tenant_id'))
            ->orderBy('name')          // wave 0b's order: roles.name ASC,
            ->orderBy('id')            //                  then roles.id ASC
            ->lockForUpdate()
            ->first();

        return $role;
    }

    /**
     * A, as a query. OQ-1's applied default is the principal_kind clause: a
     * SERVICE principal does NOT count toward A, because a tenant whose only
     * administrator is a machine has nobody who can log in when the token is
     * lost, revoked or expired. Task 2a-0 landed the column, so this is a real
     * predicate and not a comment (Q-w2-4).
     *
     * @return Builder<User>
     */
    private function candidateQuery(string $tenantId, string $adminRoleName): Builder
    {
        /** @var Builder<User> $query */
        $query = User::query()
            ->select('users.id')
            ->where('users.tenant_id', $tenantId)
            ->where('users.principal_kind', PrincipalKind::Human->value)
            ->where('users.status', UserStatus::Active->value)
            ->whereExists(static fn ($q) => $q
                ->from('user_company_memberships')
                ->whereColumn('user_company_memberships.user_id', 'users.id')
                ->where('user_company_memberships.status', 'active'));

        return $query->whereHas('roles', static fn ($q) => $q->where('name', $adminRoleName));
    }

    private function isAdminRole(string $roleName, string $tenantId): bool
    {
        return $this->adminRoleName($tenantId) === $roleName;
    }

    /** The unlocked read, used ONLY by the two early-return predicates above. */
    private function adminRoleName(string $tenantId): ?string
    {
        /** @var Role|null $role */
        $role = Role::query()
            ->where('template_key', 'admin')
            ->where(static fn (Builder $q): Builder => $q
                ->where('tenant_id', $tenantId)
                ->orWhereNull('tenant_id'))
            ->orderBy('name')
            ->orderBy('id')
            ->value('name');

        return $role;
    }
}
~~~

      > **Why the two early-return predicates may read unlocked.** `assertRoleRemovalSurvives()` and `assertRoleReplacementSurvives()` call `adminRoleName()` **before** taking any lock, purely to answer *"is this operation even about the admin role?"*. A stale answer there can only be wrong in the direction of **doing more work** — it sends a non-admin operation into `assertPopulationSurvivesWithout()`, which then locks, re-reads and finds survivors. It can never skip the floor for an operation that *is* about the admin role, because the only way the admin-template role's **name** changes is `RoleController::update`, which Task 2a-1 refuses on `is_system`. The locked read inside `assertPopulationSurvivesWithout()` is the one the decision is made from.

      > **The two floors emit `RoleUpdateRefused`, not `AuthorizationDenied`.** Every refusal — F-2's five codes and F-1's one — dispatches `RoleUpdateRefused` with `role_id`, the `RoleUpdateRefusalReason` case, `principal_id` and `token_id`, which is EC-13's required stable event (spec §4.6.5). It is **not** a denial: the caller *is* authorized to reach the endpoint, so Task 2a-7's `authz.denied` rule must not fire, and `RoleUpdateRefusedTest` asserts a `RoleUpdateRefused` row lands **and** an `authz.denied` count of zero.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

/**
 * The ONE construction site for a floor refusal, so the 422 body is one shape
 * across nine call sites. Constructor-injected (CLAUDE.md rule 13); it dispatches
 * the audit event and returns the exception the caller throws, so a refusal can
 * never be audited without being raised or raised without being audited.
 */
final readonly class LastAdminFloorViolation
{
    /** @var array<string, RoleUpdateRefusalReason> */
    private const REASONS = [
        'LAST_ADMIN_ROLE_REMOVAL' => RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminRoleRemoval,
        'LAST_ADMIN_DEACTIVATION' => RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminDeactivation,
        'LAST_ADMIN_DELETION' => RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminDeletion,
        'LAST_ADMIN_MEMBERSHIP_REMOVAL' => RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminMembershipRemoval,
    ];

    public function __construct(
        private \Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher $events,
        private \Illuminate\Contracts\Auth\Factory $auth,
        private CurrentAccessTokenId $tokenId,
    ) {}

    /**
     * $roleId is REQUIRED and has no default. EC-13's attribution contract has no
     * nullable arm, and rev 2's `?int $roleId = null` meant every one of the
     * floor's own call sites emitted role_id = null while the verification
     * checklist claimed otherwise (plan gate r2, MAJOR 2). The ONE case with no
     * role to name -- a tenant carrying no admin-template row at all -- is not a
     * floor refusal and throws AdminTemplateRoleMissingException instead.
     */
    public function make(string $code, int $roleId): LastAdminFloorViolationException
    {
        $reason = self::REASONS[$code] ?? throw new \LogicException(
            "LastAdminFloor emitted an undeclared refusal code '{$code}' — add it to "
            .'RoleUpdateRefusalReason and to this map, so the audit chain carries a closed set.',
        );

        $this->events->dispatch(new RoleUpdateRefused(
            roleId: $roleId,   // never null -- the parameter has no default
            reason: $reason,
            principalId: $this->auth->guard()->id() === null ? null : (string) $this->auth->guard()->id(),
            tokenId: $this->tokenId->current(),
            occurredAtIso: now()->toIso8601String(),
        ));

        return new LastAdminFloorViolationException($code, self::messageFor($reason));
    }

    private static function messageFor(RoleUpdateRefusalReason $reason): string
    {
        return match ($reason) {
            RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminRoleRemoval =>
                'This is the tenant\'s last active administrator. Grant the admin role to another '
                .'active user with a company membership before removing it from this one.',
            RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminDeactivation =>
                'This is the tenant\'s last active administrator and cannot be deactivated.',
            RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminDeletion =>
                'This is the tenant\'s last active administrator and cannot be deleted.',
            RoleUpdateRefusalReason::LastAdminMembershipRemoval =>
                'Removing this membership would leave the tenant with no administrator who has '
                .'access to any company.',
            default => throw new \LogicException('Unreachable: '.$reason->value),
        };
    }
}

/**
 * The thrown type. Rendered by the existing handler as 422 { code, message };
 * a controller may also catch it and return its own forbidden()-shaped 422. The
 * tests assert the CODE and the unchanged rows, never the rendering path.
 */
final class LastAdminFloorViolationException extends \RuntimeException
{
    public function __construct(
        public readonly string $errorCode,
        string $message,
    ) {
        parent::__construct($message);
    }
}

/**
 * The tenant carries NO role whose template_key is 'admin'. Wave 1's sync
 * guarantees the adopted row exists on every tenant, so this is a provisioning
 * defect, not an operator refusal -- and it is deliberately NOT a
 * LastAdminFloorViolationException, because there is no role to name and
 * EC-13's RoleUpdateRefused has no nullable role_id arm (plan gate r2, MAJOR 2).
 *
 * Rendered as 409 ADMIN_TEMPLATE_ROLE_MISSING. It still REFUSES the mutation --
 * fail-closed is preserved -- but it names the real problem, and the operator
 * remedy is `php artisan permissions:sync --tenant=<uuid>`, which the message
 * carries.
 */
final class AdminTemplateRoleMissingException extends \RuntimeException
{
    public const CODE = 'ADMIN_TEMPLATE_ROLE_MISSING';

    public function __construct(public readonly string $tenantId)
    {
        parent::__construct(
            "Tenant {$tenantId} carries no role with template_key = 'admin', so the "
            .'last-administrator floor cannot be evaluated. This tenant has never been '
            ."synced. Run: php artisan permissions:sync --tenant={$tenantId}",
        );
    }
}
~~~

      > **The role is resolved through `template_key`, never through the literal `'admin'`.** The NULL-team legacy topology means the row may carry `tenant_id IS NULL`, which is why the predicate is *tenant-or-null* exactly as wave 1's sync resolves roles (spec §4.3.0 clause 3). Hard-coding `'admin'` would silently mean "no admin role" on a tenant whose row is NULL-team, and the floor would then refuse **every** removal rather than none — fail-closed, but wrong.

      > **`CurrentAccessTokenId` and `CurrentUserId` are two two-line shared services, not `app()` calls or `Auth::` facade reads.** Both live in `apps/api/app/Shared/Infrastructure/Auth/` and both are created **here**, in Task 2a-2's commit, because four later sites consume them and must not disagree:
      > - **`CurrentAccessTokenId::current(): ?string`** — `$this->auth->guard()->user()?->currentAccessToken()?->id`, `null` in console and queued contexts. Consumed by Task 2a-5's `AuditService` edit, Task 2a-7's denial recorder and `RoleUpdateRefused`'s `token_id`.
      > - **`CurrentUserId::current(): ?string`** — `$id = $this->auth->id(); return $id === null ? null : (string) $id;`. Consumed by Task 2a-7's recorder and by **2b's `MembershipRevocationService`**, whose `recordTenantWide()` call needs an `?string $actorUserId` that is genuinely null under a queued worker rather than a `0` or an empty string. A bare `Auth::id()` inside the service would be a facade read in application code and would also make the forced-failure test unbindable.
      >
      > Both are constructor-injected everywhere (CLAUDE.md rule 13) and both are in Task 2a-2 Step 6's add list.

- [ ] **Step 3 — wire all writers.** Constructor-inject `LastAdminFloor` into `RoleController` and `UserController` (`private readonly`). **Each writer opens one transaction whose first statement is `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)`; the floor call is the statement after it, before any write.** (The floor acquires the lock itself as well — the re-entrancy note in the Global Constraints — so the two are consistent, not redundant: the controller's own acquisition is what wave 0b's `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest` enumerates, and the floor's is what makes the floor correct when called from a writer wave 0b has not enumerated.)
      | # | Writer | Lock | Floor call | Site to re-pin |
      |---|---|---|---|---|
      | 1 | `RoleController::removeRole` | already `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` (0b) | **`$this->lastAdminFloor->assertRoleRemovalSurvives($user, $roleName, (string) $actor->tenant_id);`** — the real call, in the real variables (see the note below) | `dev:390-410`, re-pinned onto 0b's rewritten body |
      | 2 | `UserController::deactivate` | **add to `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`** — it writes `users.status`, which the floor's *A* reads | `assertDeactivationSurvives` | `dev:630-642` |
      | 3 | `UserController::destroy` | **add to `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`** | `assertDeletionSurvives` | `dev:470-484` |
      | 4 | `RoleController::destroy` on the admin role | already `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` (0b) | **already refused** by `is_system` (Task 2a-1) — asserted, not re-guarded | `dev:272` |
      | 5 | **`UserController::update`'s `syncRoles`** | already `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` (0b) | `assertRoleReplacementSurvives($target, [$validated['role']], $tenantId)` | `dev:380-389` |
      | 6 | `RoleController::update` emptying `admin`'s permissions | already `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` (0b) | Floor **F-1**, Task 2a-3 | `dev:246` |
      | 7 | membership removal | **2b**, via `MembershipRevocationService` — which is a `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` writer in the commit that introduces it | `assertMembershipRemovalSurvives` — **wired in 2b**; 2a leaves `UserController`'s inline revocation at `dev:988-998` as it is and records it as a 2b obligation | `dev:988-998` |
      | 8 | impersonated calls to any of 1–6 | inherited from the controller | reach the same controllers; covered by the centralised service, asserted by the impersonation test case | — |
      | 9 | `permissions:sync` | wave 1's own (it acquires the lane key as its transaction's first statement) | `admin = activeKeys()` preserves the floor; wave 1's | — |
      > **Row 1's call is written in the variables the method actually has** (gate r3, Wave 2 **MAJOR 1**). Rev 3 prescribed `assertRoleRemovalSurvives($target, $role, $tenantId)` against a method whose own signature it supplies four pages earlier as `(User $target, string $roleName, string $tenantId)` — so it passed a role **object** where a role **name string** is required, and named two variables (`$target`, `$role`) that exist in neither the current body nor 0b's rewritten one.
      > **What `removeRole` looks like when wave 2 edits it, which is AFTER 0b:** 0b rev 6.3 rewrites this method in full, adding the transaction, the team boundary and the `FOR UPDATE` it has none of today (`5257eb0f1:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-0b.md:3176-3229`). Its variables are `$actor` (resolved and `abort_unless`-narrowed **before** the transaction), `$roleName` (`$validated['role']`), and `$user` (from `resolveTenantUser($userId)` **inside** the closure). The lock is `$this->permissionWriteLock->acquire((string) $actor->tenant_id)` — **`permissionWriteLock`, not `writeLock`**, per the producer-residual note in the header.
      > **So wave 2's diff to this method is ONE inserted line**, between `$user = $this->resolveTenantUser($userId);` and `$user->removeRole($roleName);`:
      > ```php
      > $this->lastAdminFloor->assertRoleRemovalSurvives($user, $roleName, (string) $actor->tenant_id);
      > ```
      > **Phase 0 re-reads 0b's body at the lane base** and re-pins the variable names before this line is written. Today's un-0b'd body at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php:390-410` resolves `$user` at `:394` and `$roleName` at `:397` but resolves `$actor` only at `:402`, **after** the removal — so against **today's** tree the insert would also have to hoist `$actor`. It does not, because 0b hoists it first; **if 0b has not merged, this task does not start** (Entry condition).

      **Two writers get the refusal but NO floor call, and both are declared rather than absent** (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-3** — rev 1's file inventory and this table disagreed): **`RoleController::assignRole`** and **`UserController::activate`** (with the membership restore at `dev:1057-1070`). Both can only *grow* *A* — assignment adds a holder, activation adds an active principal — so a floor call there could only ever pass, and a floor that can only pass is noise a later reader will delete for the wrong reason. Each is an `ASSERTED_EXEMPT` entry in Step 4's census with that one-line reason.
- [ ] **Step 4 — write `LastAdminFloorWriterCensusTest`.** Static analysis over `app/`, reusing **wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus`** (`tests/Architecture/Support/PermissionWriterCensus.php` — `collect()`, `writerMethods()`, `methodSource(array $unit)`, `callersOf(array $units, string $name)`; visitor constructed as `new PermissionWriterVisitor(string $relativePath, string $source)`) rather than a second AST walker, which would be a duplicate surface (convention 11). For every production call to `syncRoles`, `removeRole`, `assignRole`, `Role::…->users()->detach`, a `users.status` write and a membership revocation, the enclosing **method** must either reach a `LastAdminFloor` call or appear in an **`ASSERTED_EXEMPT`** map with a one-line stated reason.
      **The map ships with exactly these entries and is SHRINK-ONLY** — *A* is a **growing** set for each of them, which is the declared reason a floor call would be unreachable:
      ```php

          'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php::assignRole'
              => 'assigning a role can only ADD a holder to A',
          'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php::activate'
              => 'activation can only ADD an active principal to A (incl. the membership restore)',
          'app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/ServiceAccountProvisioningService.php::provision'
              => '2b: creates a principal_kind = service row, which never counts toward A (OQ-1)',
      ];
      ```
      A fourth entry needs a reviewer and an owner note; the test pins the map by **exact equality** so the exemption can only shrink.
- [ ] **Step 4a — extend wave 0b's `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS`** by `UserController::deactivate` and `UserController::destroy`, **in this commit**. `INITIALIZATION_ONLY` does not grow, and no wave-2 writer is added to `LOCK_INHERITED_FROM` (every one of them acquires in its own body). Wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus` markers (`ROLE_QUERY_CLASSES`, `ROLE_TABLE`, `ROLE_LEXICAL_MARKERS`) are **not** widened: if a new writer is not decidable at its call site, the repair is to make the chain decidable, not to widen the census (`…-wave-0b.md` rev 5, Task 15).

## Task 2a-2a: `refuseServiceTarget()` — the real predicate, and a census over the ENUMERATED write methods

> **New in rev 2** (**Q-w2-4**, plan gate r1 **BLOCKER B1-2** and **MAJOR M1-6**). Rev 1 shipped this helper with a `return null;` body in 2a and its census in 2b. Task 2a-0 has landed `principal_kind`, so it ships with behaviour, its census, and a runtime test — and the census is narrowed to the methods the accepted contract actually names.

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php` (five writes), `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php` (`assignRole`, `removeRole`)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceTargetRefusalTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest.php`

**Interfaces**

- Produces: one `private function refuseServiceTarget(User $target): ?JsonResponse` per controller — `null` when the target is a human, a 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES` body naming the `/service-accounts/*` route that does the job otherwise. **Two private methods, not a shared trait**: the two controllers return their own error-body shapes (`UserController::forbidden()` and `RoleController`'s inline shape) and a trait would have to pick one.
- Consumes: `User::isServicePrincipal()` (Task 2a-0).

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` first (red).** The service principal is inserted **through the model factory** with `principal_kind = 'service'`, `password = null`, `email = null`, `pos_pin = null`, `status = 'active'` — the shape §5.1's CHECK permits — so the refusal is exercised for real even though no *production* path can create such a row until 2b. Seven cases, each asserting the target's rows are **unchanged**:
      | Route | Method | Expected |
      |---|---|---|
      | `PATCH /users/{id}` | `update` | 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES`; `users` row and `model_has_roles` unchanged |
      | `DELETE /users/{id}` | `destroy` | 422; row not soft-deleted; tokens not revoked |
      | `POST /users/{id}/activate` | `activate` | 422; `users.status` unchanged |
      | `POST /users/{id}/deactivate` | `deactivate` | 422; `users.status` unchanged |
      | `POST /users/{id}/reset-password` | `resetPassword` | 422 **before** the `NO_EMAIL` branch at `dev:790` — the refusal is the **kind** check, not the accidental "no email" |
      | `POST /users/{userId}/roles` | `RoleController::assignRole` | 422 **before** any write; `model_has_roles` unchanged. **The sharpest one**: `users.assign-roles` alone would otherwise let a caller change a service principal's **owner grants**, which is the exact quantity `owner grants ∩ token scope` is written over |
      | `DELETE /users/{userId}/roles` | `RoleController::removeRole` | the same |
      Plus two negatives: **`GET /users` and `GET /users/{id}` still return the service principal** to a `users.view` caller (support must be able to see one exists), and **`PATCH /users/{id}/pos-pin` keeps its own, more specific `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN`** on both arms (`dev:706`, `dev:739`) — *"a machine cannot hold a PIN"* is a truer message than *"use the other route"*, and 2b's Task 2b-2 owns that code.
- [ ] **Step 2 — write the helper and its seven call sites.** First statement after the target row is resolved, before any write:
      ```php
      /**
       * A service principal is managed only through /service-accounts/*. This is the
       * generic surface's refusal; the PIN surface has its own, more specific one.
       */
      private function refuseServiceTarget(User $target): ?JsonResponse
      {
          if (! $target->isServicePrincipal()) {
              return null;
          }

          return $this->forbidden(
              'SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES',
              'This principal is a service account. Manage it under /api/v1/service-accounts/'
              .$target->id.' — the generic user routes do not apply to machine principals.',
              request(),
              422,
          );
      }
      ```
      > **`return null;` here is the helper's ANSWER, not a stub.** It reads a real column on a real row and means *"this target is a human; proceed"* — which is why Step 1 can assert both branches. That is exactly what rev 1's version could not do, because the column did not exist and the method returned `null` unconditionally.
- [ ] **Step 3 — write `UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest`, over an ENUMERATED write set.** Rev 1's rule was *"every public method on `UserController` OR `RoleController` that resolves a `users` row by route parameter calls the refusal"*, which is **over-broad and contradicts the accepted contract**: user *reads* stay open to `users.view` (spec §4.1.2's ruling), and `store` has no target row to refuse (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-6**). The census is therefore stated as a **closed list plus a closed exemption list**, both pinned by exact equality:
      ```php
      /** @var list<string>  every method that MUST call refuseServiceTarget() */
      private const MUST_REFUSE = [
          'UserController::update', 'UserController::destroy', 'UserController::activate',
          'UserController::deactivate', 'UserController::resetPassword',
          'RoleController::assignRole', 'RoleController::removeRole',
      ];

      /** @var array<string, string>  method => why it does NOT refuse */
      private const EXEMPT = [
          'UserController::index'      => 'read — users.view; support must see a service principal exists',
          'UserController::show'       => 'read — users.view',
          'UserController::store'      => 'no target row exists; CreateUserRequest may not set principal_kind',
          'UserController::setPosPin'  => 'refuses with the more specific SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN',
          'RoleController::userRoles'  => 'read — roles.view / users.assign-roles shaping (D4)',
      ];
      ```
      The **anti-growth direction** is what makes it a census rather than a list: any **other** public method on either controller that resolves a `users` row by route parameter **and performs a write** (an Eloquent save/update/delete, a `syncRoles`, a pivot attach/detach, or a raw `DB::table('users')->update`) and is in neither list **fails**, naming itself and pointing at both constants. A third controller acquiring a user-targeting write therefore cannot quietly become a fourth surface, while a new *read* is silently fine — which is the accepted contract.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run** (PG lane) and **commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceTargetRefusalTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest.php
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 0.4.2a: Refuse a service-account target on every generic user write and census the write set`**, both trailers.

## Task 2a-3: floor F-1 — the admin role's permission set is not operator-editable

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloor.php`
- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloorViolation.php`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AdminPermissionFloorTest.php`
- Modify: `RoleController.php` (`dev:223-266`, the unconditional `syncPermissions` at `dev:246`)

**The defect, verbatim.** `RoleController::update` guards **only the rename**: the check at `dev:229` fires only when `$request->has('name') && input('name') !== $role->name`, and `$role->syncPermissions($validated['permissions'])` at `dev:246` then runs **unconditionally, including on `admin`**. A `roles.manage` holder can `PATCH /api/v1/roles/{adminRoleId}` with `{"permissions": []}` and empty the tenant's admin role, **with no audit row** (`dev:223-266` emits none). It is latent today only because `roles.manage` is held by `admin` alone.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `AdminPermissionFloorTest` first (red).** EC-13, **including the deny path** — today's `dev:apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RBACTest.php:137-165` exercises an allow path only:
      - `PATCH /roles/{adminRoleId}` with `{"permissions": []}` → **422 `ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR`**, and `role_has_permissions` for that role is asserted **unchanged and equal to `PermissionRegistry::activeKeys()`**;
      - the same with a *subset* → 422, same assertion;
      - the same with a *superset* (a key not in `activeKeys()`) → 422, because the set is a function of the registry, not of the request;
      - `PATCH /roles/{adminRoleId}` with **only** `{"description": "…"}` and no `permissions` key → **200**, and the grant set is unchanged: the floor refuses an *attempt to set* permissions, it does not freeze the whole row;
      - the refusal is audited **as a floor refusal, not as a 403 denial** (spec EC-13's rev-2 note: the caller *is* authorized to reach the endpoint, so Task 2a-7's denial rule must not fire) — asserted by **a `RoleUpdateRefused` row landing on `audit_events` with `role_id` = the admin role, `reason` = `admin_permission_floor`, a non-null `principal_id` and a `token_id` that is present for a token call and absent for a session call**, and by counting `authz.denied` rows at **zero**. Rev 1 named the event nowhere and created only `RoleCreated`/`RoleUpdated`/`RoleDeleted`/`AuthorizationDenied`, so EC-13's stable event had no deliverable at all (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-4**);
      - a **non-admin** system role and a **custom** role both accept a permission edit normally, so the floor is proven passable.
- [ ] **Step 2 — write the floor.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role;

/**
 * Floor F-1 — spec 4.6.2. The admin role's permission set is a FUNCTION OF THE
 * REGISTRY (sync step 8: admin = activeKeys()), not an operator-editable field.
 * PATCH /roles/{id} on it therefore refuses any supplied `permissions`, rather
 * than silently ignoring it — a silent ignore is how an operator concludes the
 * save worked.
 */
final readonly class AdminPermissionFloor
{
    /**
     * Four collaborators, all constructor-injected (CLAUDE.md rule 13). Rev 2
     * declared only $registry while assertEditable() used $this->events,
     * $this->auth and $this->tokenId -- the class did not compile. CurrentAccessTokenId
     * is the SAME two-line service Task 2a-2 creates, so F-1 and F-2 cannot
     * disagree about what "the acting credential" means.
     */
    public function __construct(
        private PermissionRegistry $registry,
        private \Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher $events,
        private \Illuminate\Contracts\Auth\Factory $auth,
        private \App\Shared\Infrastructure\Auth\CurrentAccessTokenId $tokenId,
    ) {}

    public function guards(Role $role): bool
    {
        return $role->template_key === 'admin';
    }

    /**
     * @param  array<array-key, mixed>|null  $requestedPermissions  null = the request supplied none
     * @throws AdminPermissionFloorViolation
     */
    public function assertEditable(Role $role, ?array $requestedPermissions): void
    {
        if (! $this->guards($role) || $requestedPermissions === null) {
            return;
        }

        // role_id is ALWAYS present here: guards() already proved $role is the
        // admin-template row, so it is persisted and has an id (plan gate r2, MAJOR 2).
        $this->events->dispatch(new RoleUpdateRefused(
            roleId: (int) $role->id,
            reason: RoleUpdateRefusalReason::AdminPermissionFloor,
            principalId: $this->auth->guard()->id() === null ? null : (string) $this->auth->guard()->id(),
            tokenId: $this->tokenId->current(),
            occurredAtIso: now()->toIso8601String(),
        ));

        throw new AdminPermissionFloorViolation(
            "The admin role's permission set is derived from the permission registry ("
            .count($this->registry->activeKeys()).' active keys) and cannot be edited. '
            .'Grant or revoke capability by moving a user between roles, or by editing a non-admin role.',
        );
    }
}

/**
 * F-1's thrown type. Its code is fixed — there is exactly one way to violate
 * this floor — so it is a constant on the class rather than a constructor
 * argument that a caller could get wrong.
 */
final class AdminPermissionFloorViolation extends \RuntimeException
{
    public const CODE = 'ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR';

    public function __construct(string $message)
    {
        parent::__construct($message);
    }

    public function errorCode(): string
    {
        return self::CODE;
    }
}
~~~

      The constructor gains the same three collaborators the F-2 factory takes, all `private readonly` (CLAUDE.md rule 13):
      ```php
      public function __construct(
          private PermissionRegistry $registry,
          private \Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher $events,
          private \Illuminate\Contracts\Auth\Factory $auth,
          private CurrentAccessTokenId $tokenId,
      ) {}
      ```

- [ ] **Step 3 — call it in `RoleController::update`,** constructor-injected, **before** the rename guard and before `syncPermissions()` at `dev:246`. Pass `$request->has('permissions') ? $validated['permissions'] : null` so "absent" and "empty array" are different inputs — which is the whole distinction between case 4 and case 1 of the test.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run** (same PG env; `tests/Feature/Identity/AdminPermissionFloorTest.php tests/Feature/Compliance/RoleUpdateRefusedTest.php`) and **commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloor.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/AdminPermissionFloorViolation.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AdminPermissionFloorTest.php
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 0.4.3: Refuse operator edits to the admin role's permission set (floor F-1) and audit the refusal as RoleUpdateRefused`**, both trailers. The body states the latent-today reasoning so a reviewer does not read this as a fix for an observed incident, and states why the refusal is a `RoleUpdateRefused` rather than an `authz.denied`.

## Task 2a-4: D4 read gates — the payload shaping 0b-15 could not ship

**Files**

- Modify: `RoleController.php` — `index` (`dev:123-161`), `show` (`dev:162-186`), `permissions` (`dev:315-349`), `userRoles` (`dev:429-437`)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RoleReadShapingTest.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:58,77` — the two misleading comments become true statements

**Why this is 2a's and not 0b's.** Wave 0b task 0b-15 **gated** the four read routes (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:59,61,64,78`); it explicitly did not touch `RoleController`'s response builders, and recorded that as its residual **R-0b-8**. This task discharges it.

**The three shapes, spec §4.6.4.**

| Endpoint | `users.assign-roles` only | `roles.view` |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /roles` | `{id, name, is_system, users_count}` per role | the above **plus** `permissions` (and the three template columns) |
| `GET /users/{userId}/roles` | **`{user_id, roles: [{id, name, is_system}]}` — the `permissions` array is ABSENT** | the above plus `permissions` |
| `GET /users/{id}/effective-permissions` | **403** unless the caller *is* the subject | full payload (§4.6.6) — Task 2a-6 |

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `RoleReadShapingTest` first (red).** Assert on **rows and keys**, never on status alone:
      - a caller with `users.assign-roles` and **not** `roles.view` gets `GET /roles` 200 with `permissions` **absent from every element** (`assertArrayNotHasKey`, not `assertEmpty` — an empty array is a different, wrong, answer);
      - the same caller gets `GET /users/{userId}/roles` 200 with `permissions` **absent**, and `roles[*]` carrying exactly `id`, `name`, `is_system`;
      - a `roles.view` caller gets `permissions` **present and equal to the target's resolved key set**;
      - a caller with **neither** gets 403 on all four routes;
      - `GET /permissions` requires `roles.view` and 403s for a bare assigner;
      - **I-18's regression**: a caller with **no** role permission at all cannot read any tenant user's full permission list from `GET /users/{userId}/roles` — today it is ungated and returns `getAllPermissions()` for any authenticated caller.
- [ ] **Step 2 — shape the four builders.** One private `RoleController::shapeForGate(Request $request): bool` returning "may see permissions" (`$request->user()?->can(IdentityPermission::RolesView->value) === true`), consulted by all four. **`App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\IdentityPermission` is imported into `RoleController` in this commit** — the Global Constraints' no-bare-literal rule — and the `?->` / `=== true` shape is what keeps the `bool` return honest for the unauthenticated request the route can no longer produce but the signature still admits. **Not four independent `if`s** — convention 11 applied to a response shape.
- [ ] **Step 3 — `userRoles` keeps `getAllPermissions()` in THIS commit, and Task 2a-6 re-points it in its own.**
      > **Rev 4 left a dependency inversion here and papered it with a choice** (gate r4, Wave 2 **MAJOR 1**). It told `Phase 0.4.4` to call `EffectivePermissionResolver::forTarget()` — a class `Phase 0.4.6` creates — and then said *"either order is fine"* and *"the default order runs 2a-6 first"*, which contradicts both the document order and the ordinals. A commit that references a class two commits in its own future does not compile, and *"either order is fine"* is not an order.
      > **The ruling, and it is one sentence: the commit that CREATES the resolver is the commit that RE-POINTS its callers.** `Phase 0.4.4` does the D4 shaping only. `Phase 0.4.6` — whose add list **already** stages `RoleController.php` and whose Files block **already** names `RoleController::userRoles` — replaces the raw `getAllPermissions()` at `dev:429-437` with `EffectivePermissionResolverInterface::forTarget($user, null)` in the same diff that introduces the interface. **No ordinal moves, no task is resequenced, and neither commit references a symbol that does not yet exist.**
      In this commit `userRoles` therefore keeps its existing `getAllPermissions()` call and gains **only** Step 2's shaping. `RoleReadShapingTest`'s *"a `roles.view` caller gets `permissions` present and equal to the target's resolved key set"* case is green on both sides of `0.4.6` — the resolver's `forTarget($user, null)` returns the same unnarrowed set §4.1.3a rule 1 gives `getAllPermissions()` for an admin read, which is precisely why the re-point is behaviour-preserving and safe to carry in the resolver's own commit.
- [ ] **Step 4 — make the two route comments true** rather than deleting them: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:58` (*"Role management (requires roles.view or roles.manage permission)"*) and `:77` (*"User role assignment (requires users.assign-roles permission)"*).
- [ ] **Step 5 — run** and **commit** (`Phase 0.4.4: Shape the role read payloads by the gate that admitted the caller (D4, closing R-0b-8)`, both trailers). Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/RoleController.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RoleReadShapingTest.php
      ```
      **`routes.php` is in the list for Step 4's two comment corrections only** — 0b-15 already added the four `can:` gates and this task adds none. **The resolver files are NOT in this list and cannot be**: per Step 3 the re-point belongs to `Phase 0.4.6`, which creates them.

~~~php
final class RoleCreated extends DomainEvent
{
    /** @param list<string> $permissions */
    public function __construct(
        public readonly int $roleId,
        public readonly string $roleName,
        public readonly array $permissions,
        public readonly string $companyId,
        public readonly string $actorUserId,
        public readonly ?string $actorTokenId,
        public readonly string $occurredAtIso,
    ) {
        parent::__construct((string) $roleId);
    }

    public function getEventName(): string
    {
        return 'identity.role.created';
    }

    /** @return array<string, mixed> */
    public function getAuditPayload(): array
    {
        return [
            'role_id' => $this->roleId,
            'role_name' => $this->roleName,
            'permissions' => $this->permissions,
            'occurred_at' => $this->occurredAtIso,
        ];
    }
}
~~~

      **`RoleUpdated` and `RoleDeleted` are supplied in full, not described.** Rev 2 gave `RoleCreated` a body and instructed the implementer to *"follow it"* for the other two, which is the prose-stub standard this plan's own rule 1 forbids (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 6**).

~~~php
final class RoleUpdated extends DomainEvent
{
    /**
     * The DIFF, not the resulting set: a 300-entry array in every audit row is
     * unreadable and the question an auditor asks is "what changed". Both arrays
     * are computed BEFORE syncPermissions() runs, inside the same transaction --
     * afterwards the previous set is gone and the diff is unrecoverable.
     *
     * @param list<string> $added    keys the update GRANTS
     * @param list<string> $removed  keys the update REVOKES
     */
    public function __construct(
        public readonly int $roleId,
        public readonly string $roleName,
        public readonly ?string $previousName,   // null when the name did not change
        public readonly array $added,
        public readonly array $removed,
        public readonly string $companyId,
        public readonly string $actorUserId,
        public readonly ?string $actorTokenId,
        public readonly string $occurredAtIso,
    ) {
        parent::__construct((string) $roleId);
    }

    public function getEventName(): string
    {
        return 'identity.role.updated';
    }

    /** @return array<string, mixed> */
    public function getAuditPayload(): array
    {
        return [
            'role_id' => $this->roleId,
            'role_name' => $this->roleName,
            'previous_name' => $this->previousName,
            'added' => $this->added,
            'removed' => $this->removed,
            'occurred_at' => $this->occurredAtIso,
        ];
    }
}

final class RoleDeleted extends DomainEvent
{
    /**
     * Carries the FINAL permission set rather than a diff: after a delete there is
     * no "resulting set" to compare against, and the question an auditor asks here
     * is "what capability just disappeared".
     *
     * @param list<string> $permissions  the role's grants at the moment of deletion
     */
    public function __construct(
        public readonly int $roleId,
        public readonly string $roleName,
        public readonly array $permissions,
        public readonly string $companyId,
        public readonly string $actorUserId,
        public readonly ?string $actorTokenId,
        public readonly string $occurredAtIso,
    ) {
        parent::__construct((string) $roleId);
    }

    public function getEventName(): string
    {
        return 'identity.role.deleted';
    }

    /** @return array<string, mixed> */
    public function getAuditPayload(): array
    {
        return [
            'role_id' => $this->roleId,
            'role_name' => $this->roleName,
            'permissions' => $this->permissions,
            'occurred_at' => $this->occurredAtIso,
        ];
    }
}
~~~

      > **All three extend `DomainEvent` exactly as the siblings already in the tree do** (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/RoleAssigned.php:14-43`, `RoleRemoved.php:14-43`), and all three take `actorTokenId` from the same `CurrentAccessTokenId` service Task 2a-2 creates. `previousName` is `?string` because most updates do not rename; `RoleCreated`'s and `RoleDeleted`'s `permissions` are `list<string>` of key names, never `Permission` models.

      > **Immutable forever (CLAUDE.md rule 8).** The three names are the `identity.role.*` family the tree already uses for `identity.role.assigned` / `.removed`, and they are **unsuffixed** deliberately: an unsuffixed name that already exists cannot be versioned retroactively, so a v2 of one of these is `identity.role.updated.v2`, which is the same rule applied at the point it first can be. Wave 1's `RoleSyncedV1` / `identity.role.synced.v1` is the versioned form because it is a **new** family.

- [ ] **Step 2 — register all three in `DomainEventSubscriber`'s explicit list, in this commit.** An event class the subscriber does not register writes **no audit row, silently** — which is why Step 4's test asserts a **row lands**, not that the event was dispatched.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the attribution edit, in `AuditService::record()`, into the `$metadata` argument.**

~~~php
// apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Services/AuditService.php — immediately before the
// AuditEvent is constructed, and passed as the EXISTING metadata: argument (dev:72).
//
// NOT into the array_filter([...]) that is passed as attributes: (dev:73-81). That array
// reaches AuditEvent::__construct's $attributes parameter and goes straight to
// parent::__construct($attributes) (dev:.../Domain/AuditEvent.php:90-100) — Eloquent MASS
// ASSIGNMENT, filtered by $fillable (:44-64), which lists neither principal_id nor
// token_id. Only the separate $metadata argument is JSON-encoded into the metadata
// column (:133). An attributes: write would land NOWHERE AT ALL, with no exception.
// The impersonator_id / impersonation_session_id entries beside it work only because
// they ARE real columns on $fillable (:58-63).
$metadata = array_merge($metadata, array_filter([
    'principal_id' => $this->auth->id(),
    'token_id'     => $this->currentAccessTokenId(),
], static fn (mixed $v): bool => $v !== null));
~~~

      `principal_id` comes from the authenticated user and `token_id` from `currentAccessToken()`, **both null-safe for console and queued contexts** — no request means both are simply absent. One edit, one place, and every existing audited write inherits it: the same argument §4.1.3 makes for narrowing at `hasPermissionTo()`. **Constructor-inject** the auth guard and the token accessor; never `app()` and never the `auth()` helper inside the method.
- [ ] **Step 4 — write `RoleMutationAuditTest`**: create, update and delete a role and assert **a row lands on `audit_events`** for each, with the right `event_type`, the diff shape on update, and a non-null `company_id`.
- [ ] **Step 5 — write `AuditAttributionTest`**: the pair is **readable back off the persisted `metadata` column** (not merely passed in) for a **session** write, a **token** write and an **impersonated** write — and is **absent, not empty-string**, for a **queued** write. Assert `array_key_exists` on the decoded column.
- [ ] **Step 6 — write `RoleEventFamilyTest`**: no production path emits `RoleUpdated` from a console context, and none emits wave 1's `RoleSyncedV1` from an HTTP write other than *Re-apply template*. Static analysis over `app/`.
- [ ] **Step 7 — run** the three test files on the PG lane, then `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app/Modules/Compliance app/Modules/Identity`, then **commit** (`Phase 0.4.5: Audit every role mutation and attribute every audited write to its principal and token`, both trailers). Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/RoleCreated.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/RoleUpdated.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/RoleDeleted.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Listeners/DomainEventSubscriber.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Services/AuditService.php \

              apps/api/tests/Feature/Compliance/RoleMutationAuditTest.php \

      ```
      **`CurrentAccessTokenId.php` is NOT in this list** — Task 2a-2 creates it and stages it there; this task **consumes** it. Staging it again would be an empty hunk, and its absence here is what proves the two tasks agree on one definition of *"the acting credential"*. The body must carry the `$metadata`-versus-`$attributes` reasoning verbatim — it is the correction rev 3 of the spec owed rev 2, and a reviewer who does not read it will "simplify" the edit back into the wrong argument.
