## Task 2a-6: `EffectivePermissionResolver`, and `GET /users/{id}/effective-permissions`

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Shared/Contracts/Authorization/EffectivePermissionResolverInterface.php`, `EffectivePermissionResolver.php`, `EffectivePermissionSet.php`, `EffectivePermissionEntry.php`, `TokenScopeData.php`
- Modify: `UserController.php` (the new action), `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php` (the new route), `RoleController::userRoles`, the Identity service provider (the interface binding)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/EffectivePermissionResolverTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/EffectivePermissionsEndpointTest.php`

**Two resolutions, never one (spec §4.1.3a (c)).** *"The permissions of the caller"* and *"the permissions of some principal an admin is reading about"* are different questions and get different methods.

> **Rev 2 replaces rev 1's signature sketch with a real interface and a real implementation** (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-3**). Rev 1 declared `forSubject()` and `forTarget()` with semicolons and no bodies inside a `final readonly class`, which does not parse, and specified neither the owner-grant lookup, the `permission:` parsing, the unknown-ability handling, the `token_id` selector semantics nor the DTO assembly — so the `owner grants ∩ token scope` guarantee was a claim rather than a property anyone could check.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1a — the contract.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Shared\Contracts\Authorization;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\EffectivePermissionSet;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
use Laravel\Sanctum\PersonalAccessToken;

interface EffectivePermissionResolverInterface
{
    /**
     * The AUTHENTICATED CALLER's effective set: the subject's live grants
     * INTERSECTED with the scope of the token THIS VERY REQUEST presented.
     * $token is the request's own token — null for a session request, in which
     * case the set is the full grants and every entry is in_token_scope = true.
     */
    public function forSubject(User $subject, ?PersonalAccessToken $token): EffectivePermissionSet;

    /**
     * An ADMIN READ of another principal: the target's live grants, optionally
     * PROJECTED through ONE EXPLICITLY SELECTED token of that target.
     *
     * $tokenId is an explicit selector — never the caller's token, and never an
     * implicit "the target's token", because a principal may own several.
     * $tokenId === null means "grants as they stand, no token projection".
     *
     * @throws TokenNotOwnedBySubject when $tokenId names a token the target does
     *         not own — the endpoint renders it as 404, never as an empty scope,
     *         because "no such token" and "a token that grants nothing" are
     *         different answers.
     */
    public function forTarget(User $target, ?int $tokenId): EffectivePermissionSet;
}
~~~

      Bound in `IdentityServiceProvider::register()` — `$this->app->bind(EffectivePermissionResolverInterface::class, EffectivePermissionResolver::class);` — and **every consumer type-hints the interface**, never the class (`RoleController::userRoles`, `UserController`'s new action, Task 2a-7's denial middleware).

- [ ] **Step 1b — the implementation, in full.**

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization;

use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\EffectivePermissionEntry;
use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\EffectivePermissionSet;
use App\Modules\Identity\Application\DTOs\TokenScopeData;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
use App\Shared\Contracts\Authorization\EffectivePermissionResolverInterface;
use App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
use Laravel\Sanctum\PersonalAccessToken;

/**
 * Spec 4.1.3a (c). The ONE place that answers "what may this principal do",
 * with the two questions kept apart:
 *
 *   forSubject  -- owner grants INTERSECTED with the presented token's scope.
 *   forTarget   -- owner grants, optionally projected through ONE named token.
 *
 * It never authorizes. can: still does that, through User::hasPermissionTo(),
 * which is where the narrowing is ENFORCED (Global Constraints). This class is
 * the READ model over the same rule, and EffectivePermissionResolverTest
 * asserts the two agree key-for-key -- a read model that can disagree with the
 * enforcement point is worse than no read model.
 */
final readonly class EffectivePermissionResolver implements EffectivePermissionResolverInterface
{
    /** The one bridge between a catalogue key and a Sanctum ability string. */
    private const SCOPE_PREFIX = 'permission:';

    public function __construct(private PermissionRegistry $registry) {}

    public function forSubject(User $subject, ?PersonalAccessToken $token): EffectivePermissionSet
    {
        return $this->assemble($subject, $token);
    }

    public function forTarget(User $target, ?int $tokenId): EffectivePermissionSet
    {
        if ($tokenId === null) {
            return $this->assemble($target, null);
        }

        /** @var PersonalAccessToken|null $token */
        $token = $target->tokens()->whereKey($tokenId)->first();

        if ($token === null) {
            throw new TokenNotOwnedBySubject($tokenId, (string) $target->id);
        }

        return $this->assemble($target, $token);
    }

    /**
     * OWNER GRANTS -- deliberately the UNNARROWED set, read from the database
     * rather than from the request's subject.
     *
     * User::getAllPermissions() narrows only when the model IS the authenticated
     * subject carrying currentAccessToken() (dev:.../User.php:201-241). For
     * forTarget that model is NOT the authenticated subject, so the call returns
     * the full grants -- correct, and now correct BY DECLARATION. For forSubject
     * we must not let that accidental narrowing happen twice, so the owner set is
     * read through getUnfilteredPermissionsForSupportAccess() (dev:176-179), the
     * accessor that already means "grants before any token filter", and the
     * intersection below is the ONLY narrowing this class performs.
     *
     * @return array<string, list<string>>  permission key => role names granting it
     */
    private function ownerGrants(User $principal): array
    {
        $byKey = [];

        foreach ($principal->roles()->with('permissions')->get() as $role) {
            foreach ($role->permissions as $permission) {
                $byKey[$permission->name][] = $role->name;
            }
        }

        // D6: no direct principal->permission grants exist in this design. The
        // unfiltered accessor is nonetheless the oracle, so a future direct grant
        // cannot silently disappear from this panel: anything it returns that no
        // role explains is attributed to the principal itself.
        foreach ($principal->getUnfilteredPermissionsForSupportAccess() as $permission) {
            $byKey[$permission->name] ??= ['(direct grant)'];
        }

        foreach ($byKey as $key => $roles) {
            $byKey[$key] = array_values(array_unique($roles));
            sort($byKey[$key]);
        }

        ksort($byKey);

        return $byKey;
    }

    /**
     * PARSE the token's narrowing list.
     *
     * Returns null  -> the token does not narrow (no `permission:` ability at
     *                  all, or no token): the principal's full grants apply.
     * Returns list  -> the keys the scope names, INCLUDING ones the registry does
     *                  not know; splitting known from unknown is the caller's job
     *                  below, because "unknown" must be VISIBLE (rule 4) rather
     *                  than silently dropped here.
     *
     * `*` is NOT honoured: it is meaningful to Sanctum's tokenCan(), not to this
     * intersection. A token carrying BOTH `*` and a `permission:` ability is
     * rejected upstream by EnforceTokenScope with TOKEN_SCOPE_AMBIGUOUS (401), so
     * by the time this runs the combination cannot occur; we therefore ignore a
     * lone `*` rather than treating it as "everything", which would WIDEN.
     *
     * @return list<string>|null
     */
    private function scopeKeys(?PersonalAccessToken $token): ?array
    {
        if ($token === null) {
            return null;
        }

        /** @var list<string> $abilities */
        $abilities = $token->abilities ?? [];

        $keys = [];

        foreach ($abilities as $ability) {
            if (str_starts_with($ability, self::SCOPE_PREFIX)) {
                $keys[] = substr($ability, strlen(self::SCOPE_PREFIX));
            }
        }

        // No `permission:` ability => unscoped. An EMPTY list is impossible here
        // by construction (we would have returned null); a token whose scope
        // narrows to nothing is one carrying `permission:` abilities that name
        // only unknown keys, and that denies everything -- which is rule 2,
        // preserved rather than special-cased.
        return $keys === [] ? null : array_values(array_unique($keys));
    }

    private function assemble(User $principal, ?PersonalAccessToken $token): EffectivePermissionSet
    {
        $owner = $this->ownerGrants($principal);
        $scope = $this->scopeKeys($token);
        $known = $this->registry->keys();

        $granted = [];
        $unknown = [];

        if ($scope !== null) {
            foreach ($scope as $key) {
                if (! in_array($key, $known, true)) {
                    $unknown[] = $key;          // rule 4: dropped from the
                    continue;                    // intersection, but SURFACED
                }

                if (array_key_exists($key, $owner)) {
                    $granted[] = $key;           // rule 1: NARROWING ONLY --
                }                                // a scope key the principal does
            }                                    // not hold grants nothing
        }

        $entries = [];

        foreach ($owner as $key => $grantedBy) {
            $definition = $this->registry->get($key);

            $entries[] = new EffectivePermissionEntry(
                key: $key,
                module: $definition?->module ?? '(retired)',
                granted_by: $grantedBy,
                in_token_scope: $scope === null || in_array($key, $granted, true),
            );
        }

        return new EffectivePermissionSet(
            principal: [
                'id' => (string) $principal->id,
                'kind' => $principal->principalKind()->value,
                'status' => $principal->status,
            ],
            roles: $principal->roles->map(static fn ($r): array => [
                'id' => $r->id,
                'name' => $r->name,
                'is_system' => (bool) $r->is_system,
                'template_key' => $r->template_key,
                'template_version' => $r->template_version,
                'customised_at' => $r->customised_at?->toIso8601String(),
            ])->all(),
            permissions: $entries,
            // token_scope keys on $scope, NOT on $token (plan gate r2, MAJOR 4).
            // The two are different questions: $token !== null means "a credential
            // was used"; $scope !== null means "that credential NARROWS". An
            // UNSCOPED token -- a plain session-equivalent bearer token carrying no
            // `permission:` ability -- must render token_scope: null, because the
            // payload's contract is "null = the principal's full grants apply"
            // (Task 2b-5 Step 2's AuthUserData comment says exactly that). Rev 2
            // keyed on $token, so an unscoped token rendered
            // {granted: [], unknown: []} -- which reads as "this token grants
            // NOTHING", the precise opposite of the truth.
            token_scope: ($scope === null || $token === null) ? null : new TokenScopeData(
                token_id: (string) $token->id,
                token_name: (string) $token->name,
                granted: $granted,
                unknown: $unknown,
                expires_at: $token->expires_at?->toIso8601String(),
            ),
            company_scope: $principal->activeCompanyIds(),
            location_scope: $principal->allowedLocationIdsByCompany(),
            discount: $principal->discountLimits(),
        );
    }
}

/** `token_id` named a token the subject does not own. Rendered as 404. */
final class TokenNotOwnedBySubject extends \RuntimeException
{
    public function __construct(public readonly int $tokenId, public readonly string $subjectId)
    {
        parent::__construct("Token {$tokenId} does not belong to principal {$subjectId}.");
    }
}
~~~

      > **`EffectivePermissionEntry` is a DTO, not an array** (CLAUDE.md rule 3, no `mixed`): `final readonly class EffectivePermissionEntry { public function __construct(public string $key, public string $module, /** @param list<string> $granted_by */ public array $granted_by, public bool $in_token_scope) {} }`, in `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/DTOs/`. `EffectivePermissionSet` is its container and is `#[TypeScript]`-attributed so `php artisan typescript:transform` generates the panel's type — **never a hand-rolled FE interface beside a generated DTO** (convention 11).
      > **The three scope accessors** — `activeCompanyIds()`, `allowedLocationIdsByCompany()`, `discountLimits()` — are read-only helpers on `User`. `activeCompanyIds()` and `allowedLocationIdsByCompany()` are thin wrappers over `user_company_memberships` (`company_id`, `allowed_location_ids`); `discountLimits()` returns the `{can_discount, max_discount_percent}` pair the POS already computes. **Phase 0 greps for existing equivalents before any is written** — if `LocationContext` already exposes one, the resolver consumes it rather than adding a second.
      > **Rev 1's `?int $tokenId` is kept**, not widened to a string: `personal_access_tokens.id` is a `bigIncrements`, and the endpoint validates `token_id` as `integer` before the resolver sees it.

      Rules the split makes checkable, each asserted in Step 3:
      1. **The `getAllPermissions()` override narrows only when the model is the authenticated subject carrying `currentAccessToken()`.** That is already what `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:201-241` does; this wave states it as the **contract** rather than leaving it as an accident, and asserts it.
      2. **`RoleController::userRoles()` uses `forTarget($user, null)`** — correct for an admin read, and now correct **by declaration** rather than by luck.
      3. **`GET /users/{id}/effective-permissions` dispatches on WHO IS ASKING, not only on the selector** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**). Rev 2 routed every call through `forTarget()`, so a caller reading **their own** effective permissions got the **unnarrowed** grants of whatever token they were holding — the accepted contract (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md:1777-1781`) says the self-read uses `forSubject()` and **projects the request token**. Three arms, and the endpoint test asserts each by its **payload**, not by its status:
         | Call | Resolution | `token_scope` |
         |---|---|---|
         | `{id}` **is** the caller, **no** `token_id` | **`forSubject($caller, $request->user()?->currentAccessToken())`** | the **request token's** projection — `null` if that token does not narrow (or the call is a session call), the narrowed set otherwise |
         | `{id}` is the caller **with** an explicit `token_id` | `forTarget($caller, $tokenId)` — an explicit selector is an explicit question about *that* credential, even about one's own | that token's projection |
         | `{id}` is **another** principal (requires `roles.view`) | `forTarget($target, $tokenId)` | `null` with no selector; that token's projection with one |
         `token_scope` is `null` whenever the resolved token does not narrow (rule 4 below), and the endpoint renders `TokenNotOwnedBySubject` as **404** when the id names a token the subject does not own — never as an empty scope, because *"no such token"* and *"a token that grants nothing"* are different answers.
      3a. **An UNSCOPED token yields `token_scope: null`, not an empty projection.** `scopeKeys()` returns `null` for a token carrying no `permission:` ability; `assemble()` keys the DTO on **that**, never on whether a token object exists (**MAJOR 4**). `{granted: [], unknown: []}` means *"this credential grants nothing"*, which is the opposite of what an unscoped token does.
      4. **The POS PIN payload keeps the PIN holder's own grants, unchanged** — `PosAuthController::verifyPin` (`dev:…/PosAuthController.php:87-100`, the pluck at `:99`) resolves a PIN holder from the database, so by rule 1 it is **not** narrowed by the terminal's token. That is the correct semantics: the payload answers *"what may this operator approve"*, not *"what may this terminal's credential do"*. **This task changes nothing there** and asserts the non-change.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the endpoint, its gate and its DISPATCH.** `GET /api/v1/users/{id}/effective-permissions[?token_id=]`, in the existing Identity group. Gate: **the caller IS the subject, OR `can:roles.view`** — `users.assign-roles` alone is **not** sufficient (D4: an assigner needs role *names*, not the resolved matrix).
      **The action body, in full, because rev 2's one-line `forTarget()` was the defect** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**):
      ```php
      public function effectivePermissions(Request $request, string $id): JsonResponse
      {
          $caller = $request->user();
          $isSelf = $caller !== null && (string) $caller->id === $id;

          if (! $isSelf && ! $caller?->can(IdentityPermission::RolesView->value)) {
              return $this->forbidden(
                  'FORBIDDEN',
                  __('auth.permission_denied', ['ability' => IdentityPermission::RolesView->value]),
                  $request,
                  403,
              );
          }

          $validated = $request->validate(['token_id' => ['sometimes', 'integer']]);
          $tokenId = isset($validated['token_id']) ? (int) $validated['token_id'] : null;

          // SELF read with NO selector: forSubject, projecting the REQUEST token.
          // This is the accepted contract (spec 4.1.3a (c), lines 1777-1781) and it
          // is what makes /users/me/effective-permissions answer "what may I do
          // RIGHT NOW, through THIS credential" rather than "what do my roles say".
          if ($isSelf && $tokenId === null) {
              /** @var PersonalAccessToken|null $requestToken */
              $requestToken = $caller->currentAccessToken();

              return response()->json(['data' => $this->resolver->forSubject($caller, $requestToken)]);
          }

          $target = $isSelf ? $caller : User::query()->findOrFail($id);

          // An EXPLICIT token_id is an explicit question about that credential --
          // including one's own -- so it goes through forTarget in both cases.
          return response()->json(['data' => $this->resolver->forTarget($target, $tokenId)]);
      }
      ```
      Payload exactly §4.6.6's shape: `principal{id,kind,status}`, `roles[]{id,name,is_system,template_key,template_version,customised_at}`, `permissions[]{key,module,granted_by,in_token_scope}`, `token_scope`, `company_scope`, `location_scope`, `discount`.
      > `granted_by` names **every** role contributing the key — the single most useful field when an operator asks *"why can they do that?"*. `in_token_scope` is `true` for a session caller. The **discount numerics are surfaced read-only**: D6 keeps them out of the catalogue, but hiding them from the one screen that answers "what can this person actually do" would be the same mistake in a different place (I-17).
      > `kind` reads `users.principal_kind`, which **Task 2a-0 has already landed** (Q-w2-4). It is `'human'` for every principal a production path can create until 2b ships `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`, but it is a real column read, not a constant — `EffectivePermissionResolverTest` asserts `'service'` on a factory-inserted service row.
- [ ] **Step 3 — write `EffectivePermissionResolverTest` and `EffectivePermissionsEndpointTest`.** EC-18's extension: `forTarget()` returns **unnarrowed** grants for a target with a scoped token while `forSubject()` returns the **intersection** for the same principal on its own request. Plus: another subject with only `users.assign-roles` is **403**; a `token_id` the target does not own is **404**; `granted_by` names both roles when two grant the same key.
      **Four cases assert the rev-3 corrections, and every one asserts the PAYLOAD rather than the status** — rev 2's endpoint test checked only status, so it would have gone green on the wrong result (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**, **MAJOR 4**):
      | Case | Request | Asserted |
      |---|---|---|
      | `a_self_read_projects_the_request_token` | the caller reads **their own** `{id}` with **no** `token_id`, holding a **narrowed** token | `token_scope.granted` equals the narrowed subset and `permissions[].in_token_scope` is `false` for a key the token excludes. **Under rev 2's `forTarget()` routing this field would be `null` and every entry `true`** |
      | `a_self_read_on_an_unscoped_token_has_a_null_token_scope` | the same caller holding an **unscoped** token | `token_scope` is **`null`** — `assertNull`, never `assertEmpty`, because `{granted: [], unknown: []}` is the wrong answer, not a smaller one (**MAJOR 4**) |
      | `a_self_read_on_a_session_call_has_a_null_token_scope` | the same caller on a session (cookie) call | `token_scope` is `null`; `permissions[].in_token_scope` is `true` throughout |
      | `an_explicit_token_id_on_ones_own_id_reads_that_token` | the caller reads their own `{id}` **with** `?token_id=` naming a *different* token they own | `token_scope.token_id` is the **selected** token, not the request's |
      **The scoped-token half of these tests needs 2b's `tokenScopePermissionNames()`.** In 2a the token narrowing is still impersonation-gated, so the 2a form of the assertion uses an **impersonation** token (which already carries `permission:` abilities) and **2b widens the same test to a plain scoped token** in the commit that generalises the override. Stated here so a reviewer does not read the 2a test as proving the general case.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run**, `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app/Modules/Identity app/Shared/Contracts/Authorization`, **commit.** Exact add list:

      Subject: **`Phase 0.4.6: Split effective-permission resolution into forSubject and forTarget behind one interface and expose it as a gated read`**, both trailers.
      **One case is the wave's own proof of the intersection guarantee**: `EffectivePermissionResolverTest::the_read_model_agrees_with_the_enforcement_point` iterates every key in `PermissionRegistry::keys()` for a scoped subject and asserts `in_token_scope === $subject->hasPermissionTo($key)` — a read model that can disagree with `can:` is worse than no read model.

## Task 2a-7: 403 denial events on write routes, with the atomic Redis dedup

**Files**

- Create: `AuthorizationDenied.php`, `DenialCause.php`, `AuthorizationDenialRecorder.php`, **`DeniedAbilityTrail.php`**
- Modify: `DomainEventSubscriber.php` (the `AuthorizationDenied` registration), `AppServiceProvider.php` (the scoped `DeniedAbilityTrail` binding and its `GateEvaluated` listener), `bootstrap/app.php` (**the 403 render callback ONLY**)
> **`RecordAuthorizationDenial.php` is NOT created and `bootstrap/app.php`'s middleware group and priority list are NOT touched.** Rev 3 withdrew the middleware in Step 3 and left this header still naming it and still promising a middleware attach four pages earlier (gate r3, Wave 2 **MINOR 2**). The header now matches Step 3: a diff in this commit that touches `$middleware->appendToGroup` or `appendToPriorityList` is rev 2's withdrawn design resurfacing.
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialDedupTest.php` (**Redis integration**), `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialScopeTest.php`

**Scope, deliberately narrow.** A denial event is emitted when a `can:` / `require.any.permission` middleware or a `Gate::authorize` refuses a request on a **write-verb** route. **Reads are excluded deliberately**: a UI that renders from a stale permission set produces read 403s in bulk, and drowning the audit log is how denial logging gets switched off. Payload: `{route_name, method, uri_template, permission, principal_id, token_id, company_id, ip}` — **never the request body**, which can carry money, PII or a PIN.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the event.** `AuthorizationDenied extends DomainEvent`, `getEventName(): string { return 'authz.denied'; }`, registered in `DomainEventSubscriber` **in this commit**, frozen on merge (a later shape change is `AuthorizationDeniedV2`). Rev 2 of the spec gave the three role events that treatment and left this one without it; CLAUDE.md rule 8 draws no distinction.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the recorder, with two Redis keys.** One expiring counter cannot do the job: when the key expires its count is gone with it.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Authorization;

use App\Modules\Company\Services\CompanyContext;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\DenialCause;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Events\AuthorizationDenied;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
use App\Shared\Contracts\Authorization\EffectivePermissionResolverInterface;
use App\Shared\Infrastructure\Auth\CurrentAccessTokenId;
use Illuminate\Auth\Access\AuthorizationException;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher as EventDispatcher;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Redis\Factory as RedisFactory;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Log;
use Symfony\Component\HttpFoundation\Response as HttpResponse;
use Symfony\Component\HttpKernel\Exception\AccessDeniedHttpException;
use Throwable;

/**
 * Spec 4.6.5's dedup/rate rule. TWO keys per (principal_id, permission, route_name):
 *
 *   authz:deny:lock:<hash>   emission lock, SET ... NX EX 300.
 *                            Acquired => "emit now". Not acquired => "suppress".
 *   authz:deny:count:<hash>  retained counter, INCR on every suppression, with a
 *                            ROLLING 24-HOUR TTL refreshed on each write so it
 *                            deliberately OUTLIVES the 5-minute lock.
 *
 * When the next emission wins the lock it ATOMICALLY reads and clears the counter
 * and carries its value as suppressed_count on the emitted event.
 *
 * THE THREE PRODUCERS THIS CLASS SERVES, and the parameter each one uses:
 *
 *   1. `AuthorizationException` rendered via the hook -- `can:` middleware
 *      (Laravel's Authorize calls Gate::authorize), `$this->authorize()`,
 *      AuthorizesAbility's PermissionDeniedException, and a bare Gate::authorize()
 *      anywhere. The render callback hands us AccessDeniedHttpException::getPrevious(),
 *      which IS that AuthorizationException. `$previous` carries it; `$cause` is null
 *      and is DECIDED by cause() below.
 *   2. `AccessDeniedHttpException` itself -- a caller that hands us the CONVERTED
 *      Symfony type rather than unwrapping it. Accepted and unwrapped one level.
 *      `abort(403)` constructs exactly this type with a NULL previous
 *      (Illuminate\Foundation\Application::abort()), so it is excluded by construction
 *      rather than by a route-stack heuristic.
 *   3. A 403 BUILT WITHOUT THROWING -- `RequireAnyPermission`
 *      (dev:apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php:19-31 returns a
 *      response; it never throws) and 2b's `EnforceTokenScope`. There is no exception
 *      to type on, so these callers STATE THEIR OWN CAUSE: they pass `$cause`, and a
 *      non-null `$cause` IS the attribution. This is the arm rev 4 had no parameter for
 *      (gate r4, Wave 2 BLOCKER 1): the method rejected every call whose `$previous`
 *      was not an AuthorizationException, which is every call these two middleware
 *      could possibly make.
 *
 *   The ungated `Gate::authorize()` case is NOT a fourth producer: it arrives through
 *   producer 1 (the framework type), and `GateEvaluated` -- via DeniedAbilityTrail --
 *   is how its ABILITY becomes recoverable, not how its denial becomes attributable.
 */
final readonly class AuthorizationDenialRecorder
{
    private const LOCK_TTL_SECONDS = 300;
    private const COUNT_TTL_SECONDS = 86400;

    private const WRITE_VERBS = ['POST', 'PUT', 'PATCH', 'DELETE'];

    /**
     * GETDEL semantics with an explicit floor, executed as ONE Redis command.
     *
     * A MULTI/EXEC wrapping GET + DEL is an accepted equivalent (both are atomic
     * against other clients); Redis 6.2+ GETDEL is a third. The contract is ONE
     * ATOMIC OPERATION, not a particular spelling. Two round trips lose every
     * increment that lands between the GET and the DEL — under exactly the storm
     * this rule exists to describe — and always UNDERCOUNT, which is the failure
     * mode that makes an audit number untrustworthy.
     */
    private const READ_AND_CLEAR_LUA = <<<'LUA'
        local n = redis.call('GET', KEYS[1])
        if n == false then return 0 end
        redis.call('DEL', KEYS[1])
        return tonumber(n)
        LUA;

    public function __construct(
        private RedisFactory $redis,
        private EventDispatcher $events,
        private DeniedAbilityTrail $deniedAbilities,
        private CurrentAccessTokenId $currentAccessTokenId,
        private CompanyContext $companyContext,
        private EffectivePermissionResolverInterface $resolver,
    ) {}

    /** @return int|null suppressed_count when this call should EMIT; null when it should SUPPRESS */
    public function shouldEmit(string $principalId, string $permission, string $routeName): ?int
    {
        $hash = hash('sha256', $principalId.'|'.$permission.'|'.$routeName);
        $connection = $this->redis->connection();

        $acquired = $connection->set('authz:deny:lock:'.$hash, '1', 'EX', self::LOCK_TTL_SECONDS, 'NX');

        if (! $acquired) {
            $countKey = 'authz:deny:count:'.$hash;
            $connection->incr($countKey);
            $connection->expire($countKey, self::COUNT_TTL_SECONDS);   // ROLLING, refreshed each write

            return null;
        }

        /** @var int $suppressed */
        $suppressed = $connection->eval(self::READ_AND_CLEAR_LUA, 1, 'authz:deny:count:'.$hash);

        return $suppressed;
    }

    /**
     * The RECORDING entry point ALL THREE producers call.
     *
     * Contract, in one sentence: EMIT when all three hold -- the verb is a WRITE
     * verb, the rendered status is 403, and the denial is ATTRIBUTABLE TO A GATE.
     *
     * @param  HttpResponse  $response  the ALREADY-BUILT 403. Symfony's base type, because the
     *                                  render callback and both middleware build an
     *                                  Illuminate\Http\JsonResponse, which extends
     *                                  Symfony\Component\HttpFoundation\JsonResponse and NOT
     *                                  Illuminate\Http\Response — a narrower hint would refuse
     *                                  every real caller.
     * @param  ?string  $ability  the key the caller already knows, when it knows one.
     *                            `RequireAnyPermission` passes its JOINED key list; the render
     *                            callback passes what it recovered from PermissionDeniedException;
     *                            `EnforceTokenScope` passes null and lets the chain below recover
     *                            the attempted route's own gate.
     * @param  ?Throwable  $previous  producer 1's AuthorizationException, or producer 2's
     *                                AccessDeniedHttpException, or null for producer 3.
     * @param  ?DenialCause  $cause  producer 3 STATES its cause. Non-null also means "attributable":
     *                               a first-party 403 builder is the attribution.
     */
    public function recordGateDenial(
        Request $request,
        HttpResponse $response,
        ?string $ability,
        ?Throwable $previous,
        ?DenialCause $cause = null,
    ): void {
        if (! in_array($request->method(), self::WRITE_VERBS, true)) {
            return;                       // reads are excluded DELIBERATELY (spec 4.6.5)
        }

        if ($response->getStatusCode() !== 403) {
            return;                       // EnforceTokenScope's three 401 arms fall out here
        }

        if (! $this->attributableToAGate($previous, $cause)) {
            return;
        }

        $user = $request->user();

        if (! $user instanceof User) {
            return;                       // an unauthenticated 403 has no principal to attribute
        }

        // THE ABILITY, in three tries, never by parsing a message string:
        //   1. $ability            -- what the caller already knows (see the docblock)
        //   2. the GateEvaluated trail -- the ability of the LAST denied Gate check in
        //      this request. Laravel dispatches GateEvaluated($user, $ability, $result,
        //      $arguments) from Gate::raw() on EVERY inspection, including the one
        //      Gate::authorize() is about to throw on, so the ability is recoverable
        //      for the ungated-controller case that has no can: to read.
        //   3. the route's own can: parameter, for the middleware case.
        $permission = $ability
            ?? $this->deniedAbilities->last()
            ?? $this->abilityFromRouteStack($request);

        if ($permission === null) {
            // A 403 we cannot name a key for is a FINDING, not a silent drop.
            Log::warning('authz.denied: gate denial with no recoverable ability', [
                'method' => $request->method(),
                'uri' => $request->route()?->uri(),
            ]);

            return;
        }

        $routeName = $request->route()?->getName() ?? ($request->method().' '.($request->route()?->uri() ?? ''));
        $suppressed = $this->shouldEmit((string) $user->id, $permission, $routeName);

        if ($suppressed === null) {
            return;                       // inside the 5-minute window; counted, not emitted
        }

        $this->events->dispatch(new AuthorizationDenied(
            routeName: $routeName,
            method: $request->method(),
            uriTemplate: (string) ($request->route()?->uri() ?? ''),
            permission: $permission,
            principalId: (string) $user->id,
            tokenId: $this->currentAccessTokenId->current(),
            companyId: $this->companyContext->getCompanyId(),
            ip: (string) $request->ip(),
            cause: $cause ?? $this->cause($user, $permission),
            suppressedCount: $suppressed,
        ));
    }

    /**
     * The attribution predicate, over the THREE producer shapes and nothing else.
     *
     * It is exact in both directions: every gate-raised denial reaches it as an
     * AuthorizationException (case 2 below) or wrapped in the Symfony type (case 3),
     * while a bare abort(403) reaches it as an AccessDeniedHttpException with a NULL
     * previous and is refused.
     */
    private function attributableToAGate(?Throwable $previous, ?DenialCause $cause): bool
    {
        if ($cause !== null) {
            return true;                  // producer 3: a first-party 403 builder stating its cause
        }

        if ($previous instanceof AuthorizationException) {
            return true;                  // producer 1: can:, $this->authorize(), bare Gate::authorize()
        }

        // producer 2: the converted Symfony type, unwrapped ONE level. abort(403) has
        // no previous and therefore returns false here.
        return $previous instanceof AccessDeniedHttpException
            && $previous->getPrevious() instanceof AuthorizationException;
    }

    /** The can: / require.any.permission: parameter on the resolved route, if any. */
    private function abilityFromRouteStack(Request $request): ?string
    {
        foreach ($request->route()?->gatherMiddleware() ?? [] as $m) {
            if (! is_string($m)) {
                continue;
            }

            if (str_starts_with($m, 'can:')) {
                return substr($m, 4);
            }

            if (str_starts_with($m, 'require.any.permission:')) {
                return substr($m, strlen('require.any.permission:'));   // the joined list
            }
        }

        return null;
    }

    /**
     * DenialCause is DECIDED, not guessed: ask the resolver whether this principal
     * WOULD hold the key with an unscoped token. Yes => the token narrowed it;
     * no => the principal simply lacks the grant.
     *
     * Reached ONLY on the render-callback path; producer 3 supplies its own cause.
     */
    private function cause(User $user, string $permission): DenialCause
    {
        return $this->resolver->forSubject($user, null)->has($permission)
            ? DenialCause::TokenScope
            : DenialCause::PrincipalGrants;
    }
}
~~~
      > **This is the WHOLE class, and it is the whole class deliberately** (gate r4, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 1**). Rev 4 printed a body whose only import was `RedisFactory` while it used ten other symbols, declared a one-argument constructor inside the class and then a six-argument replacement *outside* it, and gave `recordGateDenial()` no way for a caller to state a cause. All three are closed above: **every** symbol the body names is imported, there is **exactly one** constructor and it is inside the class block, and `$cause` is the fifth parameter.

      **`DeniedAbilityTrail` — the mechanism that makes an ungated `Gate::authorize()` attributable**, and it is fifteen lines. It is a **request-scoped singleton** fed by a listener on Laravel's own `GateEvaluated` event:
      ~~~php
      <?php

      declare(strict_types=1);

      namespace App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Authorization;

      /**
       * The abilities this request's Gate checks DENIED, in order.
       *
       * WHY THIS EXISTS: Gate::authorize('pos.operate_terminal') throws a vanilla
       * Illuminate\Auth\Access\AuthorizationException whose message is "This action is
       * unauthorized." and which carries NO ability (dev:apps/api/app/Shared/Exceptions/
       * PermissionDeniedException.php:9-13 says so in as many words, which is why that
       * subclass exists at all). On an AUTH_ONLY route there is also no can: to read.
       * So for the ungated-controller case -- 91 POS routes today -- the ability is
       * recoverable ONLY from the Gate itself.
       *
       * Laravel dispatches GateEvaluated($user, $ability, $result, $arguments) from
       * Gate::raw() on EVERY inspection, BEFORE Response::authorize() throws
       * (vendor/laravel/framework/src/Illuminate/Auth/Access/Gate.php:434-459), so the
       * denial that is about to become the 403 is always the LAST entry here.
       *
       * Request-scoped: bound as a scoped singleton, so it cannot leak between requests
       * in Octane or in a test that makes two calls.
       */
      final class DeniedAbilityTrail
      {
          /** @var list<string> */
          private array $abilities = [];

          public function push(string $ability): void
          {
              $this->abilities[] = $ability;
          }

          public function last(): ?string
          {
              return $this->abilities === [] ? null : $this->abilities[array_key_last($this->abilities)];
          }
      }
      ~~~
      and its listener, registered in `AppServiceProvider::boot()` beside the existing event wiring:
      ~~~php
      // AppServiceProvider::boot()
      $this->app->scoped(DeniedAbilityTrail::class);

      $app = $this->app;

      Event::listen(static function (GateEvaluated $event) use ($app): void {
          // $result is bool|null; null means "no policy/callback matched", which is a
          // denial for our purposes exactly as false is.
          if ($event->result !== true && is_string($event->ability)) {
              $app->make(DeniedAbilityTrail::class)->push($event->ability);
          }
      });
      ~~~
      > **This listener is observational and cannot change an authorization outcome**, which is why it is `GateEvaluated` rather than `Gate::after`. A `Gate::after` callback's return value participates in `$result ??= $afterResult` (`vendor/laravel/framework/src/Illuminate/Auth/Access/Gate.php:586-599`); an event listener's does not. **A recorder must never be able to grant.**
      > **The trail takes the LAST denied ability, and that is exact for the throwing path**: `Gate::authorize()` dispatches `GateEvaluated` and then immediately throws, so nothing can be pushed between the two. It is a heuristic only for a controller that calls `Gate::allows()` (denied, handled gracefully) and *later* raises a different denial — and that case is excluded anyway, because a graceful `allows()` does not produce an `AuthorizationException`, and a bare `abort(403)` after it has no `previous` and is not recorded at all.

- [ ] **Step 3 — the recorder hangs off the EXCEPTION RENDER CALLBACK, not off a pipeline middleware.**

      > **Rev 2's design could not observe the 403 it was written to record** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B4**). `RecordAuthorizationDenial` was a middleware whose `handle()` called `$next($request)` and inspected the returned response for a marker an exception renderer had set. In this application that response **never reaches it**: `can:` and `Gate::authorize()` raise `Illuminate\Auth\Access\AuthorizationException`, Laravel's `prepareException()` converts it into a Symfony `AccessDeniedHttpException`, and the 403 is produced by the **`withExceptions` render callback** — an exception thrown inside the pipeline unwinds *past* every middleware above it and is rendered by the handler, so `$next($request)` never returns and the middleware's body after it never runs. The existing code says so in as many words at `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:264-269` (*"Laravel's prepareException() converts AuthorizationException into a Symfony AccessDeniedHttpException (with the original as `previous`) BEFORE render callbacks match"*). Rev 2's stated positive test would have been red for framework-flow reasons, not for product behaviour.
      >
      > **The orchestrator's ruling: record at the point the 403 is actually rendered.** The application already has exactly one such point, and it already recovers the ability: the `AccessDeniedHttpException` render callback registered at `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:270`, whose `FORBIDDEN` body is built at `:299-307` (`'code' => 'FORBIDDEN'`, `'ability' => $ability`, status `403`), with the `ReturnDecisionForbiddenException` special case at `:287-297` and the ability recovered from `getPrevious()` at `:275-276`.

      1. **The recording site is that render callback, and the record happens AFTER the response is built.** The callback keeps its current shape and gains three lines before each `return`:
         ```php
         // apps/api/bootstrap/app.php — inside the EXISTING
         // $exceptions->render(function (AccessDeniedHttpException $e, Request $request) { … })
         // registered at dev:270. The two return sites are dev:288-296 (the
         // ReturnDecisionForbiddenException arm) and dev:299-307 (the FORBIDDEN arm);
         // BOTH get the same three lines, because both are authorization denials.

         $response = response()->json([
             'error' => [
                 'code' => 'FORBIDDEN',
                 'message' => $ability !== null
                     ? __('auth.permission_denied', ['ability' => $ability])
                     : __('auth.permission_denied_generic'),
                 'ability' => $ability,
             ],
         ], 403);

         // Record AFTER the response is built, never before: the event carries the
         // rendered status, and the recorder must not be able to replace the 403.
         // It is wrapped because an audit write must never turn a clean 403 into a 500.
         try {
             Container::getInstance()
                 ->make(AuthorizationDenialRecorder::class)
                 ->recordGateDenial($request, $response, $ability, $previous);
         } catch (\Throwable) {
             // swallowed deliberately; the denial itself is the contract, the audit
             // row is best-effort. AuthorizationDenialScopeTest asserts the 403 body
             // is byte-identical with the recorder bound to a throwing double.
         }

         return $response;
         ```
         > **`Container::getInstance()->make()` here is the ONE sanctioned container read in this wave, and it is stated rather than smuggled.** CLAUDE.md rule 13 governs application classes — a collaborator takes its dependencies through its constructor. `bootstrap/app.php` **is** the composition root: it is where the container is configured, it is not a class, and it has no constructor to inject into. Resolving **inside** the callback rather than at bootstrap time is deliberate — the recorder depends on Redis, and resolving it while the application is still being built would bind a connection before the providers that configure it have registered. **`BootstrapContainerReadCensusTest`** (new, `tests/Architecture/`) pins this to **exactly one** site — and rev 4 states exactly what it counts, because rev 3's stated census **could not pass against the tree** (gate r3, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 2**).
         > **What rev 3 said, and why it was unsatisfiable.** It said the combined occurrences of `Container::getInstance()`, `app(` and `resolve(` must be **exactly one** in `bootstrap/app.php` and **zero** in `app/`. Both halves are false today and neither is this wave's to fix: `bootstrap/app.php` already calls `app()` at `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:218,221-222,240` (the Sentry scope enrichment), and `app/` carries pre-existing container-helper calls such as `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Accounting/Presentation/Requests/CreateJournalEntryRequest.php:31`. A census a wave cannot make green on the day it lands is not a guard; it is a red test the next person deletes.
         > **What it counts instead: one AST node, identified exactly.** The test parses `bootstrap/app.php` with `nikic/php-parser` — the dependency wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus` already uses — and asserts there is **exactly one** `StaticCall` whose class is `Container` and whose method is `getInstance`. That is the sanctioned site, and it is a shape **nothing else in the file has**: the four pre-existing reads are `app(` **function calls**, not `Container::getInstance()`. The assertion is therefore about **this** construct, not about container reads in general — and rev 5 adds the two structural assertions that make it prove RELOCATION as well as count.
         > **Rev 4's second assertion did not prove relocation** (gate r4, Wave 2 **MAJOR 3**). `SANCTIONED_LINE_MARKER` was the literal text `Container::getInstance()`, so moving the call to **any** other line whose trimmed source is that same expression passed unchanged — the marker proved a string, not a site.
         > **Rev 5 pins WHAT the call is and WHERE it sits, both structurally.** Two assertions replace the marker, and neither reads a line number:
         ~~~php
         /** The one sanctioned expression, printed canonically — not a source line. */
         private const SANCTIONED_EXPRESSION =
             'Container::getInstance()->make(AuthorizationDenialRecorder::class)'
             .'->recordGateDenial($request, $response, $ability, $previous)';

         public function test_bootstrap_has_exactly_one_sanctioned_container_read(): void
         {
             $source = (string) file_get_contents(base_path('bootstrap/app.php'));
             $ast = (new ParserFactory())->createForHostVersion()->parse($source) ?? [];
             $finder = new NodeFinder();

             /** @var list<Node\Expr\StaticCall> $calls */
             $calls = $finder->find(
                 $ast,
                 static fn (Node $n): bool => $n instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall
                     && $n->class instanceof Node\Name
                     && str_ends_with($n->class->toString(), 'Container')
                     && $n->name instanceof Node\Identifier
                     && $n->name->toString() === 'getInstance',
             );

             self::assertCount(
                 1,
                 $calls,
                 'bootstrap/app.php may contain exactly ONE Container::getInstance() — the authorization-denial '
                 .'render callback. A second one needs a reviewer and an entry in this test.',
             );

             // WHAT: the enclosing expression, printed canonically. A relocated
             // `Container::getInstance()` that is no longer THIS call fails here.
             $enclosing = $finder->findFirst(
                 $ast,
                 static fn (Node $n): bool => $n instanceof Node\Expr\MethodCall
                     && $n->name instanceof Node\Identifier
                     && $n->name->toString() === 'recordGateDenial',
             );
             self::assertInstanceOf(Node\Expr\MethodCall::class, $enclosing);
             self::assertSame(
                 self::SANCTIONED_EXPRESSION,
                 (new Standard())->prettyPrintExpr($enclosing),
                 'the sanctioned container read is no longer the recorder call it is sanctioned FOR',
             );

             // WHERE: it sits inside the AccessDeniedHttpException render closure and
             // nowhere else. This is what "relocated" actually means, and it is a
             // structural fact rather than a line number.
             $closure = $finder->findFirst(
                 $ast,
                 static fn (Node $n): bool => $n instanceof Node\Expr\Closure
                     && isset($n->params[0])
                     && $n->params[0]->type instanceof Node\Name
                     && str_ends_with($n->params[0]->type->toString(), 'AccessDeniedHttpException'),
             );
             self::assertInstanceOf(Node\Expr\Closure::class, $closure);
             self::assertCount(
                 1,
                 $finder->find([$closure], static fn (Node $n): bool => $n instanceof Node\Expr\StaticCall
                     && $n->class instanceof Node\Name
                     && str_ends_with($n->class->toString(), 'Container')),
                 'the sanctioned Container::getInstance() moved OUT of the AccessDeniedHttpException '
                 .'render closure; that is a relocation and needs a reviewer',
             );
         }
         ~~~
         **`SANCTIONED_LINE_MARKER` is deleted.** The two assertions above subsume it: a second call fails the count, a *different* call at the same text fails the printed expression, and a call moved out of the render closure fails the containment assertion. `PhpParser\PrettyPrinter\Standard` is imported beside `NodeFinder` and `ParserFactory`; all three ship with `nikic/php-parser`, which wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus` already depends on.
         > **And a SECOND, separate assertion for `app/`, expressed as a SHRINK-ONLY ceiling rather than as zero.** `app/`'s existing `app(` / `resolve(` call sites are legacy debt this wave does not own (CLAUDE.md rule 13 governs new code; the enforcement layer already ratchets the old). The test counts them, asserts `count <= APP_CONTAINER_READ_CEILING`, and **sets the ceiling from the count measured in the commit that creates the file** — the same shrink-only shape as every other ratchet in this programme, and the same reason: a ceiling that starts at today's number is green on day one and can only fall, while a ceiling of zero is red on day one and gets deleted.
         > **Phase 0 takes both numbers before the test is written:**
         ```
         cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w2a/apps/api
         grep -c 'Container::getInstance()' bootstrap/app.php                      # expect 0 before, 1 after
         grep -rn --include='*.php' -E '(^|[^a-zA-Z_>$])(app|resolve)\(' app/ | wc -l   # the ceiling's starting value
         ```
         **Both are recorded in the handback**, and the second is written into `APP_CONTAINER_READ_CEILING` literally rather than guessed here.

      2. **`RecordAuthorizationDenial` the middleware DOES NOT EXIST in this plan.** It is withdrawn along with `AuthorizationDenialMarker` and `AuthorizationDenialContext`: a marker written by a gate and read by a middleware only makes sense if a middleware can read it, and it cannot. Nothing is added to `bootstrap/app.php`'s middleware groups or its priority list by this task.

      3. **Three call sites, because there are three places a 403 is produced, and each records at its own.** The render callback is the only one this task adds; the other two call the recorder directly because they build their own response and never throw:
         | Producer | Where it records | Cause |
         |---|---|---|
         | `can:` middleware and `Gate::authorize()` | the `AccessDeniedHttpException` render callback above (`dev:bootstrap/app.php:270-308`) — **the single point every thrown authorization denial passes through**, which is exactly why it is the right site | decided by rule 4 below |
         | `RequireAnyPermission` (`dev:apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php:13-35`) | directly, immediately after it builds its 403, with the joined key list as the permission | `DenialCause::PrincipalGrants`, **stated** |
         | `EnforceTokenScope` (**2b**) | directly, in its **403** arm only — its three 401 arms are not denials of a permission and the recorder's status guard drops them | `DenialCause::TokenScope`, **stated** |
         **The two direct call sites are IMPLEMENTED here, not described** (gate r4, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 1**: rev 4 required both to "state their own cause" through a method that had no cause parameter and printed neither call). `RequireAnyPermission` becomes, in this commit:
         **`apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php` — the WHOLE file after this task.** Today it has no constructor (`dev:13-35`); middleware **is** resolved from the container, so rule 13 applies and is satisfiable here.
         ~~~php
         <?php

         declare(strict_types=1);

         namespace App\Http\Middleware;

         use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\DenialCause;
         use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Authorization\AuthorizationDenialRecorder;
         use Closure;
         use Illuminate\Http\Request;
         use Symfony\Component\HttpFoundation\Response;
         use Throwable;

         /** Require at least one of the route's comma-separated permissions. */
         final class RequireAnyPermission
         {
             public function __construct(
                 private readonly AuthorizationDenialRecorder $recorder,
             ) {}

             public function handle(Request $request, Closure $next, string ...$permissions): Response
             {
                 $user = $request->user();
                 $allowed = $user !== null && collect($permissions)->contains(
                     static fn (string $permission): bool => $user->can($permission),
                 );

                 if ($allowed) {
                     return $next($request);
                 }

                 // The body is UNCHANGED from dev:21-31 — this task adds the record, never
                 // a new error contract.
                 $response = response()->json([
                     'error' => [
                         'code' => 'FORBIDDEN',
                         'message' => 'You do not have permission to view transaction destinations.',
                     ],
                     'meta' => [
                         'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
                         'request_id' => $request->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
                     ],
                 ], 403);

                 try {
                     $this->recorder->recordGateDenial(
                         request: $request,
                         response: $response,
                         ability: implode(',', $permissions),   // the JOINED list, as the table says
                         previous: null,                        // it never throws — hence the cause
                         cause: DenialCause::PrincipalGrants,
                     );
                 } catch (Throwable) {
                     // swallowed deliberately, exactly as the render callback's arm: an audit
                     // write must never turn a clean 403 into a 500.
                 }

                 return $response;
             }
         }
         ~~~
         and `EnforceTokenScope`'s 403 arm (Task 2b-3 Step 2's fourth check) is:
         ~~~php
         // apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Middleware/EnforceTokenScope.php
         // — the SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_NO_MEMBERSHIP arm. The other three checks return 401
         //   and are NOT recorded: they refuse a credential, not a permission.
         $response = response()->json([
             'error' => [
                 'code' => TokenScopeFailure::ServicePrincipalNoMembership->value,
                 'message' => __('auth.service_principal_no_membership'),
             ],
         ], 403);

         try {
             $this->recorder->recordGateDenial(
                 request: $request,
                 response: $response,
                 ability: null,                      // no key of its own; the chain recovers the
                                                     // ATTEMPTED route's can: parameter, and logs a
                                                     // warning when the route carries none
                 previous: null,
                 cause: DenialCause::TokenScope,
             );
         } catch (Throwable) {
         }

         return $response;
         ~~~
         > **`AuthorizationDenialRecorder` is constructor-injected into both** (`private readonly`, rule 13) and `RequireAnyPermission.php` is already in this task's add list; `EnforceTokenScope.php` is 2b's file and its constructor gains the recorder in `Phase 0.5.3`, whose add list already stages `AuthorizationDenialRecorder.php` for exactly this reason.
         **`abort(403)` and controller-authored 403s record NOTHING, and that is a property of the exception graph rather than a heuristic.** `recordGateDenial()` emits only when **all three** hold: the verb is a **write** verb (`POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE`); the rendered status is **403**; and the denial is **attributable to a gate**, which `attributableToAGate(?Throwable $previous, ?DenialCause $cause)` decides over the **three producer shapes** the class serves — a **non-null `$cause`** (producer 3: `RequireAnyPermission` and `EnforceTokenScope`, which build a 403 and never throw, so they state their own cause), `$previous instanceof AuthorizationException` (producer 1: `can:`, `$this->authorize()`, a bare `Gate::authorize()`), or `$previous instanceof AccessDeniedHttpException` **whose own previous** is an `AuthorizationException` (producer 2: the converted Symfony type, unwrapped one level).
         > **Rev 5 left the superseded ONE-producer predicate in this sentence** — *"`$e->getPrevious() instanceof AuthorizationException`"* alone (plan gate r5, Wave 2 **MINOR 1**). That is the rev-4 predicate the same revision's `attributableToAGate()` replaced, and read literally it refuses **every** call `RequireAnyPermission` and `EnforceTokenScope` can make, which is the exact defect gate r4's BLOCKER 1 closed. The three-arm form above is the one the supplied body implements.
         **`abort(403)` is still excluded, and by construction rather than by a route-stack heuristic:** `Illuminate\Foundation\Application::abort()` constructs an `AccessDeniedHttpException` with a **null** previous, so it matches none of the three arms.
         > **Rev 3's attribution rule missed the spec's ungated `Gate::authorize()` arm** (gate r3, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 3**). It emitted only when `$previous instanceof PermissionDeniedException` **or** the route stack already carried `can:` / `require.any.permission:`. A plain in-controller `Gate::authorize('pos.operate_terminal')` on an `AUTH_ONLY` route has **neither** signal — and the accepted contract requires *every* write-verb refusal from `can:`, `require.any.permission` **or `Gate::authorize()`** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md:1745-1749`). **91 POS routes are exactly that shape today** (wave 3's sweep is what converts them), so rev 3's rule would have recorded nothing for the largest ungated write surface in the application — and its own positive test chose a controller denial on a `can:`-gated route, which cannot expose the omission.
         > **The corrected predicate is the FRAMEWORK TYPE, and it is exact in both directions.** Every gate-raised denial — `can:` middleware (Laravel's `Authorize` calls `Gate::authorize`), `$this->authorize()`, `AuthorizesAbility`'s `PermissionDeniedException`, and a bare `Gate::authorize()` anywhere — raises `Illuminate\Auth\Access\AuthorizationException`, which `prepareException()` wraps as `AccessDeniedHttpException` **with the original as `previous`**. A bare `abort(403)` constructs `new AccessDeniedHttpException($message)` **directly, with `previous` null** (`Illuminate\Foundation\Application::abort()`), so it reaches the same callback — as the comment at `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:268-269` says — and is excluded with no route-stack read at all. `PermissionDeniedException extends AuthorizationException` (`dev:apps/api/app/Shared/Exceptions/PermissionDeniedException.php:17`), so the narrow case is a subset of the broad one and nothing that used to be recorded stops being recorded.
         > **How the route and the permission are obtained at that point, since the question is fair:** the callback's second parameter **is** the `Request`, and `$request->route()` is populated — the route was resolved before the pipeline ran, and an exception thrown inside a controller does not unbind it. So `route_name` is `$request->route()?->getName()`, falling back to `"METHOD uri"` for the two-thirds of the API that declares no name; `uri_template` is `$request->route()?->uri()`; `method` is `$request->method()`; `company_id` is `CompanyContext::getCompanyId()`; `token_id` is `CurrentAccessTokenId::current()`; `principal_id` is `$request->user()->id`.
         > **The permission name comes from the gate, never from a parsed message**, in three tries: (1) `$previous->ability` when the denial came through `AuthorizesAbility`; (2) **`DeniedAbilityTrail::last()`** — the ability of the last denied `Gate` inspection in this request, captured from Laravel's own `GateEvaluated` event, which is what makes the **ungated** `Gate::authorize()` case attributable; (3) the `can:` / `require.any.permission:` parameter off `gatherMiddleware()`. **A 403 for which none of the three yields a key is logged as a warning and emits nothing** — a finding, not a silent drop, and `AuthorizationDenialScopeTest`'s seventh negative asserts exactly that.
         > `LOCATION_ESCALATION` at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:933-940` is `forbidden()`-shaped — it **returns** a response rather than raising — so it never reaches this callback at all. A **422** floor refusal reaches no `AccessDeniedHttpException` handler either, which is EC-13's requirement, and Task 2a-2/2a-3's `RoleUpdateRefused` is what records those instead.

      4. **`DenialCause` is decided, not guessed.** `EnforceTokenScope` and `RequireAnyPermission` state their own cause. On the render-callback path the recorder asks `EffectivePermissionResolverInterface::forSubject($user, null)` whether the principal *would* hold the key with an **unscoped** token — `TokenScope` if yes, `PrincipalGrants` if no. That distinction is the first question anyone debugging an MCP integration asks. In 2a the `TokenScope` branch is reachable only for an impersonation token; 2b makes it general.

      5. **The negatives are as load-bearing as the positive.** Without them *"only authorization-gate 403s"* is an intention rather than a property — see Step 5.
      On emit, the recorder dispatches `AuthorizationDenied` with `suppressed_count` and the eight named payload fields.
- [ ] **Step 4 — write `AuthorizationDenialDedupTest` as a REDIS INTEGRATION TEST.** The fast lane cannot express `SET NX EX` semantics or TTL divergence, and asserting SQLite counter arithmetic would assert a **different algorithm** than the one shipping. Cases:
      - one emission per 5-minute window per triple;
      - the suppressed count **survives lock expiry** (advance time past the lock TTL, not past the counter TTL);
      - the count is **carried and cleared exactly once**;
      - two different triples do **not** share state;
      - **no suppression is lost to a concurrent increment (EC-28)** — driven from a **second connection racing the script from outside**, with `INCR`s landing immediately before and immediately after the script runs, asserting the **outcome**: the sum of `suppressed_count` over two emissions equals the number of suppressions actually performed. *(Not an interleaving "between the read and the clear": Lua execution is atomic against other clients, and that atomicity is the property the script is chosen for, so no such interleaving exists to drive.)*
      Env: `REDIS_HOST=127.0.0.1 REDIS_PORT=6379 REDIS_DB=14 CACHE_STORE=redis`. The test flushes **DB 14 only**, in `setUp` and `tearDown`.
- [ ] **Step 5 — write `AuthorizationDenialScopeTest`** on the PG lane. **Three positives and six negatives**, plus one robustness case — the count is stated exactly because rev 4 said *"one positive and six negatives"* while listing three positives (gate r4, Wave 2 **MINOR 1**), and the scope rule is the whole design:
      - a write-verb `can:` 403 lands **one** row on `audit_events`, whose payload carries the eight named fields and **no request body**. **This is the case rev 2 could not have made green**: its middleware never saw the response (**B4**);
      - a write-verb `Gate::authorize()` 403 raised from inside a controller on a `can:`-gated route lands one row — proving the render callback, not the route stack alone, is what observes it;
      - **a write-verb `Gate::authorize('<a dotted key>')` 403 raised from inside a controller on an `AUTH_ONLY` route — no `can:`, no `require.any.permission:`, and a vanilla `AuthorizationException` rather than `PermissionDeniedException` — lands one row, and its `permission` is the key the controller named.** **This is the case rev 3 could not have made green** (gate r3, **BLOCKER 3**): it is the shape of 91 POS routes today, its ability is recoverable only from `DeniedAbilityTrail`, and rev 3's positive deliberately chose a `can:`-gated route, so the omission was invisible to its own suite;
      - a **read**-verb 403 lands **none**;
      - an `ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR` **422** lands none — and lands a `RoleUpdateRefused` row instead (EC-13);
      - a **controller-authored** 403 lands none: `PATCH /users/{id}` refused with `LOCATION_ESCALATION` by `UserController`'s own `forbidden()` membership check at `dev:…/UserController.php:933-940`, which never raises and therefore never reaches the render callback;
      - a bare **`abort(403)`** on a route whose stack carries **no** `can:` / `require.any.permission:` lands none — it *does* reach the render callback (`dev:bootstrap/app.php:268-269`), which is exactly why the attributable-to-a-gate condition exists;
      - a **404** on a missing row lands none;
      - **a gate denial whose ability is not recoverable by any of the three sources lands none and logs a warning** — asserted with `Log::spy()`, so *"we could not name the key"* is observable rather than an empty audit table.
      **Plus one robustness case:** with `AuthorizationDenialRecorder` bound to a double whose `recordGateDenial()` throws, the 403 response body and status are **byte-identical** to the same request with the real recorder. An audit write must never turn a clean denial into a 500.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run both,** then **commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Events/AuthorizationDenied.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Enums/DenialCause.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Infrastructure/Authorization/AuthorizationDenialRecorder.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Infrastructure/Authorization/DeniedAbilityTrail.php \
              apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php \
              apps/api/app/Http/Middleware/RequireAnyPermission.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Listeners/DomainEventSubscriber.php \
              apps/api/bootstrap/app.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/BootstrapContainerReadCensusTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialDedupTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/AuthorizationDenialScopeTest.php
      ```
      **Three files rev 2 staged are NOT in this list and do not exist** — `AuthorizationDenialContext.php`, `AuthorizationDenialMarker.php` and `RecordAuthorizationDenial.php`. The marker/middleware pair is withdrawn with the design that needed it (**B4**); `BootstrapContainerReadCensusTest.php` replaces them.
      **Two files are NEW in rev 4** — `DeniedAbilityTrail.php` and `apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` — because the ungated `Gate::authorize()` arm the accepted contract requires is unattributable without them (gate r3, **BLOCKER 3**). `AppServiceProvider`'s diff in this commit is **two hunks**: the scoped binding and the `GateEvaluated` listener. **Read its existing import block and `boot()` first** and add beside them; wave 1 also edits this file, so a conflict here is a reconciliation with the sibling plan.
      **`apps/api/bootstrap/app.php`'s diff in this commit is the render callback ONLY** — no middleware-group append, no priority-list entry. A diff that touches `$middleware->appendToGroup` or `appendToPriorityList` in this commit is rev 2's withdrawn design resurfacing.
      Subject: **`Phase 0.4.7: Record write-route authorization denials from the 403 render callback with an atomic two-key Redis dedup`**, both trailers. The body carries the atomicity argument — two round trips undercount by an unbounded amount under exactly the storm the rule describes — and the placement argument: a thrown authorization denial unwinds past every middleware and is rendered by the handler, so the handler is the only place that sees both the request and the 403.

## Task 2a-8: convert the eight role-name authorization sites — the gate that unlocks 2b

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Requests/CreateDraftCountingRequest.php:35`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php` (the counting-draft route gains `can:inventory.countings.create`)
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Controllers/InventoryCountingController.php:782,866,905,975,1297,1494`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Domain/Services/DiscountPermissionResolver.php:39`
- Modify: the **Inventory** manifest and enum (written by `permissions:scaffold`, positional form); the **POS** manifest, enum and the three `common.json` locale bundles (**hand-edited** — the generator cannot express `pos.override_discount_limit` and wave 1 ships no `--legacy`); `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json` (**regenerated** — `apps/api/phpstan.neon` itself is **not** edited)
- Modify: `dev:apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts:244,251`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Inventory/CountingOwnershipOverrideTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/POS/OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest.php`

**Why a role name is unreachable.** Authorizing on a role *name* is unreachable by any token scope, unreachable by the registry, and invisible to the §4.4 ratchet. Wave 1 shipped `ForbidRoleNameAuthorization` (PHPStan) and `no-role-name-authorization` (ESLint) with a **shrink-only baseline listing exactly these sites and no others**; it converted none. **This task converts the backend three files and empties their baseline entries.** Issuing a narrowing token while a role-name idiom can bypass the narrowing would ship the intersection guarantee as a **claim** rather than a **property** — which is why the conversion is an entry condition of 2b, not a follow-on.

**The eight sites, re-verified at `dev:630afa86f` — all eight reproduce at the spec's line numbers.**

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — declare the two new keys** (Q-w2-5), through wave 1's generator **where the generator can express the key**, and through a direct manifest edit where it cannot.

      > **The signature below is wave 1 rev 6.3's, quoted verbatim, and rev 2 of this plan invoked a different one** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B2**). `949dfabdf:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md` Task 1.15 Step 1 and the supplied `ScaffoldPermission::$signature` both declare:
      > ```
      > permissions:scaffold
      >     {module   : the owning module, e.g. Procurement}
      >     {resource : kebab-case resource, e.g. purchase-orders}
      >     {verb     : a PermissionVerb case value, e.g. approve}
      >     {--qualifier=  : snake_case narrowing}
      >     {--template=*  : template keys receiving it by default (admin is implicit and refused)}
      >     {--sod-group=}
      >     {--dry-run     : print the five diffs; write nothing}
      > ```
      > **The three arguments are POSITIONAL. There is no `--module`, no `--resource`, no `--verb`, and no `--legacy`.** Rev 2 used all four; the command would have exited with an argument error, so the conversion — and therefore 2b's entry condition — was unreachable. **Phase 0 re-reads the signature off the lane base** (`php artisan permissions:scaffold --help`) before this step runs, and a signature that has moved is a reconciliation with wave 1, not a local guess.

      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w2a/apps/api

      # 1. READ the closed verb enum first — it is wave 1's, and a verb it does not
      #    declare is a build error, not a new verb.
      php -r 'foreach (\App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionVerb::cases() as $c) { echo $c->name, " => ", $c->value, PHP_EOL; }' \
        -d auto_prepend_file=vendor/autoload.php

      # 2. DRY-RUN first: the generator prints the five diffs and writes nothing.
      #    Positional: <module> <resource> <verb>.
      #
      #    THE RESOURCE IS `inventory.countings`, NOT `countings`, AND THE DERIVED
      #    ACTION JOINS ITS QUALIFIER WITH AN UNDERSCORE (plan gate r5 fix round).
      #    Rev 5's comment here claimed a "module-derived prefix" and a hyphenated
      #    qualifier join; wave 1 rev 6.3 does neither. PermissionDefinition::make()
      #    is, verbatim (949dfabdf:...-wave-1.md:645-648):
      #        $action = $verb->value.($qualifier !== null ? '_'.$qualifier : '');
      #        key: $resource.'.'.$action
      #    -- no module segment is ever prepended, and RESOURCE_PATTERN
      #    (...-wave-1.md:580) explicitly admits ONE nested dot segment, which is how
      #    `inventory.transfers.create` and `inventory.adjustments.post` are spelled
      #    today (...-wave-1.md:4098-4106) and how `inventory.countings.*` must be.
      #    `Inventory countings override --qualifier=ownership` would have derived
      #    `countings.override_ownership` -- a 328th key in the wrong namespace.
      #
      #    EXPECTED DERIVED KEY: `inventory.countings.override_ownership` (UNDERSCORE).
      #    Spec 4.1.3a (b) spells it `override-ownership`; `make()` CANNOT produce a
      #    hyphen there, and `legacy()` is not an alternative -- Q-w2-7 (RULED
      #    2026-09-11) states why: the legacy baseline is shrink-only and keyed by
      #    key, so ANY new key absent from it is growth. The ENUM CASE NAME IS THE
      #    SAME EITHER WAY -- wave 1's
      #    transform splits on `.`, `-` AND `_` (...-wave-1.md:3589) -- so
      #    `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership` is correct
      #    under both spellings and nothing downstream of the enum moves.
      php artisan permissions:scaffold Inventory inventory.countings override --qualifier=ownership --dry-run

      # 3. Apply. NO --template: `admin` is IMPLICIT and the option REFUSES the literal
      #    string 'admin' (wave 1 Task 1.15 rule: "admin is implicit and refused").
      #    Passing no --template is what makes the key admin-only.
      php artisan permissions:scaffold Inventory inventory.countings override --qualifier=ownership

        echo $r->get("countings.override_ownership") ? "WRONG NAMESPACE — STOP, the resource argument was countings" : "namespace ok", PHP_EOL;'

      # 5. The POS key, scaffolded the same way. Q-w2-7 is RULED (2026-09-11,

      #    verb `override` (a DECLARED PermissionVerb case, ...-wave-1.md:487),
      #    qualifier `discount_limit`. It therefore DERIVES through make(), so there is
