      #    legacy baseline. Rev 5/6 spelled it `pos.discount_unlimited`, which reached
      #    NEITHER constructor; see Q-w2-7 for the ruling and its reasoning.
      php artisan permissions:scaffold POS pos override --qualifier=discount_limit --dry-run
      php artisan permissions:scaffold POS pos override --qualifier=discount_limit

      #    Confirm the derived key, and that the OLD spelling is nowhere in the registry.
      php artisan tinker --execute='$r = app(\App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry::class);
        echo $r->get("pos.override_discount_limit") ? "DERIVED OK" : "DERIVED SOMETHING ELSE — STOP", PHP_EOL;
        echo $r->get("pos.discount_unlimited") ? "OLD SPELLING PRESENT — STOP, Q-w2-7 re-spelled it" : "old spelling absent", PHP_EOL;'

      # 6. Confirm the registry sees all three keys and that neither new key reached a
      #    non-admin template.
      php artisan tinker --execute='$r = app(\App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry::class);
        foreach (["inventory.countings.create","inventory.countings.override_ownership","pos.override_discount_limit"] as $k) {
          echo $k, " => ", $r->get($k) ? "PRESENT" : "ABSENT", PHP_EOL;
        }
        print_r($r->templateGrants()["manager"] ?? []);'
      ```

      **`pos.override_discount_limit` IS scaffolded — rev 6.1 deletes the hand edit, and Q-w2-7's ruling is why.** Rev 5 and rev 6 spelled this key `pos.discount_unlimited`, whose action would have needed a verb `discount`; `PermissionVerb` is a **closed** 32-case enum and declares none (`…-wave-1.md:451-493`), so `make()` refused it, and `legacy()` refused it too because wave 1's legacy baseline is **shrink-only and keyed by permission key** — *"a key absent from the baseline is growth regardless of the ceiling"* (`…-wave-1.md:668-672`, test at `:1032`). **The orchestrator ruled alternative (B) on 2026-09-11**: re-spell as resource `pos`, verb **`Override`** (declared at `…-wave-1.md:487`), qualifier `discount_limit` → **`pos.override_discount_limit`**, the exact mirror of the catalogue's existing `pos.approve_discount_limit_override` (`…-wave-1.md:4467`). The key therefore derives through `make()` like every other key in this wave, and step 5 of the block above is the whole declaration: **the generator writes all five artefacts** — manifest entry, enum case `POSPermission::PosOverrideDiscountLimit`, and the `en`/`fr`/`ar` stubs in `apps/web/src/locales/{en,fr,ar}/common.json` (`…-wave-1.md:8521`, `ScaffoldPermission::handle()`'s locale loop: `base_path('../web/src/locales/%s/common.json')`, **not** a `permissions.json`).

      **Consequences of the ruling for this step, stated so a reviewer can check them off:**
      - **No hand edit.** Rev 6's `PermissionDefinition::legacy(key: 'pos.discount_unlimited', …)` manifest block, its hand-appended enum case and its hand-written locale stubs are **deleted from this plan**. Nothing in Task 2a-8 edits a manifest or an enum by hand.
      - **No `legacy()` call anywhere in wave 2**, so `permission-legacy-action-baseline.json` and `LEGACY_ACTION_CEILING = 59` are untouched and the rev-6 executable STOP that guarded them is **removed** — there is nothing left for it to catch. (The invariant it protected still holds and is still tested: it is wave 1's test, running unchanged on this wave's commits.)
      - **No wave-1 edit and no new `PermissionVerb` case.** The ruling is a spelling change on this plan's side only, which is what makes it compatible with the Global Constraint that wave 1 is final at rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`.
      - **`admin`-only by template is unchanged** — no `--template` option and no `templateDefaults` entry, which is what preserves today's `hasRole(self::ADMIN_ROLES)` behaviour byte-for-byte.
      - **The behaviour test is `OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest`** (`apps/api/tests/Feature/POS/OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest.php`), renamed from `DiscountUnlimitedPermissionTest` because it is named for the key it exercises.
      - **Spec §4.1.3a (b) still carries the old spelling** and is owed the same one-line re-spelling. That file is outside this plan's edit scope; it is handed back as a spec-maintenance item, and Phase 0 re-reads the spec at the lane base so a reviewer sees the divergence rather than inheriting it silently.

      **Both keys are `admin`-only by template**, which preserves today's behaviour exactly — `admin` holds them, nobody else does — while making them narrowable and auditable. **`admin` is implicit** in wave 1's registry (a manifest that lists `'admin'` in `templateDefaults` is a build error), so the correct declaration is **no `--template` option and no `templateDefaults` entry**.
      **Verify `inventory.countings.create` exists** (Phase 0 recorded whether it does). **If it is ABSENT, scaffold it with the same positional form** — `php artisan permissions:scaffold Inventory countings create --template=manager --template=general_manager` — with `manager` and `general_manager` explicitly named, because Step 2's behaviour note requires them and `--template=*` is the only way a non-admin template receives a key.
- [ ] **Step 2 — `CreateDraftCountingRequest`.** Delete the `hasRole(['manager','admin'])` at `:35`; `authorize()` returns `true`. Add the gate to the route as **`->middleware('can:'.InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsCreate->value)`** — the `Concat` form the Global Constraints require, never the joined literal `'can:inventory.countings.create'`, which `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` reads through its `can:` prefix and rejects. **E-4: a FormRequest is never the sole gate**, so the gate moves to the route rather than changing shape in place, and `apps/api/app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/routes.php` imports `InventoryPermission` in this commit.
      > **Behaviour change, stated:** today the sole gate is `manager` **or** `admin`. `inventory.countings.create`'s template defaults must therefore include **`manager`** (and `general_manager`, which derives from `manager`), or a real manager loses the ability to start a counting draft on deploy day. **If the key already exists with different defaults, that is a finding to raise, not to silently widen** — record it and ask the orchestrator.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the six `InventoryCountingController` bypasses.** Each is `$counting->created_by_user_id !== $userId && ! $user->hasRole('admin')` — a creator-or-admin bypass. Replace `hasRole('admin')` with `can(InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership->value)`. **The creator clause is untouched**; only the override half moves.
- [ ] **Step 4 — `DiscountPermissionResolver::isAdmin()`.** `hasRole(self::ADMIN_ROLES)` → `can(POSPermission::PosOverrideDiscountLimit->value)`, keeping `ADMIN_MAX_PERCENT` semantics unchanged. The `ADMIN_ROLES` constant is deleted, and `use App\Modules\POS\Domain\Enums\POSPermission;` is added — **the enum case Step 1's `permissions:scaffold POS pos override --qualifier=discount_limit` run writes is what makes this line compile**, which is why the two live in one commit.
- [ ] **Step 5 — the FE half.** Delete the two `roles.includes` helpers at `dev:apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts:244,251`. Task 2a-9 owns the rest of that file; this step removes only the two role-name predicates so the ESLint baseline shrinks in the commit that shrinks the PHPStan one.
      **`apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:354` stays** — spec §4.10 item 4: removing the ladder means the offline path must fall back to the last synced permission payload instead of a role name, which is a POS release with an offline-behaviour test. **Wave 3.** It stays in the baseline with the ceiling recording it.
      **The two central-guard sites stay waived**, not baselined: `AdminLayout.tsx:60` and `adminRolePolicy.ts:52` test `SuperAdmin.role` against a policy table, not a Spatie role. The waiver is an explicit allow-list in the rule, not an absent baseline entry.
- [ ] **Step 6 — write the two behaviour tests.** `CountingOwnershipOverrideTest`: for each of the six operations, an `admin` overrides another user's counting (allow) and a `manager` **cannot** (deny) — six allow paths and six deny paths, asserting the **rows** the operation would have written are unchanged on deny. `OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest`: an `admin` gets `ADMIN_MAX_PERCENT`, a `manager` gets their own ceiling, and the numbers are byte-identical to `dev`'s.
- [ ] **Step 7 — write `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest`.**

      **The baseline is a LIST of `<file>:<line>` sites, not a map keyed by file** — wave 1 rev 6's `apps/api/tools/generate-permission-baselines.php` writes *"a sorted list of `<file>:<line>:<key>` (or `<file>:<line>`) sites"* to `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json`. Rev 1 of this plan invented both the path and a map shape, and its `assertArrayNotHasKey()` would have **passed vacuously** against the real artefact — a green gate asserting nothing (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-7**, citation audit item 4). Re-pinned:

~~~php
/** @var list<string> The three files whose conversion unlocks 2b. */
private const CONVERTED = [
    'app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Requests/CreateDraftCountingRequest.php',
    'app/Modules/Inventory/Presentation/Controllers/InventoryCountingController.php',
    'app/Modules/POS/Domain/Services/DiscountPermissionResolver.php',
];

#[Test]
public function the_role_name_baseline_carries_no_site_in_the_three_converted_files(): void
{
    $path = base_path('tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json');

    $this->assertFileExists($path, 'wave 1 Task 1.16 ships this baseline; a missing file is a '
        .'reconciliation failure with wave 1, not a green gate.');

    /** @var list<string> $sites  each "<relative path>:<line>" */
    $sites = json_decode((string) file_get_contents($path), true, 512, JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);

    $this->assertIsList($sites, 'the generator writes a sorted LIST of site strings; a map here '
        .'means the artefact changed shape and every assertion below is vacuous.');

    foreach (self::CONVERTED as $file) {
        $remaining = array_values(array_filter(
            $sites,
            static fn (string $site): bool => str_starts_with($site, $file.':'),
        ));

        $this->assertSame([], $remaining, sprintf(
            'A scoped token cannot be issued while %s can authorize on a role name: the token '
            .'narrowing runs through User::hasPermissionTo() and a role-name idiom bypasses it '
            .'entirely, which would ship the intersection guarantee as a claim rather than a '
            .'property. Convert the site, or do not route service-accounts.tokens.create. '
            .'Remaining: %s',
            $file,
            implode(', ', $remaining),
        ));
    }
}
~~~

      A second case asserts the **live source** agrees with the baseline file, so a baseline edited without a code change cannot make the gate green:

~~~php
#[Test]
public function the_live_source_carries_no_role_name_idiom_in_the_three_converted_files(): void
{
    foreach (self::CONVERTED as $file) {
        $source = (string) file_get_contents(base_path($file));

        foreach (['hasRole(', 'hasAnyRole(', 'hasAllRoles('] as $idiom) {
            $this->assertStringNotContainsString($idiom, $source, sprintf(
                '%s still calls %s. The baseline may say otherwise; the source is the oracle.',
                $file,
                $idiom,
            ));
        }
    }
}
~~~

**Why this can only happen now.** `UI_ALIAS_PERMISSIONS` maps UI-grouping keys to **role names** — `'services.view': ['admin','sales','manager']` (`dev:apps/web/src/hooks/uiAliasPermissions.ts:8-10`) — and `usePermissions` widens its `Permission` type with `UiAliasPermission`. **Wave 0b made `services.{view,create,update}` real backend permissions**; wave 1 generated `PERMISSION_MODULES` and the `Permission` union. Only now can the alias map be deleted without a nav entry vanishing.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write the vitest first (red).** `EffectivePermissionsPanel.test.tsx` renders from a fixture payload and asserts: grouping by `PERMISSION_MODULES` module, the `granted_by` chips, the "differs from role template" badge, the filter, and that a `token_scope: null` payload renders no token section. `usePermissions.test.ts` (existing, extended) asserts that a permission **absent** from the server payload is denied — no role-name fallback path remains.

Labels come from the i18n bundles wave 1's `permissions:export-label-skeleton` filled; **no user-facing string is hardcoded** (CLAUDE.md rule 11).

## Lane 2b — principals and tokens

Worktree `.worktrees/rbac-w2b`, branch `lane/rbac-w2b`, created from `dev` **after 2a merges**. Entry condition 4 (`ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` green on `dev`) is re-run immediately before the worktree is created. **Eight commits** — Task 2b-1 executed in lane 2a as **Task 2a-0** (**Q-w2-4**), so `Phase 0.5.1` is unused.

### Execution order and `Phase` ordinals — rev 3 re-sequences the lane

> **Rev 2's numbering was not buildable** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B3**). Its `Phase 0.5.4` (Task 2b-4) called `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()`, which its `Phase 0.5.6` (Task 2b-6) introduced, and routed membership removal through `MembershipRevocationService`, which its `Phase 0.5.5` (Task 2b-5) introduced. `Phase 0.5.4` could therefore neither compile nor be green in isolation, which makes the commit a **unit of intention rather than a revertable unit** — the exact property the Global Constraints' path-scoped-commit rule exists to produce.
>
> **Rev 3 keeps every task's ID and body and re-assigns its `Phase` ordinal to its position in a buildable order**, and splits the old Task 2b-5 at its own natural seam — **revocation** (which nothing in the lane has yet) from **issuance** (which needs a controller to route on). Task IDs are stable so every cross-reference in this document, in the gate registers and in the reviewer prompts still resolves; the ordinal is what moves.

| Order | `Phase` | Task | Why it must be here | Introduces | Consumes from earlier in the lane |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **`0.5.2`** | **2b-2** — the `principal_kind` census | every later writer and selector reads the column 2a shipped | the writer/selector predicates, the `AllowSelfService` service arm | — (2a's column) |
| 2 | **`0.5.3`** | **2b-3** — token scope + `EnforceTokenScope` | must land **after** the census: attaching a `principal_kind`-reading middleware to the global `api` group before every writer knows about the column is a fleet-wide 401 | `tokenScopePermissionNames()`, `EnforceTokenScope`, `TokenScopeFailure` | the census's predicates |
| 3 | **`0.5.4`** | **2b-6** — plan seats + `max_service_accounts` | **moved ahead of the service-account surface**: it introduces `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()`, which that surface calls inside its creation transaction. Compiles and is green alone — the six predicates and the tier constant need nothing from this lane | **`canAddServiceAccount()`**, `PlanLimits::MAX_SERVICE_ACCOUNTS` | — |
| 4 | **`0.5.5`** | **2b-5a** — `MembershipRevocationService`, `RevokePrincipalTokens`, `ApiTokenRevoked`, `TokenTtl`, the human TTL | **moved ahead of the service-account surface**: that surface's `PUT /memberships` routes every removal through this service. Its two production call sites — `UserController::destroy` and `::deactivate` — already exist at `dev`, so it compiles and is green alone | **`MembershipRevocationService`**, `RevokePrincipalTokens`, `ApiTokenRevoked`, `TokenTtl` | `LastAdminFloor::assertMembershipRemovalSurvives` (2a) |
| 5 | **`0.5.6`** | **2b-4** — service accounts: permissions, routes, provisioning, memberships, the two role actions | consumes **both** of the two above, and now both exist | `ServiceAccountController`, `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`, the six keys, the eight routes | `canAddServiceAccount()` (`0.5.4`), `MembershipRevocationService` (`0.5.5`), `EnforceTokenScope` (`0.5.3`) |
| 6 | **`0.5.7`** | **2b-5b** — token issuance and `/auth/me`'s `token_scope` | `POST /service-accounts/{id}/tokens` is an action **on the controller `0.5.6` creates**; it cannot precede it | the issuance action, `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest`, `AuthUserData.token_scope` | `ServiceAccountController` (`0.5.6`), `TokenTtl` (`0.5.5`) |
| 7 | **`0.5.8`** | **2b-7** — POS PIN surfaces | independent of the service-account surface; placed after it so the PIN exclusion can be asserted against a **production-created** service principal | the `pinHolders()` predicate, the two PIN-write refusals | the census (`0.5.2`), `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` (`0.5.6`) for the test fixture |
| 8 | **`0.5.9`** | **2b-8** — the service-account frontend and the 2b browser probe | consumes every backend surface above | the FE screens, the Playwright probe | all |

**`Phase 0.5.1` remains deliberately unused** (Task 2b-1 shipped in 2a as `Phase 0.4.0`). **`Phase 0.5.9` is new in rev 3** — the old Task 2b-5's split adds one commit. Waves 3 and 4 keep `0.6` and `0.7`; nothing downstream re-anchors.

**The buildability rule this table encodes, stated once so a reviewer can check it mechanically:** *for every `Phase 0.5.n`, `git checkout <that commit>` followed by `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app` and the commit's own test files must be green, with no symbol resolved from a later commit.* The verification checklist asserts it by walking the lane's commits.

**Ordering inside the lane is load-bearing, and the table above is the statement of it.** The two constraints that fix it are: `EnforceTokenScope` attaches only **after** the census edits; and **no commit may name a symbol a later commit introduces**. The column itself is already in the tree, from 2a.

## Task 2b-1 — **EXECUTED IN LANE 2a AS TASK 2a-0** (`Phase 0.4.0`)

> **Moved by Q-w2-4** (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-2**). The body below is the task, unchanged in substance; execute it **first in lane 2a**, on the `autoerp_test_w2a` PG lane, with the `Phase 0.4.0` subject and the add list given at Task 2a-0. It is left under this heading rather than relocated so that every cross-reference in this document, in the gate register and in the reviewer prompts still resolves. **Nothing in lane 2b re-does it**; 2b consumes the column.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Enums/PrincipalKind.php`
- Create: `apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_09_20_100000_add_principal_kind_to_users.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:81-97` (`$fillable`), `:115-126` (`casts()`), plus the three predicates
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PrincipalKindMigrationTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/UserFillableContractTest.php`

**Interfaces**

~~~php
enum PrincipalKind: string
{
    case Human = 'human';
    case Service = 'service';

    public function canLogIn(): bool { return $this === self::Human; }
    public function canHoldPosPin(): bool { return $this === self::Human; }
    public function canBeImpersonated(): bool { return $this === self::Human; }
}

// on User:
public function principalKind(): PrincipalKind;
public function isServicePrincipal(): bool;   // principal_kind === PrincipalKind::Service
public function isHumanPrincipal(): bool;     // principal_kind === PrincipalKind::Human
~~~

**The schema, §5.1.**

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Index | FK |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `principal_kind` | `varchar(16)` | NO | `'human'` | `index(principal_kind)` | — |
| `principal_description` | `varchar(255)` | YES | `NULL` | — | — |
| `created_by_user_id` | `uuid` | YES | `NULL` | `index` | `users.id`, `ON DELETE SET NULL` |
| `password` *(altered)* | `varchar` | **YES** (was NO) | — | — | — |

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the migration's `up()`.** Three columns; `password` made nullable; the CHECK on PG and the equivalent **insert/update trigger pair** on SQLite, following the pattern `lane/w-lot-a-1a` establishes for `provisioning_source`:

**TWO constraints, not one, and rev 2 shipped only the second** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B1**). A shape-only check is satisfied by `principal_kind = 'robot'` with the service shape, so the column's domain has to be closed **separately** — spec §5.1 names both (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md:2012-2029`). The enum cast on `User` closes the domain for Eloquent writes only; a seeder, a migration, a raw `DB::table('users')->insert()` or a `psql` session bypasses it entirely, which is the whole reason the invariant is in the database.

~~~sql
-- users_principal_kind_check — the CLOSED DOMAIN. Named separately so a
-- future third kind is one ALTER, and so a violation's error message names
-- the column rather than a compound shape predicate.
CHECK (principal_kind IN ('human','service'))

-- users_service_principal_check — the SHAPE, unchanged from rev 2.
CHECK (
  principal_kind = 'human'
  OR (pos_pin IS NULL
      AND email_verified_at IS NULL
      AND password IS NULL
      AND email IS NULL
      AND status IN ('active','inactive'))
)
~~~

      > **Why two named constraints rather than one conjunct.** `CHECK (principal_kind IN (…) AND (… shape …))` is logically identical and operationally worse: PostgreSQL reports the **constraint name** on violation, so one constraint means every failure — an invalid kind, a service row with a PIN, a service row with an email — arrives as the same string, and `PrincipalKindMigrationTest`'s five cases could not tell them apart. Two names give two messages, and the `down()` drops both (Step 2).
      > **The SQLite arm gets the same pair.** The trigger bodies are `SELECT RAISE(ABORT, '<constraint name>') WHERE NEW.principal_kind NOT IN ('human','service');` and the shape predicate, **both inside each of the two triggers** (`users_service_principal_insert`, `users_service_principal_update`) — one `BEFORE INSERT` and one `BEFORE UPDATE`, following the pattern `lane/w-lot-a-1a` establishes for `provisioning_source`. Two triggers, two clauses each; `up()` creates exactly two trigger objects and `down()` drops exactly those two by name.

      > **Every clause is load-bearing.** `password IS NULL` **not a sentinel**: `User` casts `password` as `hashed` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:115-126`), so storing `'!'` produces a **valid hash of `!`** — a sentinel a known one-character password unlocks. `email IS NULL` **not a synthetic `.invalid` address**: `email` is already nullable with a **partial unique over non-null values** (`dev:apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_03_23_000001_make_user_email_nullable.php:14-23`), so a null email needs no schema change, cannot collide, and is skipped **at the source** by the invitation path and refused with 422 `NO_EMAIL` by password reset — whereas a reserved-TLD address still flows through `Notifiable` and any future non-mail channel. `status IN ('active','inactive')` because `UserStatus` also carries `suspended` and `pending_verification` (`dev:.../Enums/UserStatus.php:10-15`), neither of which means anything for a credential-only principal.
      > **No backfill statement is written.** The `'human'` default makes this a no-op for every existing row: every existing `users` row is a human by construction, because there is no other way to create one today.
      > **Timestamp after `2026_09_06_205000_add_provisioning_source_to_roles.php`** and after wave 1's `2026_09_18_100000_add_template_columns_to_roles.php`. **Writes no `tenant_id`**, so the W-LOT CHECK's `tenant_id IS NOT NULL` clause is untouched.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the `down()`, FIVE ordered operations, and step 2 destroys data.** Rev 1 said *"drop the CHECK"* and left the SQLite arm — a **trigger pair**, not a CHECK — undropped, and did not say which prior column states are restored (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-2**). A `down()` that leaves an insert/update trigger behind on the fast lane leaves a table whose schema says nothing about `principal_kind` while a trigger still references it: every later insert errors with `no such column`.
      1. **Refuse** while any `personal_access_tokens` row belongs to a `principal_kind = 'service'` principal — the operator revokes them first. Exit with an exception naming the count and the revoke command; write nothing.
      2. **Delete every `principal_kind = 'service'` `users` row** and its `user_company_memberships` and `model_has_roles` rows. **This is data loss and the migration says so in its own exception message and log line**: a rollback destroys every service account the tenant created, because there is no representation for one in the pre-wave schema — a machine identity with no password and no email is not a user the old `users` table can hold. The `down()` **logs each deleted principal id and writes an `AuditEvent` per deletion before removing it**, so the rollback is reconstructible from the audit chain even though the rows are gone.
      3. **Drop the database-enforced invariant, on the driver that has it.** These are two different objects and both are named:
         ```php
         if (DB::getDriverName() === 'pgsql') {
             // TWO constraints, because up() creates two (Step 1): the closed
             // domain and the service shape. Dropping one leaves the other, and
             // step 5's `password NOT NULL` restore then fails on a table that
             // still forbids a human row from carrying a password — a down()
             // that aborts halfway is the failure this ordering exists to avoid.
             DB::statement('ALTER TABLE users DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS users_principal_kind_check');
             DB::statement('ALTER TABLE users DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS users_service_principal_check');
         }

         if (DB::getDriverName() === 'sqlite') {
             DB::statement('DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS users_service_principal_insert');
             DB::statement('DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS users_service_principal_update');
         }
         ```
         **Both PG constraints and both SQLite triggers, by name.** `up()` creates **two** constraints on PG (`users_principal_kind_check`, `users_service_principal_check`) and **two** triggers on SQLite — one `BEFORE INSERT`, one `BEFORE UPDATE`, each carrying **both** clauses — following the pattern `lane/w-lot-a-1a` establishes for `provisioning_source`. A `down()` that drops one of either pair leaves the other.
      4. **Drop the three columns and their indexes, in reverse creation order**, each restored to the exact prior state: drop the FK `users_created_by_user_id_foreign` and its index, then `created_by_user_id`; then `principal_description`; then the `index(principal_kind)` and `principal_kind`. `up()` added **no** other index, unique key or default to any pre-existing column, so nothing else is owed here — and the migration says so in a comment rather than leaving a reader to check.
      5. **Restore `password NOT NULL`**, its only prior state — safe **only because** step 2 removed every row that could hold a null. Without step 2 this statement fails and the whole `down()` aborts, which is the loud failure the ordering exists to avoid. `password` had no default before and gets none back.
      **`PrincipalKindMigrationTest` asserts the `down()`**, not only the `up()`: after `down()`, `Schema::hasColumn('users','principal_kind')` is false **and** — on the fast lane — an ordinary human insert succeeds, which is what proves both triggers are gone.
- [ ] **Step 3 — `$fillable` and `casts()`.** `User::$fillable` (`dev:…/User.php:81-97`) gains **exactly** `principal_kind`, `principal_description`, `created_by_user_id`; `principal_kind` joins `casts()` beside the existing entries (`:115-126`). **Without the `$fillable` change**, `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`'s `User::create()` would silently drop all three and every service account would persist as a **human** — a failure with no error at all. `UserFillableContractTest` asserts the three names are present.
- [ ] **Step 4 — `PrincipalKindMigrationTest`**, and its **first** case is the invalid kind (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B1**: rev 2's test never attempted one, so the missing domain constraint could not have been caught by it).
      **Every case is a RAW insert, never `User::create()`** — the enum cast would reject an invalid kind before the statement reached the database, and what is under test is the **database's** invariant, not Eloquent's.
      | # | Case | Driver | Assertion |
      |---:|---|---|---|
      | 1 | **`principal_kind = 'robot'` with an otherwise valid SERVICE shape** | PG **and** SQLite | rejected — PG names `users_principal_kind_check`, SQLite raises that same string from the insert trigger. **This is the case rev 2 could not fail on**: the shape predicate alone admits the row |
      | 2 | **`principal_kind = 'robot'` with an otherwise valid HUMAN shape** | PG **and** SQLite | rejected, same constraint. Proves the domain check is not reachable only through the service branch |
      | 3 | **`UPDATE users SET principal_kind = 'robot'`** on a valid human row | PG **and** SQLite | rejected — on SQLite this is what proves the **update** trigger carries the domain clause too, not just the insert one |
      | 4 | a service row with a `suspended` status, a PIN, a password **or** an email (four sub-cases) | PG **and** SQLite | rejected by `users_service_principal_check` / the shape clause |
      | 5 | a valid service row (`principal_kind='service'`, all four NULL, `status='active'`) | PG **and** SQLite | inserts |
      | 6 | every pre-existing row reads back `principal_kind = 'human'` | PG | no backfill statement ran; the column default did the work |
      | 7 | after `down()`: `Schema::hasColumn('users','principal_kind')` is false **and** an ordinary human insert succeeds | SQLite | proves **both** triggers are gone — a surviving trigger referencing a dropped column errors with `no such column` |
      **Cases 1–5 run on BOTH drivers**, in one test method parameterised by driver, because the two arms are two different implementations of one invariant and a test that exercises only PG would let the SQLite trigger pair drift silently.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run** (PG lane `autoerp_test_w2a` **and** the fast lane, because the trigger pair only exists on the latter) and **commit** with the subject and add list given at **Task 2a-0** (`Phase 0.4.0`). The body carries the sentinel and synthetic-email reasoning — both are reversals of an earlier draft and a reviewer who does not read them will propose one of them again — and the five-step `down()`'s ordering argument.

## Task 2b-3: token scope — generalise the narrowing, ship `EnforceTokenScope`

> **Executed SECOND in lane 2b, as `Phase 0.5.3`** (the lane's execution-order table above). Unchanged by rev 3.

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:185-198`, `:201-212`, `:216-241`
- Create: `EnforceTokenScope.php`, `TokenScopeFailure.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:143-153` (**attach**), `:182-185` (priority)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/EnforceTokenScopeCoverageTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/EnforceTokenScopeNoOpTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/TokenScopeNarrowingTest.php`

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — generalise the extraction.** `impersonationPermissionNames()` (`dev:…/User.php:216-241`) becomes:

~~~php
/**
 * The token's permission narrowing, or null when the token does not narrow.
 *
 * Returns null  -> token is unscoped; the principal's full grants apply.
 * Returns list  -> effective = principal grants ∩ this list (NEVER wider).
 * Returns []    -> a token that narrows to nothing; every check denies.
 *
 * @return list<string>|null
 */
private function tokenScopePermissionNames(): ?array
~~~

      The change from today is one branch: the `$isImpersonating` early return at `dev:225-234` is deleted, so **the intersection applies whenever the token carries at least one `permission:` ability, regardless of impersonation**. Four rules, each asserted:
      1. **Narrowing only, never widening.** The list is intersected with the **database** grants — a `permission:` ability naming a key the principal does not hold grants nothing. Already how the impersonation path behaves (`dev:185-198`: the scope check runs *and then* `hasPermissionToWithoutImpersonationFilter` still runs). **Sanctum's `tokenCan()` is deliberately not the enforcement point**: `HasApiTokens` adds `tokenCan()`/`tokenCant()` and never overrides `can()`, and Laravel documents the consequence by telling you to check both dimensions yourself. Wiring the narrowing into `User::hasPermissionTo()` makes it apply to **every permission-based idiom at once** — 608 route middleware strings, 202 `->can()` calls and 107 `Gate::authorize()` calls — because AutoERP enables Spatie's Gate callback (`register_permission_check_method => true`, `dev:apps/api/config/permission.php:103-107`) and that callback routes `Gate::before` through `checkPermissionTo()`, which dispatches to this override. Adding `tokenCan()` to those call sites instead would be 900+ edits and a permanently incomplete guard.
      2. **Empty list denies.** Junk that yields an empty list denies everything rather than falling open — today's behaviour, preserved.
      3. **`*` is not honoured.** Sanctum's wildcard is meaningful to `tokenCan()`, not to this intersection; a token that wants full grants carries **no** `permission:` ability. A token carrying **both** `*` and a `permission:` ability is rejected by the middleware with `TOKEN_SCOPE_AMBIGUOUS` (401) rather than silently picking one.
      4. **Unknown keys are dropped, and the drop is visible.** A `permission:` ability naming a key absent from `PermissionRegistry` is ignored for the intersection and surfaced in `token_scope.unknown` on `/auth/me`, so an operator can see the token is stale.
      5. **Impersonation stays exactly as it is.** An impersonation token carries both `impersonation:` and `permission:` abilities; under the generalised rule the same intersection happens, and `getUnfilteredPermissionsForSupportAccess()` (`dev:176-179`) keeps its meaning.
      **`getAllPermissions()`'s contract is stated, not left as an accident**: it narrows **only when the model is the authenticated subject carrying `currentAccessToken()`**, which is what `dev:201-241` already does, and `EffectivePermissionResolverTest` asserts it.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the middleware.** `App\Modules\Identity\Presentation\Middleware\EnforceTokenScope`. It does **not** authorize — `can:` still does that. **First statement in `handle()`:**

~~~php
if (! $request->user() instanceof \App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User) {
    return $next($request);
}
~~~

      > **The no-op contract is stated over the SUBJECT TYPE, not only over the token.** The global `api` group also wraps the central admin pipeline — `POST/GET /api/v1/admin/auth/*` and the whole `admin` prefix (`dev:apps/api/routes/api.php:37-48,55-97`), all on `auth:sanctum-admin` — whose authenticated subject is a central `App\Models\SuperAdmin` with **no `principal_kind`, no `tenant_id` and no memberships** (`dev:apps/api/app/Models/SuperAdmin.php:14-32`). Every one of the four checks reads a field that model does not have, so an unguarded global attachment would either fatal or silently read `null` as "not a service principal". The guard is placed **before** the ambiguity check, so even `TOKEN_SCOPE_AMBIGUOUS` cannot fire on a central token. It covers `SuperAdmin` today and any future guard **by construction**, not by an allow-list of guards.

      Then four cheap, fail-closed checks and nothing else:
      | Check | Failure |
      |---|---|
      | token carries both `*` and a `permission:` ability | 401 `TOKEN_SCOPE_AMBIGUOUS` |
      | principal is `Service` and the token carries no `tenant:` claim | 401 `SERVICE_TOKEN_MISSING_TENANT_CLAIM` — **no grandfathering** (service tokens are all new; the human grandfathering at `dev:…/EnforceTokenTenantClaim.php:90-92` is untouched). **Defence in depth, not the primary guard**: `ResolveTenancy` runs *before* authentication and selects the tenant database from that same ability (`dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:166-185`; `ResolveTenancy.php:114-134`), so under db-per-tenant a bearer token without the claim usually cannot load its tenant `User` at all. The real guarantee is made **at issuance** (Task 2b-5) |
      | principal is `Service` and `status` is not `active` | 401 `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE` |
      | principal is `Service` and holds no active membership in the resolved company | 403 `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_NO_MEMBERSHIP` |
- [ ] **Step 3 — ATTACH, then order. Two changes, not one.**
      1. **Attach.** Append `EnforceTokenScope::class` to the global **`api`** group beside the existing entries at `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:143-153`. **Priority sorts middleware a request already carries; it attaches nothing.** The current global `api` group carries neither `SetPermissionsTeam` nor either token middleware — protected routes attach them per group (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:40,51,57`; `dev:apps/api/routes/api.php:50-54`) — so a priority-only edit would mean every check this task promises **never runs**.
      2. **Order.** `appendToPriorityList(after: EnforceTokenTenantClaim::class, append: EnforceTokenScope::class)` at `dev:182-185`, and the existing `ImpersonationContext` append **re-anchors to `EnforceTokenScope`**. Group membership alone does not guarantee it runs **after** `Authenticate`, and it must — every check needs a resolved `User`. Placing it after `EnforceTokenTenantClaim` means the tenant is bound and the permissions team is set (`dev:178-181`), so the membership read is on the right database; placing it before `ImpersonationContext` means an impersonation session is evaluated with the scope already validated.
      **Because it is on the group rather than a hand-picked route set, status and membership enforcement reach every authenticated surface — including the ungated and self-service ones the ratchet deliberately leaves without a `can:`.** In particular **`GET /api/v1/auth/me` is covered**: it is an `authz.self` route with no action gate, so before this an inactive service principal, or one whose last membership had been revoked, could still read its own effective-permission payload.
      **List it explicitly in the `/service-accounts/*` group too** (Task 2b-4) even though the global attachment covers it — listing it makes that surface's dependency visible in the file a reviewer reads.
      > **The duplicate is a duplicate DECLARATION, never a duplicate EXECUTION, and rev 5 proves it rather than asserting it** (gate r4, Wave 2 **MAJOR 4**). Rev 4 said *"a duplicate entry in a stack is idempotent"* and tested nothing. It is true, and it is true for a reason that is one function deep: `Router::resolveMiddleware()` expands the `api` group name into its class list and then sorts (`vendor/laravel/framework/src/Illuminate/Routing/Router.php:844-881`, laravel/framework **v12.58.0**), and `SortedMiddleware::sortMiddleware()` returns `Router::uniqueMiddleware($middlewares)` (`…/Routing/SortedMiddleware.php:63`), which collapses equal string values keeping the first (`…/Routing/Router.php:1472-1487`). `Route::gatherMiddleware()` cannot collapse the pair on its own (`…/Routing/Route.php` — it dedups `'api'` the *string* against `EnforceTokenScope::class`, which are different values), so the collapse happens after expansion, which is exactly where it must.
      > **Step 4's coverage test asserts it**, so the claim is executable: for `POST /api/v1/service-accounts` — the route that carries **both** the group and the explicit entry — `Route::gatherRouteMiddleware()` resolves `EnforceTokenScope::class` **exactly once**. If a future Laravel stops deduplicating, that assertion is what says so, rather than a service account silently paying two membership queries per request.
- [ ] **Step 4 — `EnforceTokenScopeCoverageTest`, an architecture test with NO allow-list.** Boot the live router (the mechanism `dev:apps/api/tests/Architecture/AuthLifecycleTest.php` already uses) and assert **every** route whose resolved middleware stack contains `auth:sanctum` also resolves `EnforceTokenScope`. A route group that opts out of the `api` group and forgets the middleware fails it. **Plus an explicit assertion that `auth:sanctum-admin` routes are NOT in its required set** — the two guards are different pipelines and the coverage test must not silently start demanding tenant-scope enforcement on the central one. **Plus the single-execution assertion Step 3 owes** (gate r4, **MAJOR 4**):
      ~~~php
      public function test_the_service_account_group_resolves_the_scope_middleware_exactly_once(): void
      {
          $route = collect(Route::getRoutes()->getRoutes())
              ->first(static fn (RoutingRoute $r): bool => $r->uri() === 'api/v1/service-accounts'
                  && in_array('POST', $r->methods(), true));

          self::assertInstanceOf(RoutingRoute::class, $route);
          self::assertSame(
              1,
              count(array_keys(
                  app(Router::class)->gatherRouteMiddleware($route),
                  EnforceTokenScope::class,
                  true,
              )),
              'the global api group and the /service-accounts group BOTH declare EnforceTokenScope; '
              .'the router is expected to collapse them to one execution (SortedMiddleware::sortMiddleware '
              .'-> Router::uniqueMiddleware). If this fails, remove the explicit group entry.',
          );
      }

## Task 2b-4: service accounts — permissions, routes, provisioning, memberships

> **Executed FIFTH in lane 2b, as `Phase 0.5.6`** — moved from `Phase 0.5.4` by rev 3 (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B3**). It calls `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()` (Task 2b-6, now `Phase 0.5.4`) and routes every membership removal through `MembershipRevocationService` (Task 2b-5a, now `Phase 0.5.5`); at `Phase 0.5.4` neither symbol existed, so the commit could not compile. **Both now land before it.**

**Files**

- Modify: the Identity manifest (via `permissions:scaffold`)
- Create: `ServiceAccountController.php`, `ServiceAccountProvisioningService.php`, `CreateServiceAccountRequest.php`, `UpdateServiceAccountMembershipsRequest.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php` (the new group), `UserController.php` + `RoleController.php` (Q-w2-4's helper gets its predicate)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountProvisioningTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/ServiceAccountWriterCensusTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountLocationScopeTest.php`

**Six permissions, all declared by the Identity manifest through `permissions:scaffold`, template defaults `admin` ONLY.** `general_manager` and `manager` receive none — issuing a machine credential is an administrative act, and D2 says a new permission never lands in a non-admin template without a deliberate declaration. **Every one decomposes through `make()`** — the four CRUD keys on resource `service-accounts` (kebab, passes `RESOURCE_PATTERN`) and the two token keys on the **nested** resource `service-accounts.tokens`, each with a declared `PermissionVerb` case and **no qualifier** — so **none** of them touches the `legacy()` ceiling Q-w2-7 is about, and none of them STOPs on derivation.

| Key | Enum case (`IdentityPermission`) | Scaffold invocation — **Step 0** | Gates |
|---|---|---|---|
| `service-accounts.view` | `ServiceAccountsView` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts view` | `GET /service-accounts`, `GET /service-accounts/{id}` |
| `service-accounts.create` | `ServiceAccountsCreate` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts create` | `POST /service-accounts` |
| `service-accounts.update` | `ServiceAccountsUpdate` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts update` | `PATCH /service-accounts/{id}`, `PUT /service-accounts/{id}/memberships`, `POST|DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/roles` |
| `service-accounts.delete` | `ServiceAccountsDelete` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts delete` | `DELETE /service-accounts/{id}` (soft: status → inactive, tokens revoked) |
| `service-accounts.tokens.create` | `ServiceAccountsTokensCreate` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens create` | `POST /service-accounts/{id}/tokens` — **not routed until entry condition 4 is green** |
| `service-accounts.tokens.delete` | `ServiceAccountsTokensDelete` | `permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens delete` | `DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/tokens/{tokenId}` |

> **The last two rows were re-spelled by Q-w2-7's ruling (orchestrator, 2026-09-11), and rev 6.1 removes the derivation STOP they carried.** Rev 6 spelled them `service-accounts.issue-token` / `.revoke-token`, which reached **neither** constructor: `make()` builds the action as `$verb->value.'_'.$qualifier` (`949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:645`), so `issue --qualifier=token` would have derived `service-accounts.issue_token` with an UNDERSCORE rather than the hyphen the plan and the spec wrote — and more fundamentally **`PermissionVerb` declares neither `issue` nor `revoke`** among its 32 cases (`…-wave-1.md:451-493`), while `legacy()` is closed to any key absent from its shrink-only baseline. **The ruling took the token out of the qualifier and made it the resource**: nested resource **`service-accounts.tokens`** with the declared verbs **`Create`** (`…-wave-1.md:455`) and **`Delete`** (`…-wave-1.md:457`), no qualifier, giving `service-accounts.tokens.create` and `service-accounts.tokens.delete`. **Nesting is the shipped idiom, not an invention**: `RESOURCE_PATTERN` admits *"at most one nested segment"* (`…-wave-1.md:580`) and the catalogue already spells `inventory.transfers.create` and `inventory.adjustments.post` that way (`…-wave-1.md:4098-4106`); `catalog.attributes.*` is the other family. The rejected alternative — adding `Issue`/`Revoke` to wave 1's closed verb enum — is a **producer** change to a final plan and is refused by this plan's Global Constraints. **All six keys now derive, so Step 0 scaffolds all six unconditionally.**

- [ ] **Step 0 — declare the six keys, dry-run first, and verify every derived key and enum case.**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w2b/apps/api

      # 1. The four CRUD keys on the resource `service-accounts`. --dry-run prints the
      #    five artefacts and writes nothing.
      for v in view create update delete; do
        php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts "$v" --dry-run
      done

      # 2. The two token keys, on the NESTED resource `service-accounts.tokens` with
      #    declared verbs and NO qualifier (Q-w2-7, RULED 2026-09-11). Rev 6 spelled
      #    these `issue --qualifier=token` / `revoke --qualifier=token`, which derived
      #    nothing: PermissionVerb declares neither `issue` nor `revoke`, and a
      #    qualifier joins with an UNDERSCORE. RESOURCE_PATTERN admits one nested

      php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens create --dry-run
      php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens delete --dry-run

      # 3. Apply all six. NO --template: admin is implicit and the option refuses the literal.
      for v in view create update delete; do
        php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts "$v"
      done
      php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens create
      php artisan permissions:scaffold Identity service-accounts.tokens delete

      # 4. Confirm all SIX keys AND their six enum cases exist — the parity test asserts
      #    set equality in BOTH directions, so a manifest entry without its case is red.
      #    The second probe fails the step if a rev-6 spelling survived anywhere.
      php artisan tinker --execute='$r = app(\App\Shared\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry::class);
        $keys = ["service-accounts.view","service-accounts.create","service-accounts.update",
                 "service-accounts.delete","service-accounts.tokens.create","service-accounts.tokens.delete"];

        }

      **`permissions:scaffold` writes five artefacts per key** — the manifest entry, the **enum case**, and the `en`/`fr`/`ar` locale stubs (`…-wave-1.md:8521`) — which is where `IdentityPermission::ServiceAccountsCreate` and its siblings come from, **including `ServiceAccountsTokensCreate` and `ServiceAccountsTokensDelete`**: the case-name transform is PascalCase of the whole key splitting on `.`, `-` and `_` (`…-wave-1.md:3589`), so the nested resource simply contributes a segment. Nothing in this task hand-writes an enum case.

**Route group middleware stack**, following `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:57`:
`['api', 'auth:sanctum', SetPermissionsTeam::class, EnforceTokenTenantClaim::class, EnforceTokenScope::class]` plus **one `'can:'.IdentityPermission::<Case>->value` per route** — the `Concat` form the Global Constraints require. Every `can:<key>` written in the tables of this task is shorthand for that expression; **`routes.php` never carries the joined literal**, which `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` reads through its `can:` prefix and rejects, and `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php` imports `App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\IdentityPermission` in this commit.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the creation contract, §4.1.2 verbatim.**

~~~php
// apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Requests/CreateServiceAccountRequest.php
//
// Rev 4 expressed authorize() and the typed 403 as a COMMENT HEADER and shipped only
// rules() (gate r4, Wave 2 MAJOR 2). Both are implemented below, against the human
// path they are required to mirror: dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/
// Requests/CreateUserRequest.php:19-29 (authorize) and :60-82 (failedAuthorization).

/**
 * service-accounts.create, PLUS the SAME location gate the human path uses whenever
 * any membership carries allowed_location_ids.
 */
public function authorize(): bool
{
    $actor = $this->user();

    if (! $actor?->can(IdentityPermission::ServiceAccountsCreate->value)) {
        return false;
    }

    // The human path reads a FLAT `allowed_location_ids`; a service account's lists are
    // per membership, so the predicate is "any membership carries one".
    $memberships = $this->input('memberships');
    $carriesLocationList = is_array($memberships) && collect($memberships)->contains(
        static fn (mixed $m): bool => is_array($m) && array_key_exists('allowed_location_ids', $m),
    );

    return ! $carriesLocationList || $actor->can(IdentityPermission::UsersManageLocationAccess->value);
}

/**
 * Preserve the location-grant API error contract before validation runs — the body is
 * REUSED VERBATIM from dev:CreateUserRequest.php:68-80, so the two paths ship one
 * error contract rather than two that drift.
 */
protected function failedAuthorization(): void
{
    $actor = $this->user();
    $memberships = $this->input('memberships');
    $carriesLocationList = is_array($memberships) && collect($memberships)->contains(
        static fn (mixed $m): bool => is_array($m) && array_key_exists('allowed_location_ids', $m),
    );

    if (
        $carriesLocationList
        && $actor instanceof User
        && $actor->can(IdentityPermission::ServiceAccountsCreate->value)
        && ! $actor->can(IdentityPermission::UsersManageLocationAccess->value)
    ) {
        throw new HttpResponseException(response()->json([
            'error' => [
                'code' => 'FORBIDDEN',
                'message' => 'You do not have permission to manage location access.',
            ],
            'meta' => [
                'timestamp' => now()->toIso8601String(),
                'request_id' => $this->header('X-Request-ID', (string) uuid_create()),
            ],
        ], Response::HTTP_FORBIDDEN));
    }

    parent::failedAuthorization();   // the generic 403 for a plain missing-key refusal
}

public function rules(): array
{
    // The ACTOR is obtained here, exactly as the existing AssignRoleRequest does
    // it (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Requests/AssignRoleRequest.php:28-35,
    // `$actor = $this->user();` before `new AssignableRole($actor)`). Rev 2 wrote
    // `new AssignableRole($currentUser)` with $currentUser never defined, so the
    // method raised an undefined-variable error on every call (plan gate r2, MAJOR 5).
    // A FormRequest is NOT constructor-injectable -- Laravel resolves it from the
    // container per request -- so $this->user() is the sanctioned accessor here and
    // the repository already uses it at the site above.
    $actor = $this->user();

    return [
        'name'                                 => ['required', 'string', 'max:255'],
        'description'                          => ['nullable', 'string', 'max:255'],
        'memberships'                          => ['required', 'array', 'list', 'min:1'],
        'memberships.*.company_id'             => ['required', 'uuid'],
        'memberships.*.allowed_location_ids'   => ['sometimes', 'nullable', 'array', 'list'],
        'memberships.*.allowed_location_ids.*' => ['uuid'],
        'roles'                                => ['required', 'array', 'list', 'min:1'],
        'roles.*'                              => ['string', 'exists:roles,name', new AssignableRole($actor)],
    ];
}
~~~
> **Imports for the block above**, since it is a file and not a fragment: `App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\IdentityPermission`, `App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User`, `App\Modules\Identity\Presentation\Rules\AssignableRole`, `Illuminate\Foundation\Http\FormRequest`, `Illuminate\Http\Exceptions\HttpResponseException` and `Symfony\Component\HttpFoundation\Response` — **six**: the five `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Requests/CreateUserRequest.php:5-12` carries minus `Illuminate\Validation\Rule` (which this request does not use), **plus `IdentityPermission`**, which the Global Constraints' no-bare-literal rule makes mandatory here. Rev 5 said *"the same five"* while the body carried four bare literals (plan gate r5, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 1**).
> **`UpdateServiceAccountMembershipsRequest` and `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest` take the same `$actor = $this->user();` line** wherever they construct `AssignableRole`, for the same reason and citing the same site. A FormRequest that constructs an escalation guard without an actor is the shape of this defect, not an instance of it.

      > **Memberships are established in the same transaction as the principal.** A service token without an active company membership is refused by `EnforceTokenScope` and is therefore useless; a route that creates one and leaves memberships to a second call ships a dead credential.
- [ ] **Step 2 — `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`, the ONE writer, and it is a LOCKED writer.** Constructor-injected into `ServiceAccountController` (`private readonly`, rule 13). It creates the `users` row, the `user_company_memberships` rows and the location grants, and assigns the roles — **the same shape `UserController::store` uses inline today (`dev:217-258`), extracted rather than duplicated**, so `ServiceAccountWriterCensusTest` can assert no other production path inserts a `principal_kind = 'service'` row.
      **It assigns roles, so it takes wave 0b's shared lock** (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-1**): one transaction; `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` as its **first** statement on the `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` key; the `roles` rows it will attach `FOR UPDATE` in **`roles.name` ASC then `roles.id` ASC** order; a **re-read** of those rows after the lock (a role renamed or deleted between validation and write is refused with 422 `ROLE_NO_LONGER_EXISTS` rather than silently skipped); then the writes. `ServiceAccountProvisioningService::provision` joins `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` **in this commit**, and `LastAdminFloorWriterCensusTest`'s `ASSERTED_EXEMPT` map already names it (Task 2a-2 Step 4), so the two censuses agree. It calls **`PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()`** (Task 2b-6) inside the creation transaction, refusing with 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_LIMIT_REACHED`.
      A service account **cannot** be assigned a role the actor does not hold — the existing `AssignableRole` escalation guard applies unchanged. The last-admin floor is unaffected in either direction, because a service principal never counts toward *A* (OQ-1's applied default).
- [ ] **Step 3 — the generic surface's refusal is ALREADY LIVE, from 2a.** Q-w2-4 moved `refuseServiceTarget()` and its seven call sites into lane 2a (**Task 2a-2a**), with the real predicate, `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` and `UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest`. **2b writes no new guard here.** Its obligation is to **re-run** both tests against a service principal created the production way — through `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` rather than through the factory — and to add that one case to `ServiceTargetRefusalTest`, which is the only thing 2a could not prove. The route table below is retained as the reviewer's map of where the seven guards sit. **422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES`**, whose body names the `/service-accounts/*` route that does the job.
      | Route | Method | Where the guard goes |
      |---|---|---|
      | `POST /users` | `store()` `dev:192-302` | **no target row exists** — instead `CreateUserRequest` may not set `principal_kind`, and the provisioning service is the only `'service'` inserter. **Asserted, not guarded** |
      | `PATCH /users/{id}` | `update()` `dev:303-429` | after the target lookup, before the `syncRoles`/`update` at `dev:357-391` |
      | `DELETE /users/{id}` | `destroy()` `dev:430-508` | before the membership revocation at `dev:988-998` and the token delete at `dev:470-484` |
      | `POST /users/{id}/activate` | `activate()` `dev:509-579` | before the status write and the membership restore at `dev:1057-1070` |
      | `POST /users/{id}/deactivate` | `deactivate()` `dev:580-669` | before the status write at `dev:630-642` |
      | `PATCH /users/{id}/pos-pin` | `setPosPin()` `dev:670-756` | **already refuses** with `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN` on **both** arms (`dev:706`, `dev:739`) — that more specific code **wins and is not replaced**: *"a machine cannot hold a PIN"* is a truer message than *"use the other route"* |
      | `POST /users/{id}/reset-password` | `resetPassword()` `dev:757-827` | before the `NO_EMAIL` branch at `dev:790` and the `notify()` at `dev:804`, so the refusal is the **kind** check rather than the accidental *"no email"* |
      | **`POST /users/{userId}/roles`** | `RoleController::assignRole` `dev:350-359` | **first statement after the target row is resolved, before the `LastAdminFloor` check and any write.** This is the sharpest one: `users.assign-roles` alone would otherwise let a caller change a service principal's **owner grants** — the exact quantity the `owner grants ∩ token scope` guarantee is written over |
      | **`DELETE /users/{userId}/roles`** | `RoleController::removeRole` `dev:390-398` | the same |
      **`GET /users` and `GET /users/{id}` are reads and stay open to `users.view`**, because support needs to see a service principal exists; `index()` gains the `principal_kind` **filter** instead.
- [ ] **Step 3a — the two dedicated role actions are LOCKED writers too.** `POST|DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/roles` attach and detach `model_has_roles` rows, which is exactly what wave 0b's lock serialises. Each opens one transaction, acquires `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` first, `FOR UPDATE`s the named role in `name`→`id` order, re-reads, writes — and each joins `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` in this commit. Rev 1 described these two actions with no lock at all (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B1-1**).

      | Route | Gate | Body |
      |---|---|---|
      | `POST /api/v1/service-accounts/{id}/roles` | `can:service-accounts.update`, plus `new AssignableRole($actor)` on `role`, with **`$actor = $this->user();`** taken first — the **same escalation guard** `AssignRoleRequest::rules()` applies at `dev:AssignRoleRequest.php:28-35`. Rev 4 reintroduced the undefined `$currentUser` here after correcting it twenty lines above (gate r4, Wave 2 **MAJOR 2**); `$actor` is this plan's one spelling | `{ "role": "<name>" }` |
      | `DELETE /api/v1/service-accounts/{id}/roles` | the same two | `{ "role": "<name>" }` |
      `service-accounts.update` is deliberately the gate rather than a seventh `service-accounts.assign-roles` key: changing which roles a machine holds is the same administrative act as changing its memberships.
- [ ] **Step 5 — `PUT /service-accounts/{id}/memberships`.** `PATCH /service-accounts/{id}` covers name, description and active status **only**; this is the one place a service principal's companies and `allowed_location_ids` change, gated on `service-accounts.update` plus `users.manage_location_access` when a location list is supplied, and it **routes through `MembershipRevocationService` (Task 2b-5) for any removal** so the token-revocation trigger cannot be bypassed.
- [ ] **Step 6 — `UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest` is 2a's and is NOT re-specified here.** Rev 2 restated it as *"every public method … that resolves a `users` row by route parameter"* — the **over-broad rule the accepted contract already rejected**, which would have pulled `index`, `show` and `userRoles` (all reads, all deliberately open to `users.view`) into `MUST_REFUSE` and contradicted the closed list twenty lines earlier in this same plan (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 8**; the original finding is plan gate r1 **MAJOR M1-6**).
      **The authoritative statement is Task 2a-2a Step 3**: a closed `MUST_REFUSE` list of **seven write methods**, a closed `EXEMPT` map of **five**, both pinned by exact equality, and an anti-growth direction that fires only on a method that resolves a user by route parameter **and performs a write**. 2b adds **no** constant, **no** entry and **no** rule to it.
      **2b's only obligation here is the one case 2a could not prove**: re-run `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` and `UserServiceTargetRefusalCensusTest` against a service principal created the **production** way — through `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` rather than through the model factory — and add that single case, as Step 3 above already states. Neither constant changes; the census file is staged in this commit **only** if that case lives in it.
- [ ] **Step 7 — `ServiceAccountProvisioningTest` (EC-32b)**, asserting the **rows** written (user, membership, location grant, role pivots) and the 422 when the plan cap is reached; **`ServiceAccountLocationScopeTest`** (convention-09 second-location axis): a service token whose principal's membership carries `allowed_location_ids` is refused a write at another location, and the identical payload succeeds once the restriction is removed; **EC-34's seven refusals**, each asserting the target's rows are **unchanged**, and the same caller holding `service-accounts.update` succeeding on the dedicated route.
- [ ] **Step 8 — lower the write ratchet ceiling** by the eight newly-gated routes and run `php tools/feature-lane-manifest-check.php`.
