---

## Task 2b-5 — SPLIT BY REV 3 INTO TWO COMMITS AT ITS OWN SEAM

> **Rev 3 splits this task** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B3**). Revocation and issuance have opposite dependency directions: `MembershipRevocationService` needs **nothing** from lane 2b — its two production call sites, `UserController::destroy` and `::deactivate`, are already in the tree at `dev` — while issuance is an action **on the controller Task 2b-4 creates**. Keeping them in one commit forced that commit either before its own controller or after the surface that routes through it; neither is buildable.
>
> | Half | Task id | `Phase` | Position | Contains |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | **revocation** | **2b-5a** | **`0.5.5`** | FOURTH | `MembershipRevocationService`, `RevokePrincipalTokens`, `ApiTokenRevoked`, `TokenTtl`, the human-TTL edit to `AuthController::tokenExpiresAt()`, and re-pointing `UserController::destroy`/`::deactivate` at the service |
> | **issuance** | **2b-5b** | **`0.5.7`** | SIXTH | `POST /service-accounts/{id}/tokens`, `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest`, `DELETE …/tokens/{tokenId}`, `AuthUserData.token_scope`, `/auth/me` |
>
> The steps below are marked **[2b-5a]** or **[2b-5b]**, and each half has its own add list and its own subject at the end.

**Files**

- Create: `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest.php`, `MembershipRevocationService.php`, `RevokePrincipalTokens.php` (job), `TokenTtl.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/DTOs/AuthUserData.php:27-28`, `AuthController::me()` `dev:616-628`, `UserController::destroy`/`deactivate` (route their revocation through the service)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountTokenIssuanceTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/ServiceAccountRevocationTest.php`

**Steps**

- [ ] **[2b-5a] Step 0 — `TokenTtl` and the human-TTL edit land HERE, in `Phase 0.5.5`.** `TokenTtl`'s three constants and its three named constructors, plus the `AuthController::tokenExpiresAt()` edit the note in Step 1 gives verbatim, are the revocation half's because they need **nothing** from lane 2b: both call sites already exist at `dev`. Issuance (`Phase 0.5.7`) then *consumes* `TokenTtl::forService()`. Splitting them this way is what lets `Phase 0.5.5` be green alone (**BLOCKER B3**).
- [ ] **[2b-5b] Step 1 — issuance.** `POST /service-accounts/{id}/tokens` under `service-accounts.tokens.create`, **an action on the `ServiceAccountController` `Phase 0.5.6` created**. It **always** mints the `tenant:<uuid>` ability and there is **no code path that can create a service token without it** — `ServiceAccountTokenIssuanceTest` asserts it. Abilities are `permission:<key>` entries forming a **narrowing subset**; `*` is rejected; an ability naming a key outside `PermissionRegistry` is accepted but surfaced as `unknown` (rule 4). TTL from `TokenTtl` (OQ-2): **service 365 d**, **human 90 d**, **POS terminal 1 y unchanged**, all as explicit per-row `expires_at`, **no unlimited option**, plus `sanctum:prune-expired --hours=24` on the daily schedule.
      > **The human half is an EDIT to the login path, not a declaration.** Rev 1 chose 90 days for human back-office tokens and modified no issuance path, so every human token would have kept `null` and fallen through to the global 30-day `config('sanctum.expiration')` (`dev:apps/api/config/sanctum.php:43-53`) — the very policy the plan said it was changing (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M1-1**). The edit is one method:
      > ```php
      > // dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:154-157
      > private function tokenExpiresAt(Request $request): \DateTimeInterface
      > {
      >     return $this->isPosClient($request)
      >         ? TokenTtl::forPosTerminal()   // now()->addYear() — byte-identical to today
      >         : TokenTtl::forHuman();        // now()->addDays(90) — was null, i.e. the global 30 d
      > }
      > ```
      > Its return type narrows from `?\DateTimeInterface` to `\DateTimeInterface`: **there is no null path left**, which is what makes *"no unlimited option"* a type-level property rather than a convention. Both call sites already pass the value into `createToken()`'s third argument (`dev:294-310` and the register/device path), so neither changes shape.
      > **`HumanTokenTtlTest`** asserts the three lifetimes on the **persisted rows** — a web login token's `expires_at` 90 days out, a POS token's a year, a service token's 365 days — and that **none** is null. The global `sanctum.expiration` stays at 43 200 minutes and is **not edited**, so no existing token's lifetime changes retroactively; `AppServiceProvider`'s per-row override (`dev:apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:201-219`) is what makes the explicit values win.
      **`X-Company-Id` for service accounts.** A service token has no session and no company switcher, so `CompanyContextMiddleware` (`dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:148`) resolves the company from the `X-Company-Id` header. **Required** for a service principal on any route needing a company (absence → 400 `COMPANY_CONTEXT_REQUIRED`); the value must name a company in which the principal holds an **active** membership (otherwise 403 `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_NO_MEMBERSHIP`); for a human principal the header keeps its current, optional behaviour. **A service account is never given a "default company"** — an ambiguous machine call is a bug, and a required header makes it a loud one.
- [ ] **[2b-5b] Step 2 — `/auth/me` gains ONE additive field.** `AuthUserData` keeps `roles` and `permissions` where they are (`dev:AuthUserData.php:27-28`), and `permissions` continues to be the **effective** list, already intersected by `User::getAllPermissions()`.

~~~php
public ?TokenScopeData $token_scope,   // null when the token does not narrow

final readonly class TokenScopeData
{
    /** @param list<string> $granted @param list<string> $unknown */
    public function __construct(
        public string $token_id,
        public string $token_name,
        public array $granted,          // keys the scope names AND the principal holds
        public array $unknown,          // keys the scope names that are not in the registry
        public ?string $expires_at,
    ) {}
}
~~~

      So an MCP server reads `/auth/me` **once**, sees exactly what it may do, and gets a **403 from `can:`** if it tries anything else. There is no MCP-specific permission, no MCP-specific role and no MCP-specific bypass.
- [ ] **Step 3 — `MembershipRevocationService`, the single call path.** No standalone membership-removal writer exists today: revocation only happens inside deletion/deactivation (`dev:UserController.php:988-998`). *"When a membership is removed"* is not a rule a reviewer can check; *"every caller goes through this service"* is. Constructor-injected, `private readonly`.
      **The membership mutation is tenant-side and the token delete is central-side, so they cannot share a transaction** — `CentralPersonalAccessToken` is central-connection pinned (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Infrastructure/CentralPersonalAccessToken.php:11-41`, registered at `dev:apps/api/app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:196-219`). The contract: **commit the tenant-side membership change first, then attempt the central-side revoke best-effort; on failure push `RevokePrincipalTokens` onto the queue with the principal id and retry with backoff, and record the pending revocation in the audit chain.** The window is bounded and observable rather than hidden behind a false atomicity claim, and `EnforceTokenScope`'s membership check is what makes it harmless in practice: a token whose principal has no active membership in the resolved company is refused on the **very next request** regardless of whether its row is gone yet.
      Three triggers: (1) the principal's **last active membership in the tenant** is removed → **all** its tokens revoked (a removal leaving at least one active membership revokes nothing; the removed company is denied by the `X-Company-Id` check instead); (2) principal deactivated or deleted → all tokens revoked (this trigger **already exists** at `dev:470-484,630-642` and is re-pointed at the service); (3) explicit `DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/tokens/{tokenId}`.

      > **Rev 3 supplies the three bodies. Rev 2 supplied none** — it described the best-effort contract, the retry, the pending audit evidence and `ApiTokenRevoked` in prose and shipped no service, no job and no event, which is the no-stub standard this plan's own rule 1 forbids (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 7**).

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Services;

use App\Modules\Compliance\Services\AuditService;
use App\Modules\Identity\Application\Jobs\RevokePrincipalTokens;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LastAdminFloor;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Events\ApiTokenRevoked;
use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\CentralPersonalAccessToken;
use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\Locks\PermissionWriteLock;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Bus\Dispatcher as BusDispatcher;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher as EventDispatcher;
use App\Shared\Infrastructure\Auth\CurrentUserId;
use Illuminate\Database\ConnectionInterface;
use Throwable;

/**
 * The ONE call path every membership-removing writer uses, and a LOCKED writer.
 *
 * WHY A SERVICE AND NOT A RULE: "when a membership is removed, revoke the
 * principal's tokens" is not something a reviewer can check; "every caller goes
 * through this class" is, and MembershipWriterCensusTest is what checks it.
 *
 * WHY TWO PHASES: the membership row is TENANT-side and personal_access_tokens
 * is CENTRAL-side (CentralPersonalAccessToken is connection-pinned,
 * dev:.../Infrastructure/CentralPersonalAccessToken.php:11-41, registered at
 * dev:.../Providers/AppServiceProvider.php:196-219). Two connections cannot share
 * a transaction, so this class does NOT pretend to atomicity: it commits the
 * tenant side, then attempts the central side best-effort, and on failure queues
 * a retry and records the revocation as PENDING in the audit chain.
 *
 * WHY THAT WINDOW IS HARMLESS: EnforceTokenScope (Task 2b-3) refuses a service
 * token whose principal holds no active membership in the resolved company on the
 * VERY NEXT REQUEST, regardless of whether its row is gone yet. The window is
 * observable (an audit row says `pending`) rather than hidden behind a false claim.
 */
final readonly class MembershipRevocationService
{
    public function __construct(
        private ConnectionInterface $tenantDb,
        private PermissionWriteLock $writeLock,
        private LastAdminFloor $floor,
        private CentralPersonalAccessToken $centralTokens,
        private BusDispatcher $bus,
        private EventDispatcher $events,
        private AuditService $audit,
        private CurrentUserId $currentUserId,     // null-safe in console/queued contexts
    ) {}

    /**
     * Trigger (1): one membership removed.
     *
     * @throws \App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\LastAdminFloorViolationException
     */
    public function revokeMembership(User $principal, string $companyId, string $tenantId): void
    {
        $wasLast = $this->tenantDb->transaction(function () use ($principal, $companyId, $tenantId): bool {
            // LOCKED writer, wave 0b's contract: shared advisory lock FIRST, on the
            // lane key, then this writer's own rows. Membership state is what the
            // floor's A reads, so an unlocked removal races the floor's decision.
            $this->writeLock->acquire($tenantId);

            $this->floor->assertMembershipRemovalSurvives($principal, $companyId, $tenantId);

            $this->tenantDb->table('user_company_memberships')
                ->where('user_id', $principal->id)
                ->where('company_id', $companyId)
                ->orderBy('id')
                ->lockForUpdate()
                ->delete();

            return $this->tenantDb->table('user_company_memberships')
                ->where('user_id', $principal->id)
                ->where('status', 'active')
                ->doesntExist();
        });

        // A removal that leaves at least one active membership revokes NOTHING:
        // the token stays valid for the companies that remain, and the removed
        // company is refused by EnforceTokenScope's X-Company-Id membership check.
        if ($wasLast) {
            $this->revokeAllTokens($principal, 'last_membership_removed');
        }
    }

    /** Trigger (2): the principal is deactivated or deleted. Called by UserController::destroy/deactivate. */
    public function revokeForPrincipal(User $principal, string $reason): void
    {
        $this->revokeAllTokens($principal, $reason);
    }

    /** Trigger (3): DELETE /service-accounts/{id}/tokens/{tokenId}. */
    public function revokeToken(User $principal, int $tokenId): void
    {
        $this->attemptCentralDelete(
            $principal,
            fn (): int => $this->centralTokens->newQuery()
                ->where('tokenable_id', $principal->id)
                ->whereKey($tokenId)
                ->delete(),
            'explicit_revocation',
            [$tokenId],
        );
    }

    private function revokeAllTokens(User $principal, string $reason): void
    {
        /** @var list<int> $ids */
        $ids = $this->centralTokens->newQuery()
            ->where('tokenable_id', $principal->id)
            ->pluck('id')
            ->map(static fn (mixed $id): int => (int) $id)
            ->all();

        if ($ids === []) {
            return;
        }

        $this->attemptCentralDelete(
            $principal,
            fn (): int => $this->centralTokens->newQuery()
                ->where('tokenable_id', $principal->id)
                ->delete(),
            $reason,
            $ids,
        );
    }

    /**
     * The best-effort half. A HARD DELETE of the personal_access_tokens rows --
     * not a status flag -- because Sanctum authenticates on row presence and a
     * soft-deleted token is a valid token.
     *
     * @param  \Closure(): int  $delete
     * @param  list<int>        $tokenIds
     */
    private function attemptCentralDelete(User $principal, \Closure $delete, string $reason, array $tokenIds): void
    {
        try {
            $delete();
            $status = 'revoked';
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            // Queue the retry and RECORD the pending state. Swallowing the
            // throwable is deliberate: the tenant-side change is already
            // committed and re-throwing here would 500 a request whose primary
            // effect succeeded, while EnforceTokenScope has already made the
            // surviving token useless.
            $this->bus->dispatch(new RevokePrincipalTokens(
                (string) $principal->id,
                (string) $principal->tenant_id,
                $tokenIds,
                $reason,
                $this->currentUserId->current(),
            ));
            $status = 'pending';

            // TENANT-WIDE, not company-scoped: a revocation is about a PRINCIPAL and a
            // CREDENTIAL, and this path also runs from a queued worker and from
            // UserController::destroy, neither of which is guaranteed a company.
            // AuditService::record() requires companyId, userId, eventType,
            // aggregateType and aggregateId BEFORE the optional payload/metadata
            // (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Services/AuditService.php:43-51) --
            // rev 3 called it with two named arguments and would have fatalled on
            // ArgumentCountError at runtime and failed PHPStan L8 statically, while
            // php -l passed because named-argument PHP is syntactically valid
            // (gate r3, Wave 2 BLOCKER 4).
            //
            // recordTenantWide() is wave 1 rev 6.3's, shipped by its Task 1.9:
            //   recordTenantWide(string $tenantId, ?string $actorUserId, string $eventType,
            //                    string $aggregateType, string $aggregateId,
            //                    array $payload = [], array $metadata = []): AuditEvent
            $this->audit->recordTenantWide(
                tenantId: (string) $principal->tenant_id,
                actorUserId: $this->currentUserId->current(),   // null in a queued/console context
                eventType: 'identity.api_token.revocation_pending',
                aggregateType: 'User',
                aggregateId: (string) $principal->id,
                payload: [
                    'principal_id' => (string) $principal->id,
                    'token_ids' => $tokenIds,
                    'reason' => $reason,
                ],
                metadata: [
                    'error' => $e::class,
                ],
            );
        }

        $this->events->dispatch(new ApiTokenRevoked(
            principalId: (string) $principal->id,
            tenantId: (string) $principal->tenant_id,
            tokenIds: $tokenIds,
            reason: $reason,
            status: $status,                 // 'revoked' | 'pending'
            occurredAtIso: now()->toIso8601String(),
            actorUserId: $this->currentUserId->current(),
        ));
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Application\Jobs;

use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Events\ApiTokenRevoked;
use App\Modules\Identity\Infrastructure\CentralPersonalAccessToken;
use App\Modules\Tenant\Domain\Tenant;
use Illuminate\Bus\Queueable;
use Stancl\Tenancy\Tenancy;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Events\Dispatcher as EventDispatcher;
use Illuminate\Contracts\Queue\ShouldQueue;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Bus\Dispatchable;
use Illuminate\Queue\InteractsWithQueue;
use Illuminate\Queue\SerializesModels;

/**
 * The queued retry for the central-side delete. It is IDEMPOTENT by construction:
 * deleting rows that are already gone deletes zero rows and succeeds, which is the
 * property that lets it be retried without a guard.
 *
 * QUEUE NAME: 'identity-revocation', and it MUST be listed in
 * apps/api/config/horizon.php's defaults.*.queue -- an unlisted onQueue() name is
 * silently never consumed (CLAUDE.md rule 20) and EC-16a's "queued retry" would
 * become "the token is never revoked". HorizonQueueCoverageTest is the CI guard.
 */
final class RevokePrincipalTokens implements ShouldQueue
{
    use Dispatchable, InteractsWithQueue, Queueable, SerializesModels;

    /** Six attempts over ~1h: the central database being down is the case this exists for. */
    public int $tries = 6;

    /** @var list<int> */
    public array $backoff = [30, 120, 300, 900, 1800];

    /**
     * `$tenantId` and `$actorUserId` are carried BY THE JOB because a queued worker
     * has no tenancy and no CompanyContext (CLAUDE.md rule 20): the closing
     * ApiTokenRevoked this job emits must land on the SAME tenant's audit chain as
     * the `pending` row that preceded it, and there is nothing in the worker's
     * context to derive it from. Rev 3's job carried neither (gate r3, Wave 2
     * BLOCKER 5).
     *
     * @param list<int> $tokenIds
     */
    public function __construct(
        private readonly string $principalId,
        private readonly string $tenantId,
        private readonly array $tokenIds,
        private readonly string $reason,
        private readonly ?string $actorUserId = null,
    ) {
        $this->onQueue('identity-revocation');
    }

    public function handle(CentralPersonalAccessToken $centralTokens, EventDispatcher $events, Tenancy $tenancy): void
    {
        // The DELETE is CENTRAL-side and needs no tenancy; the EVENT is written to
        // the tenant's audit chain and does. Initialise explicitly, and end it in a
        // finally so a throw cannot leak a bound tenant into the next queued job on
        // this worker.
        $deleted = $centralTokens->newQuery()
            ->where('tokenable_id', $this->principalId)
            ->whereIn('id', $this->tokenIds)
            ->delete();

        $tenancy->initialize(Tenant::query()->findOrFail($this->tenantId));

        try {
            // Emitted on EVERY successful run, including the zero-row one, so the
            // audit chain carries the close of the pending window rather than leaving
            // a `pending` row as the last word.
            $events->dispatch(new ApiTokenRevoked(
                principalId: $this->principalId,
                tenantId: $this->tenantId,
                tokenIds: $this->tokenIds,
                reason: $this->reason,
                status: 'revoked',
                occurredAtIso: now()->toIso8601String(),
                actorUserId: $this->actorUserId,
                retriedRows: $deleted,
            ));
        } finally {
            $tenancy->end();
        }
    }
}
~~~

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

namespace App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Events;

use App\Shared\Domain\Events\DomainEvent;

/**
 * `identity.api_token.revoked`. Immutable forever (CLAUDE.md rule 8); a later
 * shape change is ApiTokenRevokedV2.
 *
 * `status` is the field that makes the two-phase window OBSERVABLE: 'pending'
 * means the tenant side committed and the central delete has not yet succeeded,
 * 'revoked' means the rows are gone. An auditor asking "when did this credential
 * stop working" reads 'revoked'; an operator asking "is anything stuck" queries
 * for 'pending' with no later 'revoked' for the same token ids.
 */
final class ApiTokenRevoked extends DomainEvent
{
    /**
     * `tenantId` is REQUIRED and is the field that makes this event PERSISTABLE.
     *
     * Rev 3 omitted it, and the subscriber could not have written the row (gate r3,
     * Wave 2 BLOCKER 5): DomainEventSubscriber::persistEvent() takes a non-null
     * `string $companyId` (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Listeners/
     * DomainEventSubscriber.php:1186-1193) and a token revocation HAS no company --
     * a principal's credential is tenant-wide, and this event is also emitted from
     * a queued worker with no CompanyContext at all. The spec already defines the
     * company-less pattern for exactly this shape (roles-permissions-catalogue-design
     * 1732-1737): recordTenantWide() + persistTenantWideEvent(), both shipped by
     * wave 1 rev 6.3 Task 1.9.
     *
     * @param list<int> $tokenIds
     */
    public function __construct(
        public readonly string $principalId,
        public readonly string $tenantId,
        public readonly array $tokenIds,
        public readonly string $reason,
        public readonly string $status,          // 'revoked' | 'pending'
        public readonly string $occurredAtIso,
        public readonly ?string $actorUserId = null,
        public readonly ?int $retriedRows = null,
    ) {
        parent::__construct($principalId);
    }

    public function getEventName(): string
    {
        return 'identity.api_token.revoked';
    }

    /** @return array<string, mixed> */
    public function getAuditPayload(): array
    {
        return [
            'principal_id' => $this->principalId,
            'tenant_id' => $this->tenantId,
            'token_ids' => $this->tokenIds,
            'reason' => $this->reason,
            'status' => $this->status,
            'retried_rows' => $this->retriedRows,
            'occurred_at' => $this->occurredAtIso,
        ];
    }
}
~~~

      **`ApiTokenRevoked`'s HANDLER, in `DomainEventSubscriber`, in THIS commit — and it goes through `persistTenantWideEvent()`, not `persistEvent()`** (gate r3, Wave 2 **BLOCKER 5**). Rev 3 said only *"register it"*, and the subscriber's one persistence method requires a non-null company id, so the row could not have landed:
      ~~~php
      // DomainEventSubscriber::subscribe() returns a MAP of event class => handler
      // method name (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Compliance/Listeners/
      // DomainEventSubscriber.php:1236-1240), so the registration is ONE ARRAY ENTRY
      // added beside the existing ones, inside the returned array:
      //
      //     ApiTokenRevoked::class => 'handleApiTokenRevoked',
      //
      // and the handler is a PUBLIC method on the same class, because
      // persistTenantWideEvent() is private to it:
      public function handleApiTokenRevoked(ApiTokenRevoked $event): void
      {
          // persistTenantWideEvent() is wave 1 rev 6.3's sibling to persistEvent()
          // (949dfabdf:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:5616-5648). It takes
          // the actor FROM THE EVENT rather than from Auth::id(), which is exactly what a
          // queued revocation needs: the operator who triggered it is on the event, and
          // the worker has no authenticated user.
          $this->persistTenantWideEvent(
              event: $event,
              tenantId: $event->tenantId,
              actorUserId: $event->actorUserId,
              aggregateType: 'User',
              aggregateId: $event->principalId,
              eventType: $event->getEventName(),
              payload: $event->getAuditPayload(),
          );
      }
      ~~~
      > **An unregistered event writes no audit row, silently** — which is why Step 4's test asserts a **row lands** rather than that the event was dispatched, and why it asserts `company_id IS NULL` and a non-empty `event_hash` on that row rather than only its presence.
      > **Wave 1 rev 6.3 is what makes this possible**, and it is an entry condition rather than an assumption: `AuditEvent::__construct`'s guard widened to `if ($companyId !== null || isset($attributes['tenant_id']))`, `AuditService::recordTenantWide()` and `DomainEventSubscriber::persistTenantWideEvent()` all ship in wave 1's Task 1.9 (`949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:5479,5552-5648`). **If wave 1 has not merged, 2b does not start** — which the Entry condition already requires.
      > **`CentralPersonalAccessToken` is constructor-injected as a model instance** and queried through `newQuery()`, never through a static facade — CLAUDE.md rule 13, and it is what makes EC-16a's forced failure bindable to a double.
      > **`AuditService::record()`'s exact signature is read off the tree before this is written** — Task 2a-5 edits that method and its parameter names are the tree's, not this plan's.
- [ ] **[2b-5a] Step 4 — `ServiceAccountRevocationTest` (EC-16, EC-16a)**, both branches, **plus `MembershipWriterCensusTest` asserting no other production path removes a membership**. EC-16a forces the central delete to fail — by binding `CentralPersonalAccessToken`'s connection to a double whose `delete()` throws — and asserts **four** things: the job is **really pending**; an `ApiTokenRevoked` audit row exists carrying `status: pending`; the token row is **still present**; and **the very next request with that token is 403 `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_NO_MEMBERSHIP`** — which is what makes the window harmless rather than merely bounded.
      > **The queue oracle is a `jobs`-table assertion, NOT `Queue::assertPushed`** (gate r3, Wave 2 **MAJOR 2**). Rev 3 asked for both `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database` *"so the job is not run inline"* **and** `Queue::assertPushed` — and those are mutually exclusive: `Queue::assertPushed` is provided by `Queue::fake()`, which swaps the queue manager for a fake and therefore **bypasses the database connection entirely**, so nothing is ever written to `jobs` and the "really pending" half of the contract is unprovable. The two halves are split:
      > - **`the_central_delete_failure_queues_a_retry` (the REAL-pending case, and the one EC-16a names):** `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database`, **no `Queue::fake()`**. Assert `DB::connection(config('queue.connections.database.connection'))->table('jobs')->where('queue', 'identity-revocation')->count() === 1`, and decode that row's `payload` to assert the serialised class is `RevokePrincipalTokens` and its `tenantId` is the principal's. **That is what "pending" means** — a consumable row on the named queue — and it is also what proves the `onQueue('identity-revocation')` name against `config/horizon.php`, which `HorizonQueueCoverageTest` then guards statically.
      > - **`the_retry_job_is_dispatched_once_per_failure` (the DISPATCH case):** `Queue::fake()` and `Queue::assertPushed(RevokePrincipalTokens::class, 1)`. This one is about **call arity** — that a single failure queues one job and not two — and it deliberately says nothing about the database.
      > **Two tests, two oracles, neither pretending to be the other.** A single test that fakes the queue and then claims the job is "really pending" is the shape of assertion that passes for years while the queue name is wrong.
      **`MembershipWriterCensusTest`** is the static half, and it is what turns *"every caller goes through this service"* from prose into a property: every production write that deletes or deactivates a `user_company_memberships` row — an Eloquent `delete()`/`update(['status' => …])` on the model, or a `DB::table('user_company_memberships')` `delete()`/`update()` — must be **inside `MembershipRevocationService`** or in a stated `ASSERTED_EXEMPT` map. **It ships with EXACTLY ONE entry, and the map is fixed at dispatch rather than at run time** (gate r3, Wave 2 **MAJOR 3**):
      ```php
      /** @var array<string, string>  method key => why it does not route through MembershipRevocationService */
      private const ASSERTED_EXEMPT = [
          'app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php::activate'
              => 'the membership RESTORE at dev:1057-1070 re-activates a row; it GROWS the active-membership '
               . 'set, so it revokes nothing and a revocation call there could only ever be a no-op',
      ];
      ```
      > **Rev 3 left this conditional and its own change log contradicted it** — the change log claimed an empty `ASSERTED_EXEMPT`, the task said to add the restore *"if the census flags it"*, and the verification checklist accepted either outcome. **A static census whose map is decided by running it is not a contract**, and three documents disagreeing about one map is worse than any of the three being wrong.
      > **The census predicate is `delete()` or `update(['status' => …])` on a `user_company_memberships` row, and the restore at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:1057-1070` matches it** — it is an `update` on `status`. So it **is** flagged, it **is** in the map, and the map has **one** entry. Phase 0 re-runs the predicate at the lane base and the handback records the result; **a second flagged site is a finding that needs a reviewer and an owner note**, not a second entry added quietly. The two sites this task re-points — `UserController::destroy` (`dev:988-998`) and `::deactivate` — route through the service and are therefore **not** exempt.
      > Reuse wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus` AST walker (`collect()`, `methodSource()`, `callersOf()`) rather than writing a second one (convention 11).
- [ ] **[2b-5a] Step 5 — run** (PG lane, plus the Horizon coverage test) and **commit the REVOCATION half.** Exact add list:

      Subject: **`Phase 0.5.5: Revoke a principal's tokens through one locked membership path with an observable pending window, and give human tokens an explicit 90-day expiry`**, both trailers.
      **Five files rev 2's single list carried are NOT here** — they belong to the issuance half (`Phase 0.5.7`): `IssueServiceAccountTokenRequest.php`, `ServiceAccountController.php`, `AuthUserData.php`, `TokenScopeData.php`, `ServiceAccountTokenIssuanceTest.php`. **Four are NEW in rev 3** — `ApiTokenRevoked.php`, `DomainEventSubscriber.php`, `MembershipWriterCensusTest.php` and `HorizonQueueCoverageTest.php` — because rev 2 named the event, the pending audit evidence, the writer census and the queue entry in prose and staged none of them (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 7**).
      > **`RevokePrincipalTokens` needs a Horizon queue entry.** Its `onQueue('identity-revocation')` name must be added to `apps/api/config/horizon.php`'s `defaults.*.queue`; **an unlisted `onQueue()` name is silently never consumed** (CLAUDE.md rule 20), which would turn EC-16a's *"queued retry"* into *"the token is never revoked"*. `HorizonQueueCoverageTest` is the CI guard, is staged **in this commit** because this commit is what makes it fail otherwise, and must stay green. **Read `config/horizon.php` first** and reuse a listed queue if one fits rather than adding a name.
      > **`MembershipRevocationService` is a LOCKED writer**: it removes membership state the floor's *A* reads and calls `LastAdminFloor::assertMembershipRemovalSurvives()`, so it opens one transaction, acquires `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` first, and joins `LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` in this commit.
      > **`HorizonQueueCoverageTest`'s path is RESOLVED, not deferred** (gate r3, Wave 2 **MAJOR 4**). `git ls-files '*HorizonQueueCoverageTest.php'` at `dev` returns exactly one path — **`apps/api/tests/Unit/Config/HorizonQueueCoverageTest.php`** — and that is what the add list above names. It is a pre-existing CI guard (CLAUDE.md rule 20); this commit **modifies** it (adding `identity-revocation`), it does not create it. Phase 0 re-runs the `git ls-files` at the lane base and records the result; **a path that has moved is re-pinned there**, which is a different thing from a path that was never resolved.

Phase 0 re-reads 2a's committed `TokenScopeData` and confirms: if 2a has already added the field, the file is **removed** from this list in the handback with that one line as the reason. **Either way the list is exact before dispatch, and the decision is recorded** — it is never left to whoever runs `git add`.

## Task 2b-6: plan seats — all six counters, and `max_service_accounts`

> **Executed THIRD in lane 2b, as `Phase 0.5.4`** — moved from `Phase 0.5.6` by rev 3 (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B3**). It **introduces** `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount()`, which Task 2b-4 calls inside its creation transaction; it must precede its caller. It compiles and is green in isolation: the six predicates read `principal_kind` (2a's column) and the tier constant needs nothing from this lane.

**Files**

- Modify: the six counters below; `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Billing/Domain/PlanLimits.php:19-35,161-173`; `PlanEnforcementService`
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/PlanSeatCensusTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Billing/ServiceAccountSeatExclusionTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Billing/PlanLimitsContractTest.php`

**Subscription usage counts every `users` row today.** Left alone, creating a service account would silently consume a **paid human seat**. The ruling (spec §4.1.7): service principals are **excluded from the human seat count** and capped by a new plan limit `max_service_accounts`, **default 5**. All three reference ERPs bill a bot as a user, which is precisely what drives operators to share one human login with a script.

**The ruling applies to EVERY counter, not one.** A service account excluded from the display but still consuming the admission check is **worse** than not excluding it at all, because the two numbers then disagree.

| # | Site | Drives | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `dev:apps/api/app/Services/PlanLimitsService.php:83-85` | the usage array | predicate; **plus** a `service_accounts` key counting `principal_kind = 'service'` |
| 2 | **`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Billing/Application/Services/PlanEnforcementService.php:162-176`, count at `:165`** | **admission** — the check run before a human is created | predicate. **This is the real enforcement point** |
| 3 | `PlanEnforcementService::getUsageStats()` `dev:290-305`, count at `:296` | the tenant-facing usage/percent panel | predicate |
| 4 | `PlanEnforcementService::calculateUserOverage()` `dev:426-444`, count at `:434-436` | **per-user overage billing** | predicate — without it an integration credential is **invoiced as an extra human** |
| 5 | `dev:apps/api/app/Http/Controllers/Api/Admin/SuperAdminController.php:88-97`, count at `:93` | support/admin display | predicate; surface `service_accounts_count` beside it so support can still see them |
| 6 | `dev:apps/api/app/Services/TenantFleetStatsService.php:36-51`, count at `:49` | fleet totals | predicate |

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — `PlanSeatCensusTest` first (red).** Static analysis: **no production `users` row count exists outside this list without the predicate.** Same census shape as the last-admin floor's, and the reason a single edit would not have been enough.
- [ ] **Step 2 — apply the six predicates.**
- [ ] **Step 3 — `max_service_accounts` joins the limits contract properly.** A **constant on `PlanLimits`** beside `MAX_USERS` (`dev:19-35`), added to **every** plan-tier default array — `trial()` (`dev:161-173`) **and each of its siblings** — because a limit absent from a tier's array falls back to whatever default the reader passes, which is how a cap silently becomes zero or unlimited (Q-w2-6). Enforcement point: a new `PlanEnforcementService::canAddServiceAccount(Tenant $tenant): bool`, in the same shape as `canAddUser()` (`dev:162-176`), called by `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` **inside the creation transaction**, refusing with 422 `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_LIMIT_REACHED`.
- [ ] **Step 4 — `PlanLimitsContractTest`** asserts **every** tier array contains the key, so a ninth tier added later fails the build. **`ServiceAccountSeatExclusionTest`** asserts all six numbers agree on a tenant with 3 humans and 2 services: usage says 3, admission admits a 4th human, the panel says 3, overage bills 3, the support display says 3 with `service_accounts_count = 2`, fleet totals say 3 — and that a 6th service account is refused.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run** and **commit** (`Phase 0.5.4: Exclude service principals from every human seat counter and cap them with max_service_accounts`, both trailers). Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Services/PlanLimitsService.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Billing/Application/Services/PlanEnforcementService.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Billing/Domain/PlanLimits.php \
              apps/api/app/Http/Controllers/Api/Admin/SuperAdminController.php \
              apps/api/app/Services/TenantFleetStatsService.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/PlanSeatCensusTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Billing/ServiceAccountSeatExclusionTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Billing/PlanLimitsContractTest.php
      ```
      **This commit introduces `canAddServiceAccount()` and has NO caller yet** — Task 2b-4 (`Phase 0.5.6`) is the caller, and it lands two commits later. A public method with no production caller is correct here and is what makes this commit independently green; `PlanLimitsContractTest` is what exercises it.

## Task 2b-7: POS — service accounts can never hold a PIN

> **Executed SEVENTH in lane 2b, as `Phase 0.5.8`** — moved from `Phase 0.5.7` by rev 3's re-sequence. Placed after the service-account surface so the PIN exclusion is asserted against a principal created the **production** way rather than through the factory.

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/PosAuthController.php` — `pinHolders()` `:46-57`, `verifyPin` `:64-118` (the `Hash::check` at `:90`), `pinData` `:170-250`, `hasPins` `:336-350`, `setupPin` `:120-168` (the write at `:146`), `syncPins` `:251-335` (the write at `:307`)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Feature/POS/ServicePrincipalPosExclusionTest.php`

**Four changes, all narrow; the POS authorization model itself is sound and is not redesigned.**

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — the human predicate, added ONCE, inside `pinHolders()`.** One clause — `->where('principal_kind', PrincipalKind::Human->value)` — on the builder at `dev:53-56`, and **nowhere else**. `pinData` (`dev:170-176`) and `hasPins` (`dev:336-344`) already consume `pinHolders()` (`dev:87`, `dev:206`, `dev:344`), so they inherit it: rev 1's wording — *"the same predicate goes on `pinData` and `hasPins`"* — reads as three insertions and would leave two redundant clauses for a later reader to reconcile (plan gate r1, **minor m1-1**). **One insertion, three behavioural tests**, one per surface, each asserting the service principal is **absent from the returned rows** rather than merely denied.
      A service principal has `pos_pin IS NULL` by the §5.1 CHECK, **so the predicate is belt-and-braces; it is added anyway** because `pinHolders()` is documented as *"the single definition of an operator PIN this terminal may act on"* (`dev:34-45`), and a definition that depends on another invariant holding is one refactor away from being wrong.
- [ ] **Step 2 — the kind check before `Hash::check`** on the PIN path, at `dev:90`, exactly as on login.
- [ ] **Step 3 — refuse the two PIN writes**: `setupPin` (`dev:146`, **the caller's own row** — the self-service path) and `syncPins` (`dev:307`, the raw update), both 422 `SERVICE_PRINCIPAL_CANNOT_HOLD_PIN`.
- [ ] **Step 4 — the approval-scope derivation reads the registry.** The device's `pos.approve_*` scope derivation reads from `PERMISSION_MODULES`-grouped registry data rather than a hand-maintained list, so a new approval permission reaches the terminal on the next deploy **without a POS release**. **Approval scopes themselves are unchanged**: the manager-PIN step-up flow, `PinVerifier::verifyForApproval` and the `pinHolders()` population contract keep their semantics exactly.
- [ ] **Step 5 — the PIN payload is NOT narrowed, and the test says so.** `verifyPin` returns `$user->getAllPermissions()->pluck('name')` on a principal loaded from the database (`dev:99`), which by the `getAllPermissions()` contract is the holder's **unnarrowed** grants — formally `EffectivePermissionResolver::forTarget($pinHolder, null)`. That is the **correct** semantics: the payload answers *"what may this operator approve"*, not *"what may this terminal's credential do"* — narrowing an approver's authority by the till's token would make a manager override depend on which device it was performed at. `ServicePrincipalPosExclusionTest::the_pin_payload_is_not_narrowed_by_the_terminal_token` asserts the non-change.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run** (PG lane) and **commit** (`Phase 0.5.8: Exclude service principals from every POS PIN surface and derive approval scopes from the registry`, both trailers). Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/PosAuthController.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/POS/ServicePrincipalPosExclusionTest.php
      ```
      **`apps/pos/src` is not touched by this task.** The legacy role ladder at `dev:apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:354` and `dev:apps/pos/src/lib/auth/roles.ts` are **wave 3**.

## Task 2b-8: the service-account frontend, and the 2b browser probe

> **Executed EIGHTH and LAST in lane 2b, as `Phase 0.5.9`** — moved from `Phase 0.5.8` by rev 3's re-sequence, which adds one commit to the lane by splitting Task 2b-5.

**Files**

- Create: `apps/web/src/features/settings/ServiceAccountsPage.tsx`, `components/ServiceAccountForm.tsx`, `components/IssueTokenDialog.tsx`, `components/TokenList.tsx`, `api/serviceAccounts.ts`, `__tests__/`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx` (the gated route), `apps/web/src/components/organisms/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx` (the nav entry), the three locale bundles
- Create: `apps/web/e2e/rbac/service-accounts.spec.ts`

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — vitest first (red)** for the three components: the form refuses submit without at least one membership and one role; the issue-token dialog shows the **plaintext token exactly once** with a copy affordance and a "you will not see this again" notice; the token list shows `expires_at` and a revoke affordance gated on `service-accounts.tokens.delete`.
- [ ] **Step 2 — build the screens.** Route gated with `RequirePermission permission="service-accounts.view"` **and** `moduleKey` per the both-layer gating rule (CLAUDE.md rule 12). Types come from the generated DTOs (`php artisan typescript:transform` after the PHP DTOs land) — **never hand-rolled beside a generated DTO** (convention 11). TanStack keys via `tenantScopedKey`. Design tokens only. Every string via `t()`.
- [ ] **Step 3 — the probe**, `apps/web/e2e/rbac/service-accounts.spec.ts`, same scripted harness and the same two captures asserted at zero. Journey: log in as `admin` → Settings → Service accounts → create one with a membership restricted to location A and the `viewer` role → issue a token narrowed to a **subset** of `viewer`'s keys → assert the plaintext appears once → then, **out of band with `curl`**, the MCP-shaped probe:
      ```
      TOKEN=<the issued plaintext>
      curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "X-Company-Id: $COMPANY" http://127.0.0.1:8011/api/v1/auth/me | jq '.data.token_scope'
      # expect: granted = the narrowed subset, unknown = []
      curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "X-Company-Id: $COMPANY" \
        http://127.0.0.1:8011/api/v1/<a route gated on a key the OWNER holds but the TOKEN excludes>
      # expect: 403 — NOT 500
      curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" http://127.0.0.1:8011/api/v1/products
      # expect: 400 COMPANY_CONTEXT_REQUIRED (no X-Company-Id)
      ```
      → back in the browser, revoke the token → assert the same `curl` now returns 401. **Paste the verbatim `curl` output into the handback** — this is the master plan's exit-checklist item ④ and a screenshot is not it.
- [ ] **Step 4 — run** `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, the playwright spec and the curl block, then **commit** (`Phase 0.5.9: Add the service-account and token screens and prove an MCP-shaped narrowing end to end`, both trailers). Exact add list:

              apps/web/src/features/settings/components/ServiceAccountForm.tsx \
              apps/web/src/features/settings/components/IssueTokenDialog.tsx \

              apps/web/src/features/settings/__tests__/IssueTokenDialog.test.tsx \
              apps/web/src/features/settings/__tests__/TokenList.test.tsx \

              apps/web/src/locales/en/common.json \

      **`packages/shared/types/generated.ts` is in the list** because Step 2 runs `php artisan typescript:transform` after 2b's PHP DTOs land, and a regenerated type left unstaged is a red `pnpm typecheck` on the next lane (CLAUDE.md rule 7). **Confirm the generated file's path off the tree** — `git status --porcelain packages/shared` after the transform names it.

## Convention-09 obligations (spec §6, wave-2 row)

Roles, permissions and users are **already excluded** from `TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest` with stated reasons (`dev:apps/api/tests/Architecture/TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest.php:131-132,160,175`). **This wave's obligation is not to add an exclusion but not to invalidate an existing one** — it adds no unique key at all, so the ratchet must be **green and unedited**. Two of the three axes still apply as real tests:

| Axis | Test | Assertion (on **data meaning**, never on status) |
|---|---|---|
| **Second company** | `LastAdminFloorTest::the_floor_is_per_tenant_not_per_company` | Two companies in one tenant; removing the admin role from the only admin is refused **even though company B has its own owner** (§4.6.3 clause b). Plus `ServiceAccountTwoCompanyTest`: one service account with memberships in **both** companies resolves the same tenant-scoped roles in each while `X-Company-Id` selects which company's rows it may touch |
| **Second location** | `ServiceAccountLocationScopeTest` | A service token whose principal's membership carries `allowed_location_ids` is refused a write at another location, and the **identical payload** succeeds once the restriction is removed — asserting the **rows written**, never the status alone |
| **Re-run / idempotency** | `ServiceAccountIdempotencyTest` | Issuing the same token twice creates **two distinct tokens** (no dedup — a token is a credential, not a resource); revoking a principal's membership **twice** is idempotent (second call writes nothing, asserted by query-log count); re-applying a template twice is a no-op (wave 1's, re-asserted here because 2a's RolesPage exposes the button) |
| **Token narrower than principal** | `TokenScopeNarrowingTest` | A token naming keys the principal does **not** hold grants none of them; a token naming a subset grants exactly the intersection |

## Reviewer gate

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on both lanes, on the whole diff.** Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w2a` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) — and separately
> `lane/rbac-w2b` — against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9
> §§4.1, 4.4.2, 4.5.4, 4.6, 4.10, 5.1, 5.3, 6 and 8's wave-2 row, and against this
> plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify against the tree, never from the
> diff alone.
>
> 1. **Is the narrowing enforced in exactly one place?** Find every production
>    site that reads a token ability. There must be `tokenScopePermissionNames()`
>    and `EnforceTokenScope` and nothing else — no `tokenCan()` in a controller,
>    a policy or a FormRequest. Then prove the generalisation did not change an
>    impersonation session's behaviour.
> 2. **Is `EnforceTokenScope` ATTACHED, not merely prioritised?** Read
>    `bootstrap/app.php` and name the line that appends it to the `api` group.
>    Then boot the router and find a route with `auth:sanctum` that does not
>    resolve it. There must be none, and the coverage test must have no
>    allow-list.
> 3. **Does the subject-type no-op actually protect the central pipeline?** Read
>    `handle()`'s first statement. Then construct a central `SuperAdmin` request
>    and say which of the four checks would have touched a field that model does
>    not have.
>  4. **Is the last-admin floor really one implementation, and does it take the
>    SHARED lock first?** List every writer that can shrink *A* and show each
>    reaches `LastAdminFloor`. Pay particular attention to
>    `UserController::update`'s `syncRoles` — the wholesale replacement that
>    never touches `removeRole`. Then read the lock sequence and name, in order,
>    the four statements the floor issues: is `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()`
>    first, on the `wlota1a:` key? Are the `roles` rows locked `name` then `id`,
>    i.e. wave 0b's order? Are the `model_has_roles` pivots locked at all? A
>    second advisory key, a second row order, or an unlocked pivot is a BLOCKER.
>    Then say what happens when two admins remove each other.
>  4a. **Are there any structural stubs left?** `grep -n "return null;"` in
>    `refuseServiceTarget()` and read the body: does it branch on a real column?
>    Then read `LastAdminFloor`'s human clause: is it a `where()` or a comment?
>    Rev 1 shipped both as placeholders and rev 2 moved the schema to fix it —
>    verify the schema is actually in the 2a diff.
>  4b. **Does `EffectivePermissionResolver` have bodies?** Open the file. Two
>    method declarations ending in `;` inside a class do not parse. Then check
>    the owner-grant read is the **unnarrowed** one, that `permission:` parsing
>    drops unknown keys into `token_scope.unknown` rather than silently, that
>    `*` is never honoured as "everything", and that a foreign `token_id` is a
>    404 rather than an empty scope.
>  4c. **Does every named failure type and event exist?**
>    `LastAdminFloorViolation`, `LastAdminFloorViolationException`,
>    `AdminPermissionFloorViolation`, `RoleUpdateRefused` and
>    `RoleUpdateRefusalReason` — each as a file in the diff, each registered in
>    `DomainEventSubscriber` where it is an event, and `RoleUpdateRefused`
>    carrying `role_id`, `reason`, `principal_id` and `token_id` (EC-13).
> 5. **Is the attribution in the right argument?** Find the merge in
>    `AuditService::record()`. If it is in `attributes:`, the pair lands nowhere
>    and the test that "passes" is asserting the wrong thing — check the test
>    reads the value **back off the persisted column**.
> 6. **Is the denial dedup atomic, and is it recorded where the 403 is actually
>    produced?** Find the read-and-clear. Two round trips is a finding. Then find
>    the recording site: it must be the `AccessDeniedHttpException` render
>    callback in `bootstrap/app.php` (plus `RequireAnyPermission` and
>    `EnforceTokenScope`, which build their own responses). **If you find a
>    middleware inspecting `$next($request)` for a 403, that is a BLOCKER** — a
>    thrown authorization denial unwinds past it and is rendered by the handler,
>    so the middleware's body after `$next()` never runs. Then check the test
>    drives the race from a second connection and asserts the **outcome**, not an
>    impossible interleaving, and that a throwing recorder leaves the 403 body
>    byte-identical.
> 7. **Is the shaping real or nominal?** For `GET /users/{userId}/roles`, show
>    the `permissions` key is **absent** — not empty — for a bare
>    `users.assign-roles` holder.
> 8. **Are the eight role-name sites converted without widening authority?**
>    For each, say who could do it before and who can do it after. Any widening
>    is a finding, not a refactor.
> 9. **Does a service account have exactly one write surface?** Enumerate every
>    production path that mutates a `principal_kind = 'service'` row. Then check
>    the two role routes: a `users.assign-roles` holder must be refused on the
>    generic pair with the target's `model_has_roles` rows **asserted unchanged**.
> 10. **Do all six seat counters agree?** Re-derive each and name the predicate.
>     Then check `max_service_accounts` is in **every** tier array.
> 11. **Is the `down()` honest?** Read all **five** steps and confirm the
>     data-loss statement is in the migration itself, that an `AuditEvent` is
>     written **before** each deletion, that step 3 drops **both** PG
>     constraints and **both** SQLite triggers by name, and that step 4 cannot
>     run before step 2.
>  11a. **Is the `principal_kind` DOMAIN closed in the database?** Read the
>     migration and find `CHECK (principal_kind IN ('human','service'))` as its
>     own named constraint, and the matching `NOT IN` clause inside **both**
>     SQLite triggers. Then find the test that inserts `principal_kind = 'robot'`
>     **raw** — not through `User::create()`, which the enum cast would reject
>     before the statement reached the database. A shape-only check is satisfied
>     by an arbitrary kind and is a BLOCKER.
> 12. **Convention 09 and 11:** name the second-company, second-location and
>     re-run tests and say what data meaning each asserts. Then look for a
>     second surface for any noun this wave introduces.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory on both lanes.** Ask specifically: is `uiAliasPermissions.ts` **deleted** and every consumer re-pointed at a real backend key? Does `usePermissions` have **no** role-name path and **no** fail-open fallback left? Is `Permission` the generated union only? Are the new screens built from **generated DTOs** rather than hand-rolled interfaces (convention 11)? Every TanStack key through `tenantScopedKey`? Design tokens only, no hardcoded Tailwind colours? Every user-facing string through `t()`, in all three locales? Is there exactly **one** RolesPage grouping implementation (Q-w2-2)?

**`fiscal-pos-reviewer` — mandatory for the PIN path (2b Task 2b-7) and advisory for `DiscountPermissionResolver` (2a Task 2a-8).** Ask: does the `principal_kind` check precede **every** `Hash::check` on the PIN path? Do all three PIN surfaces (`verifyPin`, `pinData`, `hasPins`) share the same population? Is the PIN payload still the **holder's unnarrowed** grants — and is the non-change asserted rather than assumed? Does `pos.override_discount_limit` preserve `ADMIN_MAX_PERCENT` byte-for-byte?

**`inventory-costing-reviewer` — mandatory for the seven Inventory conversions (2a Task 2a-8).** Ask: does `inventory.countings.create`'s template default include `manager`, so a real manager does not lose the ability to start a counting draft on deploy day? Is the **creator** clause in the six bypasses untouched? Is `inventory.countings.override_ownership` `admin`-only, i.e. exactly today's behaviour?

**`treasury-reviewer` — advisory (2b Task 2b-2).** One predicate on `TreasuryAlertRecipients::forCompany()` changes the recipient set of **six** senders. Ask: is that the only edit, and does any of the six build its own query?

## Handback

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w2a-<ISO date>.md` and `docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w2b-<ISO date>.md`.

Each carries: the lane base SHA and tip SHA; the re-pinned line numbers for every `dev:` citation the lane touched, with any that moved called out; the `Phase` ordinal actually used (Q-w2-1); the Q-w2-2 finding (did wave 1 ship the RolesPage grouping?); the Q-w2-3 soak decision as the orchestrator made it; the verbatim `curl` block from 2b's MCP-shaped probe; the ceiling values before and after; the staging `down()` rehearsal evidence (2b); and the residuals below.

### Residuals

| # | Residual | Owner / wave |
|---|---|---|
| **R-w2-1** | `apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:354` still reads `user.roles.includes('super_admin') \|\| user.roles.includes('admin')`. `'super_admin'` can never appear in a tenant login's roles, so the offline-PIN-setup bypass is reachable only through the `'admin'` half. Removing the **ladder** means the offline path must fall back to the last synced permission payload instead of a role name — a POS release with an offline-behaviour test. It stays in wave 1's ESLint baseline with the ceiling recording it. | **wave 3** |
| **R-w2-2** | `MembershipRole` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Company/Domain/Enums/MembershipRole.php`) and Spatie role names are two vocabularies for one noun. `UserController.php:929` (`$membership?->isOwner()`) is the only authz **read** of the former. Reconciliation is wave 3's. | **wave 3** |
| **R-w2-3** | **OQ-1** — if the owner rules that a service admin **does** satisfy the floor, the bounded edit is three changes (drop the `principal_kind = human` clause, delete EC-4, re-word the glossary row). **Ask before 2a merges** — Q-w2-4 moved the column, the floor's human clause and EC-4 all into 2a, so a ruling arriving after 2a costs a revert rather than an edit (plan gate r2, **minor 3**). | **owner** |
| **R-w2-4** | **OQ-2** — human back-office tokens **lengthen from 30 to 90 days**. That is a policy change, applied as this wave's default, and it is stated rather than buried in a constant. The bounded edit is `TokenTtl`'s three values. | **owner** |
| **R-w2-5** | The **5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** (EC-17) is untouched. A UI may render a stale affordance for up to five minutes; **every mutating request is still 403**. Recorded as a known **UX** exposure, not a security one. | out of scope |
| **R-w2-6** | `AuthorizationDenied`'s `cause: token_scope` branch is reachable in 2a only for an impersonation token, and is generalised by 2b. If 2a merges and 2b is delayed, the field is correct but rarely populated — not a defect, but it will look like one to whoever reads the audit chain first. | 2b |
| **R-w2-10** | **2a ships a column no production path can populate** until 2b's `ServiceAccountProvisioningService` lands (Q-w2-4). Between the two merges every `users` row is `principal_kind = 'human'`, the service-target refusals are unreachable in production, and the floor's human clause is the identity in practice. That is the **correct** intermediate state — the schema is the precondition, not the feature — but it is stated so a reviewer does not read the empty `service` population as a bug. | 2a → 2b |
| **R-w2-11** | **`Phase 0.5.1` is unused** and `Phase 0.3.*` is unused across the programme (Q-w2-1, Q-w2-4). Both gaps are deliberate; a reader looking for a missing lane will not find one. | recorded |
| **R-w2-12** | **`AuthorizationDenialMarker` depends on the exception handler being the single render point for `AuthorizationException`.** If a module later catches that exception and renders its own 403, the denial goes unrecorded — silently. The mitigation is the four negatives in `AuthorizationDenialScopeTest` plus a grep in the reviewer prompt; a static census over `catch (AuthorizationException` would be stronger and is not built here. | follow-up lane |
| **R-w2-7** | The denial recorder writes to the **tenant's Redis store**. If a deployment runs one Redis for all tenants without a per-tenant prefix, two tenants' denial triples with identical `(principal_id, permission, route)` hashes cannot collide (the principal id is a UUID), but the **key space is shared** and a `FLUSHDB` by one operator clears every tenant's counters. Recorded, not fixed. | ops |
| **R-w2-8** | `EnforceTokenScope`'s membership check runs a query per request for a **service** principal. Human requests hit the subject-type/unscoped-token fast path and pay one null check. If service traffic ever becomes hot, the membership read is the thing to cache — deliberately **not** cached now, because a cached membership is exactly what makes revocation slow. | ops / future |
| **R-w2-9** | Two `roles` grouping vocabularies may coexist for one release if wave 1 shipped a grouping and 2a shipped another (Q-w2-2). The reviewer is asked to confirm there is one; if there are two, the extra one is deleted in the same lane, not deferred. | 2a reviewer |

---

## Deploy

**Lane 2a**

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | **first, always.** WRITE-free, **not** failure-free: a blocked or not-ready tenant exits non-zero and the applying run must not be started. |
| 1 | `php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling` | **2a now ships the `users` migration** (Q-w2-4) — three columns, the CHECK / trigger pair, `password` nullable, fleet-wide. This step is real work in 2a, not ceremony. |
| 2 | **`php artisan permissions:sync-fleet`** | **not optional.** 2a declares two new keys (`inventory.countings.override_ownership`, `pos.override_discount_limit`); until sync creates the rows and re-runs `admin = activeKeys()`, the six converted counting bypasses and the POS discount ceiling **deny for everyone including `admin`**. Never `tenants:run permissions:sync`; never the per-tenant command without `--tenant=`; there is no `--force`. |
| 3 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | the boot-time global flush stays as a belt. |
| 4 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | the map gains the two new keys. |

**Lane 2b**

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | as above. |
| 1 | `php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling` | 2b adds **no migration** — 2a shipped the `users` one (Q-w2-4). The step runs because the deploy sequence is fixed and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` asserts **one document, in this exact order**; a wave that skips a step because it has nothing to do fails that test. |
| 2 | **`php artisan permissions:sync-fleet`** | the six `service-accounts.*` keys and the `admin` re-run. |
| 3 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | |
| 4 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | |
| 5 | confirm `sanctum:prune-expired --hours=24` is on the daily schedule | OQ-2's other half; a TTL policy with no pruner leaves expired rows forever. |

**Deploy-checklist row (both lanes).** *After deploy, confirm `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reports `permissions_sync.healthy = true` — the **authenticated** super-admin endpoint at `dev:apps/api/routes/api.php:83`; the public `/v1/health` is unchanged. If it reports false, grep the container log for the `PERMISSIONS-SYNC-FLEET` aggregate line, which names every blocked or failed tenant id with its reason, and re-run `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --tenants=…` for those tenants — **each selector resolving to exactly one tenant**, per §4.3.3's step-1a contract — before considering the deploy complete.*

**2a additionally:** after the migration, spot-check one tenant that the CHECK exists and that no existing row moved —
```
psql -h 157.180.71.252 -p 5434 -d tenant<uuid> -c "\d+ users" | grep -i principal_kind
psql -h 157.180.71.252 -p 5434 -d tenant<uuid> -c "SELECT principal_kind, count(*) FROM users GROUP BY 1;"
# expect: exactly one row, human = <the tenant's existing user count>
```

## Rollback

**Lane 2a — revert, and 2a is now the lane whose migration `down()` DESTROYS DATA** (Q-w2-4 moved it here). Rehearse the `down()` on staging before 2a promotes; the five ordered operations are Task 2b-1 Step 2's, executed as Task 2a-0.

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 2a-9 … 2a-1 | as below | as below |
| **Task 2a-0 (the migration)** | the five ordered operations: refuse while service tokens exist → delete every service principal (**data loss**, logged and audited per deletion) → drop **both PG constraints** (`users_principal_kind_check`, `users_service_principal_check`) **and both SQLite triggers**, by name → drop the three columns and their indexes in reverse order → restore `password NOT NULL` | **everything else in 2a first, and all of 2b** — `refuseServiceTarget()`, the floor's human clause, `EnforceTokenScope` and every service-account surface read the column |
| Task 2a-9 (FE) | the alias map and the fail-open fallback return | — (but the two new keys stay in the catalogue; they are inert) |
| Task 2a-8 (role-name conversion) | the eight sites return to `hasRole()`; the baseline grows back | **2b must not have merged** — a scoped token with a role-name bypass reachable is precisely what the entry condition forbids. If 2b has merged, **do not revert this task**; fix forward. |
| Task 2a-7 (denials) | denial events stop; the Redis keys are inert and expire on their own | — |
| Task 2a-6 (resolver) | `userRoles` returns to the raw `getAllPermissions()` | Task 2a-4 first (its shaping calls the resolver) |
| Task 2a-5 (events + attribution) | role mutations stop being audited; the pair disappears from `metadata` | — (existing rows keep the pair; it is additive metadata) |
| Task 2a-4 (shaping) | I-18 returns: any authenticated caller reads any user's full permission list **only if 0b-15's gate is also reverted** — the gate stays, so the exposure does not return | — |
| Task 2a-3 (F-1) | `admin`'s permission set becomes operator-editable again | — |
| Task 2a-2 (floor) | a tenant can lose its last admin | Task 2a-1 first if `RoleController::destroy`'s admin refusal is being relied on |
| Task 2a-2a (service-target refusal) | the seven generic writes accept a service target again | **2b must not have merged** — a service principal whose owner grants can be edited from the generic surface is exactly what `owner grants ∩ token scope` is written over |
| Task 2a-1 (`is_system`) | the two name lists return | everything above |

**Lane 2b — no migration of its own.** Its riskiest revert is `EnforceTokenScope`, which is a middleware attachment rather than a schema change.

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 2b-8 (FE) | the service-account screens disappear | — |
| Task 2b-7 (POS) | the three PIN surfaces stop excluding service principals | Task 2b-1's CHECK still forbids a service PIN, so the exposure is theoretical — but the **belt** is gone and that is the whole reason it exists |
| Task 2b-6 (seats) | a service account consumes a paid human seat and is invoiced as one | — |
| Task 2b-5 (tokens/revocation) | issuance and the revocation service disappear; **existing service tokens keep working** | Task 2b-4 first, and **revoke every live service token before reverting**, or a credential outlives its management surface |
| Task 2b-4 (service accounts) | the write surface disappears while service rows remain | Task 2b-5 first |
| Task 2b-3 (`EnforceTokenScope`) | the narrowing returns to impersonation-only; status/membership enforcement disappears from every authenticated surface | **revoke every live service token first** — a service token whose principal is inactive would otherwise authenticate again |
| Task 2b-2 (census) | notification selectors may include service principals | Task 2b-1's `down()` deletes them anyway |
| **Task 2b-1** | — | **moved to 2a as Task 2a-0**; its revert row is in the 2a table above |

**The one thing a revert cannot undo** is a service account a tenant created and used: its audit rows name a `principal_id` whose `users` row the `down()` deletes. That is why step 2 writes an `AuditEvent` **before** each deletion — the rollback is reconstructible from the chain even though the rows are gone.

## Change log rev 6 → rev 6.1 (Q-w2-7 ruling applied)

*(post-6.1 editorial: apply line resource)* — Task 2a-8 Step 1's step-3 **apply** line passed the resource as `countings` while step 2's **dry-run** line passed `inventory.countings`; the two now match. The apply line reads `permissions:scaffold Inventory inventory.countings override --qualifier=ownership`, which is the invocation step 4's derived-key probe already asserts — its second echo STOPs on exactly the `countings.override_ownership` the old apply line would have produced. No revision bump: nothing but the one argument moves.

**Not a gate round.** No reviewer finding is answered here; one open question is closed by an orchestrator ruling and applied mechanically and in prose. **Wave 1 is untouched** (still final at rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`), and no baseline, ceiling or enum in any producer plan moves.

**The ruling, verbatim in effect (orchestrator, 2026-09-11, Q-w2-7): option (B), re-spell. No verb-enum change, no baseline growth.**

| Was (rev 6) | Is (rev 6.1) | Decomposition through `PermissionDefinition::make()` |
|---|---|---|
| `pos.discount_unlimited` | `pos.override_discount_limit` | resource `pos`, verb `Override`, qualifier `discount_limit` |
| `service-accounts.issue-token` | `service-accounts.tokens.create` | nested resource `service-accounts.tokens`, verb `Create`, no qualifier |
| `service-accounts.revoke-token` | `service-accounts.tokens.delete` | nested resource `service-accounts.tokens`, verb `Delete`, no qualifier |

**Verb-existence check performed before applying the ruling.** `PermissionVerb` at the pinned producer (wave 1 rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`, `…-wave-1.md:451-493`) declares `Override = 'override'` (`:487`), `Create = 'create'` (`:455`) and `Delete = 'delete'` (`:457`). **All three exist**; none is invented. Neither `issue`, `revoke`, `discount` nor `unlimited` is declared, which is what made the rev-6 spellings undeclarable. Nested resources are permitted by `RESOURCE_PATTERN` (*"kebab-case with at most one nested segment"*, `…-wave-1.md:580`) and already in the catalogue as `inventory.transfers.create` / `inventory.adjustments.post` (`…-wave-1.md:4098-4106`) and `catalog.attributes.*`.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| **Q-w2-7** | Rewritten from an open owner/next-gate question into a **RULED** entry: the ruling, the three-key mapping, the verb-existence check with `file:line`, the nested-resource precedent, the reasoning (closed verb enum is a producer artefact this wave may not edit; the legacy escape hatch is shrink-only **and keyed by permission key**, so a 60th key is growth whatever `LEGACY_ACTION_CEILING` says), why re-spelling is the smaller change (all three keys are brand new, held by nobody, in no migration and no API contract — no rename map, no deprecation), and an inventory of what the ruling changed in this plan |
| **Task 2a-8 Step 1 — the executable legacy-growth STOP** | **REMOVED.** It diffed the registry's `legacy()` keys against `permission-legacy-action-baseline.json` and refused the commit while Q-w2-7 was unruled. Wave 2 now makes **no** `legacy()` call at all, so the probe has nothing to catch; the invariant it guarded is still enforced, by wave 1's own test running unchanged on this wave's commits |
| **Task 2a-8 Step 1 — the POS hand edit** | **REPLACED by a scaffold run.** The `PermissionDefinition::legacy(key: 'pos.discount_unlimited', …)` manifest block, the hand-appended enum case, the hand-written locale stubs and the pinned `legacy()` signature quotation are deleted. Step 1's shell block gains a step 5 — `permissions:scaffold POS pos override --qualifier=discount_limit`, dry-run then apply, plus a derived-key probe that also fails if the **old** spelling appears in the registry. The registry probe renumbers 5 → 6 |
| **Task 2b-4 Step 0 — the derivation STOP** | **REMOVED**, and the two token invocations corrected from `service-accounts issue --qualifier=token` / `revoke --qualifier=token` (which derived `service-accounts.issue_token` with an underscore, from verbs that do not exist) to `service-accounts.tokens create` / `service-accounts.tokens delete`. Step 0 now scaffolds **all six** keys unconditionally, and its verification probe checks all six keys, all six enum cases, **and** that neither rev-6 spelling survives in the registry |
| **Enum case names** | Re-derived under wave 1's rule — PascalCase of the WHOLE key, splitting on `.`, `-` and `_` (`…-wave-1.md:3589`): `POSPermission::PosDiscountUnlimited` → **`PosOverrideDiscountLimit`**; `IdentityPermission::ServiceAccountsIssueToken` / `ServiceAccountsRevokeToken` → **`ServiceAccountsTokensCreate`** / **`ServiceAccountsTokensDelete`**. The rule itself is unchanged; it is applied to the re-spelled keys |
| **Test rename** | `apps/api/tests/Feature/POS/DiscountUnlimitedPermissionTest.php` → **`OverrideDiscountLimitPermissionTest.php`**, at all seven occurrences (File Structure, Task 2a-8 Steps 6/9/10, the `git add` list, the verification checklist's PG lane) |
| **Everything else the keys touch** | The `can:` middleware expressions (still always `'can:'.<Enum>::<Case>->value`, never a joined literal — the Global Constraint is unchanged), the route tables, the census tables at the top of the plan, the File Structure rows, `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest`'s failure message, Q-w2-5, the 2a/2b split table, Task 2b-5b Step 1, Task 2b-8's route-landing note, the `git add` lists, the Deploy step-2 sync note and the verification checklist |
| **Template defaults** | **Unchanged** — all three keys stay `admin`-only, i.e. no `--template` option and no `templateDefaults` entry (`admin` is implicit and the literal is refused). The ruling changed spelling, not grants, so today's behaviour is still preserved byte-for-byte |
| **Change-log history** | The rev 5 → rev 6 and earlier sections keep the **old** spellings verbatim: they record what those revisions said, and rewriting them would destroy the record the ruling is a response to |
| **Wave 3** | The two references to `service-accounts.issue-token` — the API-gate census row for `POST api/v1/service-accounts/{id}/tokens` and the permission string in its fixture payload — re-spelled to `service-accounts.tokens.create`. Wave 4 references none of the three keys and is unedited |

**Owed, outside this plan's file scope (handed back).** Three artefacts still carry a rev-6 spelling and are not this plan's to edit:

The spec is the source the plan implements, so it is owed the same three-key re-spelling.
