Fix round on **Codex plan gate r5** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r5.md`). Wave 2: **1 BLOCKER, 0 MAJOR, 1 minor**. Both applied; nothing rejected. Three defects **found while applying the blocker** are recorded here too, because the ruling required every new site to name a real enum case and none of the three would have let it.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | Wave 2 introduces unbaselined bare permission literals, so its own per-commit PHPStan contract cannot stay green: `can('roles.view')` at `:1236,1739-1740`, `can('pos.discount_unlimited')` at `:2514`, `can('service-accounts.create')` / `can('users.manage_location_access')` at `:3035-3070` | **ACCEPTED in full.** A new **Global Constraint** states the rule once for the wave, with the two permitted forms — `can(Enum::Case->value)` for a method call and `'can:'.Enum::Case->value` (a **`Concat`**) for a route middleware string — and the reason both are invisible to the rule: `flatten()` returns a key only for a plain `PhpParser\Node\Scalar\String_`, and its own docblock says *"an enum ->value, a concat, a variable — is NOT a literal and is deliberately invisible to this rule"* (`949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:9203-9243`). It carries a key→enum-case→origin table for **all eleven** keys this wave checks, and states explicitly that editing the rule, widening its baseline or adding a path to its allow-list is **not** licensed. **Every site converted**: `RoleController::shapeForGate`, `RoleController::effectivePermissions` (both the gate and the `__()` ability argument), `DiscountPermissionResolver::isAdmin`, the counting-draft route middleware, and `CreateServiceAccountRequest`'s four. The `CreateServiceAccountRequest` import note goes from *"the same five"* to **six**, naming `IdentityPermission` | Global Constraints (new bullet); Task 2a-4 Step 2; Task 2a-6 Step 2; Task 2a-8 Steps 2, 3, 4; Task 2b-4 Step 1 and its imports note; the eight-site census table |
| **m1** | The denial-recorder prose retains the superseded one-producer predicate (`$e->getPrevious() instanceof AuthorizationException`), contradicting the accepted explicit-`$cause` and wrapped-exception arms | **ACCEPTED.** The sentence is re-stated over `attributableToAGate()`'s **three producer shapes** — a non-null `$cause`, an `AuthorizationException`, or an `AccessDeniedHttpException` whose own previous is one — with the note that the rev-4 predicate, read literally, refuses every call `RequireAnyPermission` and `EnforceTokenScope` can make. `abort(403)`'s exclusion is re-stated as a property of its **null previous** rather than of the old predicate | Task 2a-7 Step 3 item 2 |
| **found: enum case names** | The plan used `InventoryPermission::CountingsOverrideOwnership` and `PosPermission::DiscountUnlimited` — the **module-prefix-stripped** form wave 1 rev 6.3 explicitly replaced with PascalCase of the **whole** key, *"collision-free by construction"* (`…-wave-1.md:3589`, and the shipped enums: `InventoryTransfersCreate`, `PosOrdersView`) | **CORRECTED throughout** to `InventoryPermission::InventoryCountingsOverrideOwnership` and `POSPermission::PosDiscountUnlimited`. Neither spelling exists in wave 1's enums, so every call site carrying the old one was a compile error | File Structure; the census table; Q-w2-5; Task 2a-8 Steps 1 and 3; the new Global Constraint |
| **found: POS artefact names** | The plan named `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Domain/Enums/PosPermission.php` and `…/Authorization/PosPermissionManifest.php` in **six** places; wave 1 ships `POSPermission` / `POSPermissionManifest`, named after the module directory (`…-wave-1.md:2929,2942,3636,4446,4459`) | **CORRECTED** in all six, including Task 2a-8 Step 10's `git add` block — which would otherwise have staged two paths that do not exist and failed the commit | File Structure; Task 2a-8 Step 1's manifest/enum edits and Step 10's add list |
| **found: the scaffold cannot derive the key it is told to derive** | Step 1's comment claimed `permissions:scaffold Inventory countings override --qualifier=ownership` derives `inventory.countings.override-ownership` *"from (module-derived prefix, resource, verb, qualifier)"*. `PermissionDefinition::make()` does neither: `$action = $verb->value.('_'.$qualifier)` and `key: $resource.'.'.$action` (`…-wave-1.md:645-648`) — **no module segment, and an underscore join** | **CORRECTED.** The resource is the **nested** `inventory.countings` (`RESOURCE_PATTERN` admits one dot segment, `…-wave-1.md:580`, which is how `inventory.transfers.create` is spelled today) and the derived key is **`inventory.countings.override_ownership`**, updated at all eleven occurrences. **The enum case name is unchanged under either spelling** — the transform splits on `.`, `-` *and* `_` — so nothing downstream of the enum moves. Check 4's STOP guard gains a second probe that fires if the wrong-namespace `countings.override_ownership` appears | Task 2a-8 Step 1 (the invocation, its comment and check 4); Q-w2-5; File Structure; the census table |

The four CRUD keys are unaffected and are scaffolded unconditionally | Task 2b-4: the six-key table (now carrying an enum-case column and the invocation), the new Step 0, the middleware-stack line, Step 9's staging note |

With it fixed, that block is `php -l` clean, which it could not be measured as before | Task 2a-6 Step 2 |

**Verification of this revision.** All 36 `php`-fenced blocks extracted and linted under **PHP 8.4.15**: **32 clean**, and the four that do not parse are *signature sketches* that were never compilable in any revision — a `PrincipalKind` enum followed by bodiless `public function …;` lines, a lone `private function tokenScopePermissionNames(): ?array` with no body, a property line followed by a class, and `CreateServiceAccountRequest` (which parses clean once wrapped in a class that has a parent for `parent::failedAuthorization()`). **Zero bare `can('…')` or `'can:<key>'` literals remain in a production block** — the one grep hit is the negative example inside the Global Constraint itself.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | The denial recorder cannot cover the three accepted producers: `recordGateDenial()` rejects every call whose `$previous` is not an `AuthorizationException`, yet `RequireAnyPermission` and `EnforceTokenScope` must call it directly and "state their own cause" through a method with **no cause parameter**; and the printed class is incomplete — one import for eleven symbols, a one-argument constructor inside the class and a six-argument replacement outside it | **ACCEPTED, and rewritten as ONE complete class.** `recordGateDenial()` gains a fifth parameter, `?DenialCause $cause = null`, and a private `attributableToAGate()` over the **three producer shapes**: a non-null `$cause` (producer 3 — a first-party 403 builder that never throws), `$previous instanceof AuthorizationException` (producer 1 — `can:`, `$this->authorize()`, bare `Gate::authorize()`), and `$previous instanceof AccessDeniedHttpException` whose own previous is an `AuthorizationException` (producer 2 — the converted Symfony type, unwrapped one level). `abort(403)` still returns false: it builds that type with a **null** previous. `$response` is hinted `Symfony\…\Response`, because the render callback and both middleware build an `Illuminate\Http\JsonResponse`, which does **not** extend `Illuminate\Http\Response`. All fifteen imports are present; there is exactly one constructor and it is inside the class. **Both direct call sites are implemented**: `RequireAnyPermission` as a whole file (constructor-injected recorder, body unchanged from `dev:21-31`, `implode(',', $permissions)` as the ability, `DenialCause::PrincipalGrants`) and `EnforceTokenScope`'s 403 arm (`ability: null` so the chain recovers the attempted route's `can:`, `DenialCause::TokenScope`; its three **401** arms are dropped by the status guard and that is stated). **`php -l` clean** | Task 2a-7 Step 2 (the class); Step 3 item 3 (the two call sites) |
| **M1** | Lane 2a has an unresolved dependency inversion: `Phase 0.4.4` calls `EffectivePermissionResolver::forTarget()`, which `Phase 0.4.6` creates, and the plan says *"either order is fine"* | **ACCEPTED.** **The commit that CREATES the resolver is the commit that RE-POINTS its callers.** `0.4.4` does the D4 shaping only and `userRoles` keeps `getAllPermissions()`; `0.4.6` — whose Files block already names `RoleController::userRoles` and whose add list already stages `RoleController.php` — does the re-point. **No ordinal moves and no task is resequenced.** The re-point is behaviour-preserving (`forTarget($user, null)` returns the same unnarrowed set §4.1.3a rule 1 gives `getAllPermissions()`), so `RoleReadShapingTest` is green on both sides of it | Task 2a-4 Step 3 and Step 5's add-list note |
| **M2** | `CreateServiceAccountRequest` is a partial fragment — `authorize()` and the typed 403 are a comment header — and the role-action table reintroduces the undefined `$currentUser` | **ACCEPTED.** `authorize()` and `failedAuthorization()` are **implemented**, against `dev:CreateUserRequest.php:19-29,60-82`: `service-accounts.create`, plus `users.manage_location_access` whenever **any membership** carries `allowed_location_ids` (the service shape differs from the human path's flat field, and the predicate says so), with the 403 body reused verbatim so the two paths ship one error contract. The five imports are named. The role-action table's `$currentUser` becomes `$actor = $this->user()` | Task 2b-4 Step 1 and Step 4's table |
| **M3** | The single-site census does not pin relocation: `SANCTIONED_LINE_MARKER` is the literal text `Container::getInstance()`, so relocating the call to any other line with the same text passes | **ACCEPTED.** The marker is **deleted**. Two structural assertions replace it: **WHAT** — the enclosing `->recordGateDenial(...)` expression, pretty-printed by `PhpParser\PrettyPrinter\Standard` and compared to an exact constant; and **WHERE** — the one `Container::getInstance()` is inside the closure whose first parameter is typed `AccessDeniedHttpException`, asserted by containment rather than by a line number. A second call fails the count; a *different* call at the same text fails the printed expression; a call moved out of the render closure fails containment. **`php -l` clean** | Task 2a-7 Step 3 item 1 |
| **M4** | The `/service-accounts/*` stack duplicates the global `EnforceTokenScope` and the plan asserts idempotence without testing it | **ACCEPTED as a correction to the PROOF, not to the design.** The duplicate is a duplicate **declaration**, never a duplicate execution, and rev 5 proves it: `Router::resolveMiddleware()` expands the group name then sorts (`vendor/laravel/framework/src/Illuminate/Routing/Router.php:844-881`, **v12.58.0**), and `SortedMiddleware::sortMiddleware()` returns `Router::uniqueMiddleware($middlewares)` (`…/SortedMiddleware.php:63`), which collapses equal string values (`…/Router.php:1472-1487`). `Route::gatherMiddleware()` cannot collapse them itself — `'api'` the string and `EnforceTokenScope::class` are different values — so the collapse happens after expansion, which is where it must. **`EnforceTokenScopeCoverageTest` now asserts `gatherRouteMiddleware()` resolves it exactly ONCE** for `POST /api/v1/service-accounts` | Task 2b-3 Step 3 item 2 and Step 4 |
| **m1** | *"One positive and six negatives"* while the list carries three positives | Corrected to **three positives and six negatives, plus one robustness case**, and the count is stated as exact | Task 2a-7 Step 5 |
| **m2** | The notification census says *"five selectors"* and enumerates six basename-resolved paths | Reconciled: **five notification SELECTORS** and **six directory-sensitive PATHS** are different quantities and are no longer conflated; the add-list note says **SIX** | Task 2b-2 Step 6 |
| **m3** | Phase 0 retains known-signature forks for `PermissionDefinition::legacy` although the producer is pinned and can be read exactly | **ACCEPTED.** The fork is deleted and the signature is **pinned**, quoted from wave 1 **rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`** (`…-wave-1.md:677-683`) with its two `InvalidPermissionKey` throws. Phase 0 re-reads it at the lane base and records drift — a re-pin, not an alternative | Task 2b-… the POS permission declaration step |

**Producer re-pin: NONE REQUIRED.** The gate's cross-plan item 1 states that *"both producer plans have now received CHANGES-REQUIRED"* and that waves 2/3/4 must therefore re-pin to successor revisions. **That statement is false and is rejected with evidence** — see the rejected-findings note below.

> **Rejected: the cross-plan "newly required producer changes".** Wave 0b's and wave 1's most recent gates are **r8**, and both returned **`VERDICT: DISPATCH-READY`** with **0 BLOCKER / 0 MAJOR** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-0b-codex-gate-r8.md`, `…-w1-codex-gate-r8.md`). Each gate's single editorial minor was closed in the very revision these plans are pinned to: 0b **rev 6.3 `5257eb0f1`** (*"gate r8 editorial (M7-1 retrospective wording)"*) and wave 1 **rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`** (*"gate r8 editorial (AppServiceProvider import-block citation)"*). **The pins in all three plans already name those exact revisions**, so there is no successor to re-pin to and **no producer editorial is owed at re-pin**. The claim traces to the round-4 prompt's stale context line (*"the wave 0b + wave 1 plans just received CHANGES-REQUIRED"*), carried forward from the round-1 prompt template and no longer true at the time the gate ran. The **standing** re-pin obligation is unchanged and unrelated: Phase 0 of each lane re-reads the producer's shipped names and signatures at the lane base, because a plan is not a merge.

## Change log rev 3 → rev 4

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r3** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r3.md`), wave-2 section: **5 BLOCKER / 4 MAJOR / 2 minor**, plus two cross-plan findings. **Every one is applied.** Nothing below is claimed beyond what the edit actually does.

### BLOCKERs

| ID | Finding | What rev 4 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | Task 2a-7 supplies no compilable denial recorder — the class ends after `shouldEmit()` while the render hook calls an undefined `recordGateDenial()` | **Supplied `recordGateDenial(Request, Response, ?string $ability, ?Throwable $previous): void` in full**, plus `abilityFromRouteStack()`, `cause()`, and the six-dependency constructor it needs. The file-inventory row already promised the method; it now exists | Task 2a-7 Step 2 (the recorder body); the constructor block |
| **B2** | The single-site container census is unsatisfiable — `bootstrap/app.php` already calls `app()` at `:218,221-222,240` and `app/` has pre-existing helper calls | **Replaced the census with two separate, satisfiable assertions.** (a) an **AST** assertion that `bootstrap/app.php` contains **exactly one `Container::getInstance()` `StaticCall`**, plus a `SANCTIONED_LINE_MARKER` pinning *which*; (b) a **shrink-only `APP_CONTAINER_READ_CEILING`** over `app/`'s existing `app(`/`resolve(` sites, seeded from a Phase-0 measurement. Both Phase-0 commands are printed. The stated reason is given: a census a wave cannot make green on the day it lands is a red test the next person deletes | Task 2a-7 Step 3 item 1 (the census note); file inventory row; verification checklist |
| **B3** | The render-hook attribution omits the spec's ungated `Gate::authorize()` arm (`…catalogue-design.md:1745-1749`) | **Re-keyed attribution on the framework type**: `$e->getPrevious() instanceof Illuminate\Auth\Access\AuthorizationException`. Exact in both directions — every gate path wraps that type, `abort(403)` constructs `AccessDeniedHttpException` with `previous` **null**, and `PermissionDeniedException extends AuthorizationException` so the old narrow case is a subset. **Added `DeniedAbilityTrail`** — a request-scoped singleton fed by Laravel's own `GateEvaluated` event (`vendor/…/Auth/Access/Gate.php:434-459`) — because a vanilla `Gate::authorize()` carries no ability and an `AUTH_ONLY` route has no `can:` to read. **Stated how route/permission are obtained at that point** (`$request->route()` is still bound; three ability sources in order; a 403 with none logs a warning and emits nothing). **Added the positive test rev 3 could not have made green** — an ungated in-controller `Gate::authorize()` on an `AUTH_ONLY` route — and a seventh negative for the unrecoverable-ability case. `GateEvaluated` is used rather than `Gate::after` **because a listener cannot change the outcome and an after-callback can** | Task 2a-7 Step 3 items 1 and 3; `DeniedAbilityTrail` + its listener; Step 5 |
| **B4** | `Phase 0.5.5` cannot pass PHPStan or run — `AuditService::record(eventType:, metadata:)` omits five required parameters (`AuditService.php:43-50`) | **Replaced the call with `recordTenantWide(tenantId:, actorUserId:, eventType:, aggregateType:, aggregateId:, payload:, metadata:)`** — wave 1 rev 6.3's own company-less path, quoted from `949dfabdf:…-wave-1.md:5564`. A revocation has no company and also runs from a worker, so tenant-wide is correct on its merits, not only for compilation. **Added `CurrentUserId`** as the null-safe actor source (a facade read would be rule-13 debt and unbindable in the forced-failure test), created beside `CurrentAccessTokenId` in 2a-2's commit and in its add list | Task 2b-5's service body + imports + constructor; Task 2a-2 Step 2 note and Step 6 add list |
| **B5** | `ApiTokenRevoked` cannot be persisted — it carries no company/tenant while `persistEvent()` requires a non-null company id (`DomainEventSubscriber.php:1186-1209`), and the plan says only "register it" | **`tenantId` and `actorUserId` added to the event** (with the reason in its docblock), **carried by the queued job** and re-bound with `Tenancy::initialize()`/`end()` in a `finally`, and **the handler supplied**: `handleApiTokenRevoked()` calling `persistTenantWideEvent()` with wave 1's exact parameter names, plus the `subscribe()` map entry in the shape the tree actually uses (`dev:…DomainEventSubscriber.php:1236-1240`, a class⇒method-name map, not `listen()`) | Task 2b-5's event, job and subscriber blocks |

### Cross-plan findings that land in this plan

- **cross-1** — **both producers re-pinned to rev 6.3**: 0b `5257eb0f1`, wave 1 `949dfabdf`. The pin paragraph states the old pins it replaces, quotes both producers' own commit subjects as evidence the revisions are editorial, and lists every consumed symbol re-read at the new SHAs. Thirteen inline `ff2a949e9` / `960548c60` citations before the change-log sections were re-pointed; the change-log sections keep theirs as provenance. **Verified byte-identical before re-pointing**: `permissions:scaffold`'s `$signature` block is unchanged between `ff2a949e9:8529-8537` and `949dfabdf:8583-8591`.
- **cross-2** — **the `$writeLock` / `$permissionWriteLock` reconciliation is settled in the pin paragraph.** 0b rev 6.3's `RoleController` constructor declares `private readonly PermissionWriteLock $permissionWriteLock` (`5257eb0f1:…-wave-0b.md:3011`, used at `:3025,3058,3105,3160,3188,3242,3244`); **that is the name that ships**, because 0b adds the parameter and runs first. Wave 1 rev 6.3's own fragments say `$writeLock` and record the reconciliation as owed. Recorded that the `$writeLock` at `5257eb0f1:…-wave-0b.md:2333` is a **different class** — `EnsurePermissionsRunner`, which wave 1 deletes — and that wave 2's own new classes (`LastAdminFloor`, `MembershipRevocationService`) keep their own `$writeLock`, which no producer constrains.

### Rejected

**Nothing in the wave-2 section was rejected.** All five blockers, four majors, two minors and both cross-plan findings are applied as written. The gate's own rejected-false-positives list is adopted unchanged — in particular that the render-hook exception to constructor injection is **accepted** (only the implementation and the census proof were rejected), that the principal-kind database ruling is closed, that `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` remains falsifiable, and that seat coverage is complete.

### Owner decisions still required

Unchanged from rev 3, and the gate confirms only these two: **OQ-1** (service principals do not count toward the last-admin floor — confirm before 2a merges) and **OQ-2** (service-token TTL 365d / human 90d / POS one year unchanged — confirm before `Phase 0.5.7`).

## Change log rev 2 → rev 3

Every finding in `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r2.md` that names this plan, with **what rev 3 actually did** — not what it intends. Where a row's fix is partial, the row says which part is done and which is not. Nothing is silently dropped; a rejected finding says so with its evidence.

### BLOCKERs

| ID | Finding | What rev 3 did | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | The `principal_kind` constraint admits arbitrary values; the test never attempts an invalid kind | **Added a SECOND named constraint** `users_principal_kind_check` = `CHECK (principal_kind IN ('human','service'))` beside the existing shape check, with the reason two names are used rather than one conjunct (PG reports the constraint name, so one name means one indistinguishable message). **Added the matching `NOT IN` clause to BOTH SQLite triggers**, insert and update. **Rewrote Step 4's test** from four cases to **seven**, of which **three are invalid-kind cases** — service shape, human shape, and an `UPDATE` — all **raw statements** rather than `User::create()`, because the enum cast would reject an invalid kind before it reached the database. Cases 1–5 run on **both** drivers. `down()` step 3 now drops **both** PG constraints by name. Two checklist rows and a reviewer-gate item 11a added | Task 2a-0/2b-1 Step 1 (the two CHECKs + the trigger note), Step 2.3 (both drops), Step 4 (the seven-case table); File Structure (migration + test rows); Q-w2-4 table; Rollback; verification checklist; reviewer gate |
| **B2** | Task 2a-8 invokes a nonexistent wave-1 scaffold interface (`--module`/`--resource`/`--verb`/`--legacy`) | **Replaced Step 1 entirely.** The signature is now quoted verbatim from wave 1 rev 6.1 — positional `{module} {resource} {verb}` plus `--qualifier=`, `--template=*`, `--sod-group=`, `--dry-run` — with a dry-run call, an apply call, and a derivation check that STOPs if the generator produces a different key. **`--legacy` is gone**: `pos.discount_unlimited` cannot be expressed by the generator (snake_case resource, no verb), so rev 3 **states the manifest edit instead** — the `PermissionDefinition` entry, the enum case, the three `common.json` stubs — exactly as wave 1's 59 other legacy keys are declared, with the ruling that a flag which does not exist is not an alternative. The inherited-symbols row now carries the real signature and says there is no `--legacy`. Step 10's add list gains the three locale bundles, which rev 2 omitted | Inherited-symbols table (`permissions:scaffold` row); Task 2a-8 files list, Step 1 (rewritten), Step 10 add list; File Structure (POS manifest row) |
| **B3** | Lane 2b's numbered commits are not independently buildable — `Phase 0.5.4` calls symbols `0.5.5` and `0.5.6` introduce | **Re-sequenced the lane and split Task 2b-5.** A new **execution-order table** at the head of lane 2b gives, per position, the `Phase` ordinal, the task, why it must be there, what it introduces and what it consumes from earlier in the lane. Seats (2b-6) move to **`0.5.4`**, ahead of the provisioning service that calls `canAddServiceAccount()`; revocation (2b-5a) to **`0.5.5`**, ahead of the surface that routes through it; service accounts (2b-4) to **`0.5.6`**; issuance (2b-5b) to **`0.5.7`**, after the controller it routes on; POS to `0.5.8`; FE to `0.5.9`. **Task IDs are unchanged** so every cross-reference still resolves; a banner on each heading names its new position. The buildability rule is stated mechanically and the checklist **walks the lane** with PHPStan at every commit | Lane 2b preamble (new table); banners on Tasks 2b-2/3/4/6/7/8; Task 2b-5 split header; all seven commit subjects; verification checklist (two new rows) |
| **B4** | Authorization-denial auditing is attached at a point that cannot observe the rendered 403 | **Withdrew the middleware and the marker; moved the recording to the render callback.** `RecordAuthorizationDenial`, `AuthorizationDenialMarker` and `AuthorizationDenialContext` **are not created by this plan at all**. The recorder is invoked from the existing `AccessDeniedHttpException` render callback (`dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:270-308`) **after** the response is built, wrapped so an audit failure cannot turn a 403 into a 500, with the container read justified as the composition root and pinned to one site by a new `BootstrapContainerReadCensusTest`. `RequireAnyPermission` and `EnforceTokenScope` record at their own sites because they build their own responses. The attributable-to-a-gate condition is stated (a `PermissionDeniedException` previous, **or** a `can:`/`require.any.permission:` entry on the resolved route) so a bare `abort(403)` — which also reaches the callback — records nothing. Step 5 grows from one positive and four negatives to **two positives, six negatives and a robustness case** | Task 2a-7 Step 3 (rewritten), Step 5, Step 6 add list + subject; File Structure (2a created rows); 2b bootstrap row; verification checklist (four new rows); reviewer gate item 6 |

### MAJORs

| ID | Finding | What rev 3 did | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | The deterministic SQL-order assertion omits the second advisory-lock statement | **Split the oracle into two cases, each asserting its own true sequence.** `the_floor_acquires_the_shared_write_lock_before_its_own` now drives the floor **directly**, with no controller, so the transaction carries **one** `acquire()` and the sequence really is `advisory, roles, users, model_has_roles`. `a_caller_that_already_holds_the_lock_takes_it_twice_and_is_not_blocked` drives the **controller** path and asserts **two** advisory statements with the same binding. The Global Constraints' re-entrancy note now says explicitly that `acquire()` always *issues* the statement — re-entrancy is PostgreSQL's, not a held-already branch — and that the oracle must name which path it measures | Global Constraints (re-entrancy note); Task 2a-2 Step 1 (both rows); verification checklist |

The one branch with no role to name — a tenant carrying no `template_key = 'admin'` row — now throws a **new `AdminTemplateRoleMissingException`** (409 `ADMIN_TEMPLATE_ROLE_MISSING`, body naming `permissions:sync --tenant=`), which still refuses the mutation and names the provisioning defect instead of mislabelling it as a last-admin refusal. `AdminPermissionFloor` casts `(int) $role->id` with the reason stated. Step 3 gains **four payload-asserting cases**, including `a_self_read_projects_the_request_token`, which is the one that would have failed under rev 2's routing. A new rule 3a states it as a contract. One of Step 3's four cases (`a_self_read_on_an_unscoped_token_has_a_null_token_scope`) asserts `assertNull`, never `assertEmpty`, because `{granted: [], unknown: []}` is the wrong answer rather than a smaller one | Task 2a-6 `assemble()` body; rule 3a; Step 3 |

Added **`MembershipWriterCensusTest`** with an empty `ASSERTED_EXEMPT`. Step 4's EC-16a case now names its four assertions and requires `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database`. The add list gains the event, the subscriber, the census and `HorizonQueueCoverageTest`, all of which rev 2 named in prose and staged nowhere | Task 2b-5 Step 3 (three bodies), Step 4, the 2b-5a add list; verification checklist (three new rows) |

Neither constant changes | Task 2b-4 Step 6 |
| **9** | Most commits have no exact `git add` block | **Added nine.** Tasks 2a-1, 2a-4, 2a-5, 2a-9, 2b-2, 2b-3, 2b-4, 2b-6, 2b-7, 2b-8 and both halves of 2b-5 now end with one. **Eighteen blocks, zero placeholders** — no `<…>`, no glob, no ellipsis. Where a path depends on what a generator wrote or on a module's internal layout, the block says which command resolves it (`git status --porcelain` after the scaffold; `git ls-files` for the six basename-cited census sites) rather than leaving a token in the list | Tasks 2a-1, 2a-4, 2a-5, 2a-9, 2b-2, 2b-3, 2b-4, 2b-5a, 2b-5b, 2b-6, 2b-7, 2b-8 |

### minors

| ID | Finding | What rev 3 did | Where |
|---|---|---|---|

The one wave-1 shape this plan consumed wrongly was `permissions:scaffold`'s CLI, which was wrong against rev 6 too and is therefore a plan defect, not a producer move | Producer-pins paragraph; inherited-symbols table; Q-w2-1; entry condition 4; Task 2a-8 |

### Cross-plan findings that land in this plan

| ID | Finding | What rev 3 did | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **cross-1** | Producer pin: all three plans say wave 1 rev 6 is latest | Same edit as minor 4. **Imports only; signatures unchanged** — the paragraph says so explicitly and lists the ten symbols re-read across the two revisions | Producer-pins paragraph |

| **citation audit** | Base headers declare stale worktree/`dev` SHAs | **NOT corrected, and deliberately.** The header records the SHAs the citations were *measured at*, which is what makes them auditable; Phase 0's first instruction is to re-pin every `dev:` line number at the lane base. Changing the header to the current `dev` without re-measuring the citations would make it a **claim** rather than a record. See **Rejected** below | — |

### Q-w disposition

No Q-w point moved. The gate's own reading — *"the 17 Q-w rulings remain consistent with the accepted spec and do not require new owner escalation"* — is adopted. **Q-w2-4's cost paragraph** gains the five-step correction and **OQ-1's ask-before** moves from 2b to 2a (minor 3); neither is a re-decision.

### Rejected

**One, with evidence.**

- **The citation-audit item asking the base header to carry the current `dev` SHA is REJECTED as written.** The gate is right that the header says worktree `72498e83f` / `dev` `630afa86f` while HEAD is `3dc54ac86` and `dev` is `33796cc08`, and right that the cited application code has not changed. But the header's sentence is *"Every `dev:` citation below was verified against **that** SHA"* — it is a **provenance record**, not a currency claim, and the gate's own citation audit confirms every line it names still resolves. Re-stamping it with `33796cc08` without re-measuring all ~120 `dev:` line numbers would convert a true record into an unverified assertion, which is the failure mode the header exists to prevent. **The plan already handles currency the right way**: Phase 0's second instruction is *"Every `dev:` line number in this plan was pinned at `630afa86f`; re-pin each one at this SHA before the task that edits it"*, and the handback carries the re-pinned numbers with any that moved called out. Rev 3 therefore leaves the header's SHAs as the measurement record and changes nothing. *(The gate itself files this under "wrong or stale citations" while noting "the cited application code has not changed, so line content remains valid" — which is the same conclusion.)*

Everything else in the r2 register reproduces and was applied. Verifications worth recording:

- **B1** — the spec's §5.1 does require both (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md:2012-2029`), and rev 2's SQL block carried only the shape predicate. A row with `principal_kind = 'robot'`, `pos_pin IS NULL`, `email_verified_at IS NULL`, `password IS NULL`, `email IS NULL`, `status = 'active'` satisfies it.
- **B2** — `ff2a949e9:docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md` Task 1.15 Step 1 and the supplied `ScaffoldPermission::$signature` both read `permissions:scaffold {module} {resource} {verb} {--qualifier=} {--template=*} {--sod-group=} {--dry-run}`. `grep -c '\-\-legacy' ` over that plan returns **0**.
- **B4** — `dev:apps/api/bootstrap/app.php:264-269` states the conversion in the codebase's own words, and `:212` opens the `withExceptions` closure. There is no middleware in the `api` group or the priority list that runs after exception rendering.
- **MAJOR 1** — wave 0b rev 6's `PermissionWriteLock::acquire()` issues `pg_advisory_xact_lock` unconditionally inside PG transactions; there is no held-already branch to suppress the second statement.
- **MAJOR 5** — `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Requests/AssignRoleRequest.php:28-35` reads `$actor = $this->user();` before `new AssignableRole($actor)`. The repository's own pattern was available and rev 2 did not use it.

## Change log rev 1 → rev 2

Every finding in `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-10-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r1.md` that names this plan, with what rev 2 did about it. **Nothing is silently dropped**; a rejected finding says so with its evidence.

### Wave-2 BLOCKERs

| ID | Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1-1** | `LastAdminFloor` violates the shared writer-lock protocol and omits a required lock set; the service-role writers likewise lack lock, transaction and locked re-read | **ACCEPTED.** The floor's constructor takes `PermissionWriteLock`; `assertPopulationSurvivesWithout()` now runs a named five-step sequence — shared lock on `'wlota1a:'.$tenantId` **first**, `roles` `FOR UPDATE` in `name`→`id` order, `users` in `id` order, `model_has_roles` in `(model_id, role_id)` order, then decide. Two new test cases assert the statement order from the query log and the re-entrancy property. `ServiceAccountProvisioningService`, the two `/service-accounts/{id}/roles` actions and `MembershipRevocationService` each become one-transaction LOCKED writers with a locked re-read, and all four join `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest::LOCKED_ENTRY_POINTS` | Global Constraints (new lock bullet); Task 2a-2 class body, Steps 1/3/4a; Tasks 2b-4 Steps 2 and 3a, 2b-5 Step 5 |
| **B1-2** | Lane 2a knowingly ships authorization helpers with no behaviour (`refuseServiceTarget()` → `return null;`, the floor's human clause deferred) | **ACCEPTED — schema moved.** `PrincipalKind`, the `users` migration, `$fillable`/`casts()` and the three predicates land in 2a as **Task 2a-0**, before their callers. The floor's human clause is a real `where('users.principal_kind', …)`; EC-4 ships in 2a; `refuseServiceTarget()` gets its predicate, its seven call sites, `ServiceTargetRefusalTest` and its census in new **Task 2a-2a**. `ServiceTargetRefusalIsInertTest` is withdrawn along with the stub it asserted. Token issuance stays in 2b behind `ScopedTokenIssuanceEntryConditionTest` | Q-w2-4 (rewritten); Task 2a-0; Task 2a-2a (new); Task 2a-2; File Structure (2a/2b tables); Deploy; Rollback |
| **B1-3** | The promised resolver is not implementable as written — bodiless declarations, no owner-grant lookup, no `permission:` parsing, no unknown handling, no selector semantics, no DTO assembly | **ACCEPTED.** Split into `EffectivePermissionResolverInterface` (contract, with the `TokenNotOwnedBySubject` throw documented) and one named `EffectivePermissionResolver` with complete bodies: `ownerGrants()` (unnarrowed, role-attributed, D6-aware), `scopeKeys()` (prefix parse, `*` never honoured, unscoped vs narrow-to-nothing), `assemble()` (intersection, `unknown` surfaced, entry and set DTOs, three scope accessors). Bound in the service provider; every consumer type-hints the interface. `EffectivePermissionEntry` added as a DTO. One test asserts the read model agrees with `hasPermissionTo()` key-for-key | Task 2a-6 Steps 1a/1b; File Structure (2a created); verification checklist |
| **B1-4** | Several named failure types/events have no deliverable; EC-13's `RoleUpdateRefused` is absent entirely | **ACCEPTED.** `LastAdminFloorViolation` (factory, with bodies), `LastAdminFloorViolationException`, `AdminPermissionFloorViolation`, `RoleUpdateRefused` and the closed `RoleUpdateRefusalReason` enum are all in the created-file inventory with bodies. Both floors emit `RoleUpdateRefused` with `role_id`/`reason`/`principal_id`/`token_id`; `RoleUpdateRefusedTest` asserts a row lands and **zero** `authz.denied` beside it. `CurrentAccessTokenId` is created once and reused by the floors, `AuditService` and the denial recorder | File Structure (2a created); Task 2a-2 (factory + exception bodies); Task 2a-3 (violation + emission); verification checklist |

### Q-w disposition (gate's own reading, applied)

`Q-w2-1`, `Q-w2-3` and `Q-w2-4` were named as *"must be corrected or re-pinned"* and are rewritten above. `Q-w2-2`, `Q-w2-5` and `Q-w2-6` stand as plan-author decisions, with **Q-w2-5** taking the minor's conditional wording. **No wave-2 finding escalates to a fifth owner question**: OQ-1 … OQ-4 remain the only owner decisions, exactly as the gate concluded.

### Rejected

**None.** Every wave-2 finding in the r1 register was verified against the tree before it was applied, and all eleven reproduce. Verifications worth recording:

- **M1-1** — `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/AuthController.php:154-157` reads `return $this->isPosClient($request) ? now()->addYear() : null;`, and `dev:apps/api/config/sanctum.php:43-53` sets the global 30-day policy the `null` falls through to. The finding is exactly right.
- **M1-7** — wave 1 rev 6 (`177b3d7d7`) Task 1.16 Step 7 stages `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json`, and its generator's docblock says *"a sorted list of `<file>:<line>:<key>` (or `<file>:<line>`) sites"*. Rev 1's path and map shape were both invented.
- **cross-M1** — `…-wave-1.md:150` declares `Phase 1.<task>`, and `grep -o "Phase 1\.[0-9]*[a-z]*:"` over that plan returns `Phase 1.1` … `Phase 1.19` with `Phase 1.4a`–`1.4i`. `Phase 0.3.*` appears nowhere in the programme.
